L365: Abbreviations: AUC: area under the curve; Cmax: maximum plasma concentration; eCTD: electronic Common Technical Document; F: female; GLP: Good Laboratory Practice; M: male; NA: not applicable; NOAEL: no observed adverse effect level.; RBC: red blood cells
L366: a Two males and 2 females at the 0.4 mg/kg/day dose were euthanized between Days 33 and 50 due to moribund health status that included adverse clinical signs (decreased activity, tremor, titubation/impaired balance, low head resulting in prostration, decreased food consumption, diarrhea, emesis and soft stool). Treatment was suspended for the remaining high dose male on Day 51 and the high dose female on Day 57 until the end of the study.
L367: The primary mirdametinib toxicities in exploratory and definitive nonclinical studies were to the gastrointestinal tract, skin, cornea, liver, bone, and systemic mineralization. Many of these target organ effects can be attributed to the pharmacological mechanism of mirdametinib. Intestinal toxicity was present in rats, dogs, monkeys, and rabbits. In dogs and monkeys, erosions or ulcerations of the intestinal mucosa, or both, was dose limiting. These effects are related to the requirement of the MEK pathway on normal intestinal function; MEK inhibition manifests in ulcerations in intestinal mucosa in dogs and monkeys and translates to intestinal toxicities in humans treated with MEK inhibitors (Mourad 2019). Skin lesions, characterized microscopically as epidermal ulceration, were observed in rats in the 1- and 3-month studies. Similar skin lesions were observed in the 1-month dog study, and in an exploratory 14-day monkey study. These skin effects are expected based on the role of MEK on skin keratinocyte survival (Jost 2001) and is a class effect with skin lesions noted in all approved MEK inhibitors.
L368: Mirdametinib caused several toxicities that were specific to the rat. For example, in the 3-month rat study, corneal opacities were detected mainly in males with the incidence being dose dependent. The fact that corneal opacities were detected almost exclusively in male rats in the 3-month study suggests that tissue mineral deposition was the likely cause. Despite inhibition of pERK in the repeat dose dog studies, corneal opacities were not observed. Mineralization of vasculature and various soft tissues (systemic mineralization) was observed in toxicology studies in rats in a time- and dose-dependent manner. However, these effects were not seen in the dog or monkey. The stomach appears to be the most sensitive tissue since mineralization of gastric fundic mucosa occurs prior to the onset of mineralization in other tissues. Male rats were consistently more sensitive to this toxicity compared to female rats and the effect was reversible. Administration of mirdametinib to rats resulted in bone lesions that
L369: included necrosis of the metaphysis and the ossifying zone of the physis and thickening of the zone of hypertrophying cartilage of the physis. These changes were characterized by localized injury to bone that appear to be due to local ischemia and/or necrosis. In the 1- and 3-month rat studies, hypocellularity in the metaphyseal region of the distal portion of the femur and/or proximal portion of the tibia occurred in males and was reversible following cessation of dosing in the 1-month study. In addition, thickening of the bony trabeculae in the femoral/tibial metaphyseal region was evident in males in the 3-month study. Bone lesions, like those observed in rats, have not been seen in mice, dogs, or monkeys administered mirdametinib.
L370: Safety margins are presented in Table 4.
L371: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L372: FDA's assessment is limited to repeat-dose general toxicology studies, and other studies, such as carcinogenicity and embryo-fetal development, are discussed in the appropriate sections. FDA did not verify the monkey findings discussed by the Applicant because this study had limited number of animals and was non-GLP.
L373: Studies # 20196649, 745-03826, 05067, 745-03833, and 05039 were previously reviewed by FDA under IND 139883. In general, FDA agrees with most of the Applicant's assessment of the results of the GLP-compliant toxicity studies in rats and dogs. FDA notes that Study# 05039 in dogs was 13-weeks in duration. The following summarizes FDA's perspective of repeat-dose studies results and indicates additional findings or differences of interpretation of results from the Applicant, where appropriate.
L374: Common clinical signs in early death dogs at 0.4 mg/kg in the 3-month study included diarrhea, emesis, and soft stools that correlated with decreased food consumption and body weight loss; clinical signs of emesis, abnormal stools were also noted at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg in the 3-month dog study. Additionally, neutrophilic infiltrates/inflammation in the tongue, stomach, and/or esophagus, as well as lymphoid depletion in multiple lymphoid organs and myeloid cell hyperplasia in the bone marrow were noted in early death dogs, suggesting a systemic inflammatory response.
L375: Skin sores were noted in rats at \ge 0.1 mg/kg in the 3-month study and at 1 mg/kg in the 1-month study and correlated with histologic signs of minimal to mild neutrophilic inflammation, crust, and/or ulceration of the epidermis. In the 1-month study in dogs, microscopic findings of minimal to mild ulceration or necrosis of the epidermis were noted in individual animals at \ge 0.1 mg/kg and correlated with clinical signs of skin sores at 0.3 mg/kg. Additionally, skin redness occurred at \ge 0.1 mg/kg in the 3-month dog study but lacked any histologic correlate. These findings trended toward improvement during the recovery phase in the 1-month studies. Recovery periods were not included in the 3-month studies.
L376: Hematologic changes included decreases in red blood cell parameters, including red blood cells, hemoglobin, and hematocrit, in the 3-month rat study at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg and in the 1- and 3-month dog studies at doses \ge 0.05 mg/kg. Increases in circulating monocytes, neutrophils, and/or large unstained cells were noted in the 1- and 3-month studies in rats at \ge 0.3 mg/kg and dogs at \ge 0.05 mg/kg. These findings correlated with histologic signs in the lymph nodes in the 3-month studies in rats and dogs, including minimal to moderate lymphoid hyperplasia, lymphoid depletion, and increases in plasma cells, as well as hypocellularity in the 3-month rat study.
L377: In addition to the mineralization findings described above by the Applicant, mineralization of the renal corticomedullary junction was noted in female rats at \ge 0.3 mg/kg in the 1-month study and mineralization of the medulla and/or corticomedullary junction in the kidney was observed in both sexes at all doses in the 3-month study. Signs of mineralization were not noted in dogs; however, mirdametinib increased phosphorus levels at all doses tested in rats (up to 37% compared to controls) and dogs (up to 49% compared to controls) in the 1-month and 3-month studies. Though the 3-month studies did not include a recovery phase, increases in phosphorus improved after a drug-free period in the 1-month studies in rats and dogs, but mineralization findings, including mineralization of the gastric fundus, were irreversible in rats. Additionally, minimal increases in calcium (up to 6% compared to controls) occurred during the first week of dosing in the 1-month rat study but improved by the second week of dosing; increases in calcium were not noted in the 1-month study in dogs or the 3-month studies in rats and dogs.
L378: Findings in the eye were noted in rats and dogs. Ocular findings in rats administered 1 mg/kg included minimal conjunctiva mineralization in the 1-month study and minimal corneal mineralization in the 3-month study. Minimal atrophy and thinning of the cornea were also noted at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg in the 3-month study in rats. In the 3-month toxicology study in dogs, corneal opacity was noted during ophthalmic examinations in one male each at 0.05 mg/kg and 0.4 mg/kg and correlated with histologic signs of minimal neutrophilic inflammation and mild corneal atrophy in the 0.4 mg/kg male.
L379: Signs of liver toxicity were noted in rats and dogs. Increases in liver enzymes, including AST (up to 107%) and ALT (up to 66%), were noted in rats at \ge 0.1 mg/kg in the 1-month study and at \ge 0.3 mg/kg in the 3-month study and correlated with histologic signs of minimal to mild mineralization, mixed cell inflammation, and/or vacuolation in male rats at \ge 0.3 mg/kg in the 3-month study. In dogs, increases in AST (up to 81%), ALT (up to 90%), and/or ALP (up to 119%) were noted at 0.3 mg/kg in the 1-month study and at 0.4 mg/kg in the 3-month study but lacked any histologic correlate in either study. These findings improved during the recovery phases in the 1-month studies. Additionally, FDA disagrees with the Applicant's assessment that increases in liver enzymes were non-adverse in the 28-day study in mice considering that these findings correlated with dose-dependent increases in incidence of hepatocellular necrosis and
L380: that signs of liver toxicity occurred in toxicology studies in rats and dogs. Of note, tissue distribution studies with radiolabeled-mirdametinib showed greatest radioactivity in the liver.
L381: Bone findings in rats at 1 mg/kg in the 1-month study included minimal to moderate hypocellularity of the metaphyseal region of the femur and distal tibia and findings at \ge 0.3 mg/kg in the 3-month study, included minimal to mild dysplasia and thickening of the trabeculae. While these bone findings occurred in both sexes, males appeared to be more sensitive to these effects. Findings in long bone are relevant considering the intended population includes pediatric patients and were included in section 8.4 of the GOMEKLI label. These findings were not noted at the end of the recovery period in the 1-month study in rats.
L382: Male and female reproductive organs were target organs in the 3-month study in rats. See Section 5.5.4 for details. Findings noted in male reproductive organs (epididymis, prostate, and testis) in the 3-month study in dogs were not added to the label as the relationship of these findings to mirdametinib was uncertain given that descriptions of these findings in the study report did not clearly distinguish differences in severity.

L396: The GLP-compliant 28-day toxicology study in non-transgenic mice (Study #: 20196649) presented by the Applicant was used to select the recommended dose levels for the 6-month carcinogenicity study of PD-0325901 in transgenic mice (Study #:20196651). Toxicology findings in the 28-day study in mice were generally consistent with findings noted in rats and dogs, with the additional finding that mirdametinib caused minimal to moderate degeneration/necrosis in the testes at doses \ge 10 mg/kg. Signs of gastrointestinal toxicity, including minimal to severe epithelial degeneration/necrosis, erosion/ulceration, and inflammatory cell infiltrates throughout the gastrointestinal tract were also noted at doses \ge 10 mg/kg.
L397: ## 5.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L398: ## The Applicant's Position:
L399: ## Data:
L400: The genotoxicity of mirdametinib was assessed in a battery of genotoxicity studies as summarized in Table 10. Mirdametinib was not mutagenic in bacteria (745-03535) and was not clastogenic in human lymphocytes in vitro (745-03511). In an in vivo rat micronucleus study mirdametinib was not clastogenic (745-03804). Additionally, in a rat bone marrow chromosomal aberration assay, mirdametinib was negative for induction of structural or numerical chromosome breaks in the treatment groups (745-04155).

The positive control also confirmed the sensitivity of the assay.
L405: Based on the totality of data from the genotoxicity studies, mirdametinib is not genotoxic.
L406: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L407: FDA does not agree with the Applicant's interpretation of the in vivo micronucleus assay. In this study, female rats treated with mirdametinib showed dose-related increases in percent of micronucleated polychromatic erythrocytes (%MNPCE) compared to concurrent controls and achieved statistical significance at the high dose of 3 mg/kg, suggesting that mirdametinib may have clastogenic potential. However, the results of the 3 mg/kg dose level did not meet the criteria for a positive response because the results were within the levels of historical controls albeit closer to the higher limit (historical control range % MNPCE = 0.16 to 0.59; at 3 mg/kg % MNPCE = 0.54). The Applicant attributes this increase to be potentially related to bone marrow toxicity, but this endpoint was not measured in the study. Overall, the FDA considered the results of the micronucleus assay to be equivocal. Follow-up testing demonstrated mirdametinib was negative for clastogenicity in the in vivo chromosomal aberration assay in rats, but this study did not rule out the potential for aneugenicity. Therefore, FDA determined that mirdametinib is genotoxic with an aneugenic mode of action.
L408: See section 5.5.1 Other Toxicology Studies for discussion of genetic toxicology of metabolites.
L409: ## 5.5.3. Carcinogenicity
L410: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L411: ## **Data:**
L412: The carcinogenic potential of mirdametinib was assessed in a 6-month study in transgenic rasH2 mice (20196651). The dose levels established for this study (0, 2, 5, 15 mg/kg/day) were based on findings in the 28-day mouse study and feedback provided by the Carcinogenicity Advisory Council at FDA.
L413: Based on microscopic evaluation, there were no carcinogenic effects of mirdametinib at any dose level in this study.
L414: A 2-year rat carcinogenicity study (20435943) will be conducted (start of dosing planned in Aug2024), and the Applicant intends to submit the results post-approval.

L417: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L418: Died or Sacrificed Moribund	
There were mirdametinib-related effects on survival in males and females at 15 mg/kg/day. Females at 15 mg/kg/day were terminated early (Day 164) as per FDA recommendation due to poor survivability. A definitive cause of death or morbidity could not be determined in 13 early death females at \ge 5 mg/kg/day. The cause of death or morbidity in 10 early death females at \ge 5 mg/kg/day was attributed to moderate to marked hepatocellular necrosis. The cause of death or morbidity in 4 males at 15 mg/kg/day was attributed to microscopic changes in the urogenital tract, including mixed cell inflammation of the prostate gland and adjacent urethra and preputial gland abscesses.
Survival at 2 or 5 mg/kg/day of mirdametinib was 100% in males and ranged from 92% to 100% in females and was comparable to controls, while survival at 15 mg/kg/day was reduced to 84% in males on Day 176 (study termination at 26 weeks) and 16% in females on Day 164 (early group termination at 24 weeks).

Body Weight and Food Consumption	
There were significant increases in mean body weight gains for 2, 5, and 15 mg/kg/day males and 2 and 5 mg/kg/day females as compared to controls from Day -1 to Day 183. Mean body weight increases were observed in the 15 mg/kg/day females from Days -1 to 162 with increased mean body weights on Day 162 (69%) as compared to controls prior to early termination on Day 164. A general dose-dependent trend of increased food consumption generally corresponded to increased mean body weight during the study.

Clinical Observations	
There were no mirdametinib-related clinical observations at 2 or 5 mg/kg/day during the study. Clinical observations were generally related to the early mortality in the 15 mg/kg/day males and females and included breathing abnormalities, prostrate, erected fur, cold to touch, decreased activity, dehydration, and hunched posture.

Non-Neoplastic Changes	Increased lymphoid cellularity and/or epithelial hyperplasia of the thymus in males and females at \ge 2 mg/kg/day
Increased hematopoietic cells in the spleen in males and females at \ge 2 mg/kg/day
Hepatocellular vacuolation in males at \ge 2 mg/kg/day and females at 2 and 5 mg/kg/day
Hepatocellular necrosis in males at 2 and 15 mg/kg/day and females at \ge 5 mg/kg/day
epithelial degeneration/necrosis or erosion/ulceration throughout the GI tract in males and females at \ge 5 mg/kg/day
Epithelial hyperplasia of the non-glandular stomach in males at \ge 5 mg/kg/day
Epithelial hyperplasia of the crypt in the intestine in 1 male at 15 mg/kg/day and females at \ge 5 mg/kg/day
Preputial gland abscess in males at \ge 2 mg/kg/day
Mixed cell inflammation of the prostate gland in males and clitoral gland in females at 15 mg/kg/day.
Multiple non-neoplastic microscopic findings were considered secondary to stress/inanition (Everds 2013, Travlos 2006) in males at 15 mg/kg/day and females at \ge 5 mg/kg/day. These findings included minimal to mild decreased cellularity in the sternal bone marrow, minimal to severe decreased cellularity of lymphoid tissues (white pulp of the spleen, germinal center of the lymphoid follicles of mandibular and mesenteric lymph nodes, and cortex of thymus), and minimal to moderate secretory depletion of the acinar cells in the pancreas and mandibular salivary glands.
L419: Neoplastic Changes	Based on microscopic evaluation, no carcinogenic effect related to mirdametinib administration was observed at any dose level in this study.
In both sexes, the NMU positive control group exhibited the expected neoplastic (increased incidence of malignant lymphoma, squamous cell papilloma, and squamous cell carcinoma) and non-neoplastic (retinal atrophy) changes, thereby validating the CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous model used in this study.
L420: Abbreviations: AUC: area under the curve; Cmax: maximum plasma concentration; eCTD: electronic Common Technical Document; F: female; FDA: Food and Drug Administration; GLP: Good Laboratory Practice; M: male; NA: not applicable; NE: not evaluated.
L421: Mirdametinib showed no evidence of carcinogenicity in the 6-month CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous mice study.
L422: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L423: The FDA previously reviewed the 6-month carcinogenicity study in CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous mice under IND 139883. The review focused on neoplastic findings. FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusion that no carcinogenic effects related to administration of mirdametinib were observed at any dose level tested in the 6-month mouse study.
L424: The Applicant submitted a special protocol assessment (SPA) for the 2-year rat study under IND 139883, which was reviewed by the FDA on June 25, 2024. The final study report will be submitted to the NDA as a post marketing requirement (PMR).
L425: ## 5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L426: The Applicant's Position:
L427: The developmental and reproductive toxicology assessment of mirdametinib consisted of a definitive fertility study in male and female rats and preliminary EFD toxicity studies in pregnant rats and rabbits.
L428: Given the significant EFD toxicities observed in both the rat and rabbit range-finding studies, and the requirement for MEK in embryonic development (Giroux 1999), neither definitive embryo-fetal toxicity studies nor pre- and post-natal development studies were conducted for mirdametinib; this was agreed upon with the FDA (email, 24Feb2023).

Body Weight and Food Consumption	Higher mean body weight gains were noted with statistical significance in the 0.1, 0.3, and 1 mg/kg/day group males, but the mean absolute body weights in these groups were generally comparable to the control group throughout the study. Mean food consumption in the 0.1, 0.3, and 1 mg/kg/day groups was unaffected by test article administration.
Clinical Observations	Scabbing on various surfaces or skin lesion were noted at increasing incidences with increasing dose level in the 0.1, 0.3, and 1 mg/kg/day group males.
Males Fertility Indices	No test article-related effects on reproductive performance (mating, fertility, and pregnancy indices) were observed at any dose level when treated males were mated with naïve females. No statistically significant differences were noted between the control and test article-treated groups. All males sired a litter.
Untreated Female Findings	There were no test article-related effects on implantation sites or intrauterine survival when test article-treated males were mated with untreated females.
Sperm Analysis	No test article-related effects were observed on sperm parameters (mean testicular sperm numbers and sperm production rate, motility, and morphology) in males at any dose level.
L431: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L432: Microscopic Observations	Gross findings at necropsy of skin scabs in males at 1 mg/kg/day were characterized microscopically by mild focal epidermal ulceration, epidermal hyperplasia, mixed cell inflammation, epidermal crust, subcorneal (under the stratum corneum) neutrophil pustules, and/or follicular pustule, and were considered nonadverse.
Treated Female Findings (treated females mated with naive sexually mature males)
Died or Sacrificed Moribund	None
Body Weight and Food Consumption	Higher mean body weight gains were noted with statistical significance in the 1 mg/kg/day group females but were generally comparable to control throughout the gestation period. The mean absolute body weight as well as food consumption was not affected at 1 mg/kg/day.
Clinical Observations	Scabbing on various surfaces or skin lesion were noted at increasing incidences with increasing dose level in the 0.1, 0.3, and 1 mg/kg/day group females.
Female Estrous Cycle	There were no test article-related effects on mean number of days between pairing and coitus and the mean lengths and number of estrous cycles at any dose level.
Female Fertility Indices	No test article-related effects on reproductive performance (mating, fertility, and pregnancy indices) were observed at any dose level when treated females were mated with naive males. No statistically significant differences were noted between the control and test article-treated groups.
Microscopic Observations	There were no microscopic findings for the ovaries (the only organ microscopically evaluated in females) of pregnant females at 1 mg/kg/day.
Uterine Findings	Mean numbers of corpora lutea, implantation sites, and intrauterine survival were unaffected by administration of the test article to the females at dose levels of 0.1, 0.3, and 1 mg/kg/day. No statistically significant differences from the control group were noted.

L439: In the definitive male and female rat fertility and early embryonic development study, mirdametinib did not have any effects on male reproductive parameters, female reproductive parameters, or embryonic development at any dose level. However, mirdametinib induced EFD toxicities (early resorptions, post-implantation loss, dose-dependent decreased fetal weights) in both rat and rabbit dose-range finding preliminary studies. Because of these EFD effects, no additional developmental toxicity studies were conducted per agreement with the FDA (email, 24-Feb-2023). Of note, EFD toxicity is considered a class effect of MEK inhibitors.
L440: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L441: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of the findings of the GLP-compliant fertility study in male and female rats and dose range finding embryo-fetal development (EFD) toxicity studies in rats and rabbits. The FDA previously reviewed the EFD studies and concluded that mirdametinib showed clear evidence of embryo-fetal toxicity. Based on the positive results of the dose range finding studies and mechanism of action, the FDA agreed that further GLP-compliant reproductive toxicology studies were not warranted.
L442: In the EFD study in rats, actual doses administered were 0.25, 0.6, 3, and 5.0 mg/kg based on formulation analysis for the 0.3, 1, 3 and 6 mg/kg groups. One rat at 0.6 mg/kg (1 mg/kg) was found dead on GD21 and necropsy results indicated 15 late resorptions and no live fetuses; the cause of death was considered due to fetal necrosis.
L443: While we agree that mirdametinib did not affect mating performance or fertility under the conditions tested in the dedicated fertility study, FDA notes that repeat-dose toxicology studies with mirdametinib had reproductive organ findings that may impair female fertility. In a 3-month repeat-dose toxicology study in rats, mirdametinib caused follicular cysts associated with decreases in the number of corpora lutea at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day (\ge 2-times the human exposure at the clinical dose of 2 mg/m^2 twice daily based on AUC) that correlated with decreased organ weight. Male reproductive organ findings in rats at 1 mg/kg (8-times the human exposure at the clinical dose of 2 mg/m^2 twice daily based on AUC) included inflammation of the prostate in individual males and marked hypoplasia of the spermatogenic epithelium in the testis and decreased content in the epididymis in one male. In a 1-month repeat-dose toxicology study in mice, mirdametinib caused testicular degeneration and/or necrosis at doses \ge 10 mg/kg/day (\ge 23-times the human exposure at the clinical dose of 2 mg/m^2 twice daily based on AUC). The reversibility of effects on ovary and testes were not assessed. Given differences in incidence and severity of male reproductive organ findings between control and mirdametinib-treated groups and literature supporting a role for MEK in spermatogenesis [Hasegawa (2013)], the possibility that findings in male reproductive organs in animals were related to administration of mirdametinib could not be eliminated; however, the clinical impact on male fertility is unclear given the low incidence of findings.

L454: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L455: ## The Applicant's Position:
L456: ## Data:
L457: ## **Phototoxicity**
L458: Mirdametinib absorbs light in the 290 to 700 nm range; therefore, an in vitro phototoxicity study was conducted (05075). In the in vitro Balb/c 3T3 mouse fibroblast neutral red uptake assay, mirdametinib exhibited a weak positive signal. However, mirdametinib was not retained in the skin or eye based on the [^{14}C] mirdametinib tissue distribution study in rats.
L459: ## **Metabolites**
L460: Separate toxicity studies of mirdametinib metabolites have not been conducted. However, the carboxylic metabolite, PD-0315209, was measured in the pivotal toxicology studies in both rats
L461: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L462: and dogs (Table 5, Table 6, Table 7, Table 8, and Table 9) and in the 6-month carcinogenicity study conducted in transgenic rasH2 mice; results are shown in Table 11.
L463: Overall, the nonclinical toxicology profile of mirdametinib is considered to be acceptable for the proposed indication.
L464: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L465: **Phototoxicity:** Although the Sponsor concludes that mirdametinib has little risk of phototoxicity given that it is not retained in the eye or skin, there were two cases of photosensitivity and one case of photophobia reported clinically. In animals, there was skin toxicity (sores, crust, neutrophilic infiltrates) observed in repeat-dose toxicology studies, but it is unclear if these were related to phototoxicity. In the clinic, dermatologic reactions, such as dermatitis acneiform and non-acneiform rashes, were also reported; however, these findings are consistent with MEK inhibitors class effects, suggesting that the majority of dermatologic disorders in patients may be related to a pharmacological effect.
L466: **Metabolites:** Two major metabolites of mirdametinib with pharmacological activity have been identified (M22 and PD-0315209) that represent &gt;10% of mirdametinib in human plasma. M22 (O-glucuronide conjugate) is a unique human metabolite while PD-0315029 (carboxylic acid) was detected in repeat dose studies in mice, rats, and dogs. M22 is a Phase 2 glucuronide conjugate and Phase 2 conjugation reactions generally render a compound more water soluble and pharmacologically inactive; thus, M22 is not expected to have an increased risk of carcinogenicity and further evaluation is not warranted. Genetic toxicity of PD-0315209 was not directly tested; however, it is expected that rats were exposed to PD-0315209 in the in vivo micronucleus test as rats can generate PD-0315209, though levels PD-0315209 were not measured in this study. FDA clarifies that toxicokinetics analyzing PD-0315209 levels during the 6-month carcinogenicity study were conducted in non-transgenic littermates.
L467: **Impurities:** The CMC review team inquired if the level of [REDACTED] (b) (4) was qualified based on a limit of NMT [REDACTED] (b) (4)%. The [REDACTED] (b) (4) was present in the mirdametinib batch # XH451101, used in the 13-week toxicology studies at [REDACTED] (b) (4)%. Based on the highest dose of 1 mg/kg of mirdametinib tested in the 13-week toxicology study in rats, which did not cause deaths, the [REDACTED] (b) (4) present in the clinical dose of mirdametinib was qualified by toxicology studies.

L472: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L473: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L474: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L475: The clinical pharmacology key review questions focused on the evaluations of the proposed dosage (including the two dosage forms and the unified dosing nomogram), food effect, and drug-drug interactions and organ impairment.
L476: The primary evidence for efficacy and safety was obtained from Study MEK-NF-201 which evaluated mirdametinib in patients \ge 2 years of age with an inoperable NF1-associated PN causing significant morbidity. The proposed recommended dosage is 2 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 BID (with or without food) for the first 21 days of each 28-day cycle (maximum dose is 4 mg BID) until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. The proposed dosage forms are capsules (1, 2 mg) and tablets for oral suspension (1 mg). The tablets may be swallowed whole or dispersed in water to be administered as an oral suspension to facilitate administration in patients who cannot swallow pills.
L477: The proposed recommended dosage is acceptable, based on the observed benefit-to-risk profiles of mirdametinib from pivotal Study MEK-NF-201 and supportive Study NF-106. Based on the clinical pharmacology review, the proposed dosage forms of capsules (1, 2 mg) and tablets (1 mg) are acceptable given the overlapping PK observed between adult and pediatric patients following administration of 2 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 BID via tablets or capsules, including comparable PK in the overlapping ages of pediatric patients who received tablets vs. capsules (age 5 to 14 years). Efficacy was observed across pediatric age groups (2 to &lt; 12, 12 to &lt; 18 years), while differences in TEAEs by dosage form appear attributable to age-related development differences. The proposed dosing nomogram is acceptable, based on the PPK simulation which showed no expected clinically meaningful differences in systemic exposures.
L478: Based on the clinical pharmacology review of the in vitro and clinical studies, administration with regard to food, DDIs and organ impairment were adequately addressed, except for the following:
L479: - The effects of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on the PK of mirdametinib is not known, and a post-marketing requirement (PMR) will be issued to provide dosage recommendations for mirdametinib in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment.
- The effect of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inducers on the PK of mirdametinib is not known. A post-marketing commitment (PMC) will be issued to provide dosage recommendations for mirdametinib when used concomitantly with strong CYP3A4
L480: inducers.
L481: ## Recommendations
L482: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information contained in NDA 219379 (tablets) and NDA 219389 (capsules). The key review issues with specific recommendations and comments are summarized below:
L483: REVIEW ISSUE	RECOMMENDATIONS / COMMENTS
Pivotal or supportive evidence of effectiveness	See Executive Summary (above) and Section 8 (Statistical and Clinical Evaluation).
General dosing instructions	2 mg/m2 BID (with or without food) for the first 21 d of each 28-d cycle (maximum dose is 4 mg BID).
Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	No restrictions or dose adjustments are needed in patient subgroups at this time.
Labeling	Overall, the proposed labeling recommendations are acceptable upon Applicant's agreement to FDA's revisions.
Bridge between the clinical trial and to-be-marketed formulations	N/A (the to-be-marketed capsules (1, 2 mg) and tablets (1 mg) were used in the pivotal Study MEK-NF-201).
L484: ## Post-Marketing Requirement (PMR) or Commitment (PMC)
L485: The following outstanding issues should be addressed as a PMR and a PMC (see Section 13):
L486: ## PMR
L487: - Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate a serious potential risk of increased drug toxicity and to determine an appropriate, safe dose of mirdametinib in patients with moderate (total bilirubin &gt; 1.5 – 3.0 x ULN, any AST) and severe (total bilirubin &gt; 3 – 10 x ULN, any AST) hepatic impairment. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled “Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling.”
L488: ## PMC
L489: - Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial with a strong CYP3A4 inducer (that also induces UGTs, P-gp, and CES enzymes) with mirdametinib to evaluate the effect of strong CYP3A4 induction on decreasing the systemic exposure of mirdametinib and to provide dosage recommendations for mirdametinib when used concomitantly with strong CYP3A4 inducers. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled “M12 Drug Interaction Studies.”
L490: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L491: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L492: ## Data:
L493: Mirdametinib PK has been evaluated in 7 clinical studies as a single agent either as a single dose (4 to 20 mg) or multiple doses (1 mg QD or BID up to 30 mg BID). Single dose PK studies were conducted in healthy adult participants to evaluate the effects of food on mirdametinib PK (Study A4581004), metabolism and elimination via radiolabeled mass balance (Study MEK-NF-101), and effects of mirdametinib and PD-0315209 on QT interval (Study MEK-NF-102). Dose-ranging studies and pharmacodynamic (PDx) data were collected in adult participants with advanced cancers (Study A4581001) and with non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC; Study A4581002). PK and PDx data were collected from adult and pediatric participants with NF1 PN at the target dose of 2 mg/m2 BID in Studies NF-106 and MEK-NF-201.
L494: Collectively, these studies were used to characterize the PK of mirdametinib with population PK (PopPK) and population pharmacokinetic pharmacodynamic (PopPKPDx) models to evaluate exposure-response relationships with safety and efficacy endpoints. The exposure-efficacy and exposure-safety analyses were used to support the recommendation of 2 mg/m2 BID, with a maximum of 4 mg BID, administered orally for 3 weeks on and 1 week off (treatment for the first 21 days of a 28- day cycle) as an optimal regimen of mirdametinib for the treatment of NF1 PN.
L495: No clinical drug-drug interaction studies were conducted due to the absence of expected risk based on nonclinical evaluations. Studies of the effects of severe renal impairment and moderate or severe hepatic impairment have also not been conducted. Mirdametinib has two major metabolites and both show less pharmacological activity than mirdametinib. PD-0315209, a carboxylic acid metabolite representing 24% of circulating drug-related material, was significantly less effective than mirdametinib (approximately 2200-fold difference) at reducing ERK phosphorylation in tumor cells grown in culture. PD-0315209 transiently suppressed pERK in tumor bearing mice; however, the PopPK predicted steady-state maximum concentration (Cmax,ss) for PD-0315209 at the proposed target dose of 2 mg/m2 BID is approximately 8-fold lower than the half-maximal inhibitory concentration (IC50) for pERK suppression. M22, a secondary O-glucuronide representing 14.7% of circulating drug-related material, was significantly less effective than mirdametinib (greater than 75-fold difference) at reducing ERK phosphorylation in tumor cells grown in culture. Neither metabolite is expected to have contributions to clinical efficacy.
L496: ## The Applicant's Position:
L497: The clinical pharmacology studies support the recommended dose for mirdametinib of 2 mg/m2 BID with cycles of 3 weeks on followed by 1 week off treatment (treatment for the first 21 days
L498: of a 28-day cycle). The BSA-driven dosing guidance ensures that PK between adult and pediatric populations are similar. The metabolites are not expected to have clinically relevant pharmacological activity.
L499: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L500: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L501: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L502: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L503: ## **Data:**
L504: The selection of mirdametinib at 2 mg/m2 BID, with a maximum dose of 4 mg BID, on a 3 weeks on/1 week off schedule as the Recommended Phase 2 Dose (RP2D) was made using the totality of evidence from nonclinical data in the human tumor xenograft and mouse NF1 neurofibroma models, tolerability assessment, PK and PDx measurements from clinical studies A4581001 and A4581002 in advanced cancer patients, and findings from a Phase 2 investigator-initiated study (NF-106) in NF1 PN.
L505: The nonclinical PKPDx relationship was evaluated using mouse and human tumor xenograft models, including an NF1 neurofibroma model. These models demonstrated human exposures at the proposed dose of 2 mg/m2 BID were consistent with doses associated with pERK inhibition in nonclinical models, which demonstrated tumor volume reductions in 85% of treated mice in the NF1 mouse model.
L506: In clinical studies of advanced cancer populations (Studies A4581001 and A4581002), mirdametinib was assessed at various dose levels (1 mg QD to 30 mg BID) and schedules (continuous, intermittent). Based on the occurrence of late-onset adverse events (AEs), dose de-escalation, inclusive of both reduction of the total daily dose as well as the utilization of different administration schedules, was explored. Due to challenges with tolerability at the initial RP2D (15 mg BID) preventing long-term administration, full evaluation of mirdametinib efficacy in participants with advanced cancers could not be performed. It was concluded that an intermittent administration schedule using doses between 2 and 10 mg BID should be explored to identify a recommended dose for long-term treatment with mirdametinib (LoRusso 2010). In Study NF-106, participants ≥16 years of age with NF1 and progressing and/or morbid PN received mirdametinib at a 2 mg/m2 dose (maximum 4 mg BID) on a 3 weeks on/1 week off schedule. In this study, mirdametinib was well tolerated and resulted in a confirmed ORR &gt;30%. Based on these supportive data, a mirdametinib dose of 2 mg/m2 (maximum 4 mg BID) on a 3 weeks on/1 week off schedule was selected for the pivotal Phase 2b Study MEK-NF-201.
L507: The results of exposure-response analyses identified a clear relationship between exposure and tumor growth, with increasing average concentration at steady-state being predictive of
L508: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L509: increasing reduction in tumor volume, increasing probability of response, and reduced time to response. Exposure-safety analyses identified relationships between mirdametinib and PD-0315209 exposure and incidence of several AEs. No relationships with AEs of Grade \ge 2 severity were identified.
L510: The actual body surface area (BSAs) and doses used in the PopPK and PKPDx modeling datasets were of a sufficiently wide range to support simulation of PK and tumor response across a range of doses. The median (minimum, maximum) of actual doses was 2.52 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2 (0.424, 18.6) in the PopPK model development and 2.21 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2 (0.806, 2.86) in participants with NF1 PN in Studies MEK-NF-201 and NF-106, which provided tumor volume measurements (Figure 4). Therefore, the range of actual doses administered in the studies used to develop these models supports their predictive ability to estimate safety and efficacy responses at target mirdametinib doses ranging from approximately 1.0 to 3.0 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2.
L511: **Figure 4: Distribution of Actual Doses in Studies MEK-NF-201 and NF-106**
L512: Histogram includes all non-zero actual doses corresponding to a tumor volume scan in Study MEK-NF-201 and NF-106 participants. The dashed vertical green lines indicate the interquartile range of actual doses.
Source: Derived from ADEX
L513: Population and trial simulations were conducted using exposure-efficacy and exposure-safety models to predict the impact of various target dose levels from 1.0 to 3.0 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2 BID (1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.2, and 3.0 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2). The simulations support that exposures associated with the 2 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2 dose of mirdametinib provide an optimal balance of robust efficacy for patients with NF1 PN with a tolerable incidence of low severity exposure-related AEs.
L514: Population simulations were also conducted to evaluate the impact of a simplified nomogram for use with both tablet and capsule formulations. Study MEK-NF-201 used separate
L515: nomograms for the 2 formulations, with the tablet nomogram using a 0.5 mg step. No meaningful differences from observed clinical study results for PK, efficacy, or safety were predicted using a single unified nomogram for both formulations. Therefore, the unified nomogram (Table 14) is proposed for marketing approval of both the tablet and capsule formulations of mirdametinib.
L516: **Table 23:** Unified Mirdametinib Dosing Nomogram

0.4 to <0.7	1
0.7 to <1.05	2
1.05 to <1.5	3
\ge 1.5	4
L518: Abbreviations: BID: twice daily; BSA: body surface area
L519: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L520: The recommended mirdametinib dose regimen of 2 mg/m^2 BID, with a maximum dose of 4 mg BID, administered on a 3 weeks on/1 week off schedule for NF1 PN patients is supported by safety, tolerability, efficacy, PopPK, and exposure-response analyses. Mirdametinib has a concentration-dependent impact on PN tumor growth. Exposure-safety relationships were only significant for low severity AEs, namely lab elevations that do not correlate with clinical tolerability findings. Simulations of tumor growth and AE incidence conducted using the PopPK and PKPDx models to predict the responses over a range of target doses of 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, and 3.0 mg/m^2 indicated that exposures associated with the 2 mg/m^2 BID dose of mirdametinib provide an optimal balance of robust efficacy for patients with NF1 PN with a tolerable incidence of low severity exposure-related AEs.
L521: Simulations from PopPK and PKPDx models also predict negligible changes to exposure and response when using the same nomograms for the tablet and capsule dose forms. Thus, mirdametinib may be dosed using a single unified nomogram, for both tablet and capsule dose forms, using 1 mg increments for each BSA band without altering the anticipated clinical outcomes for patients with NF1 PN. This unified nomogram compared with what was used in Study MEK-NF-201 will prevent errors in dose calculation that may arise from having different instructions for the two dose forms.
L522: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L523: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The proposed unified dosing nomogram for capsules (1, 2 mg) and tablets (1 mg) is acceptable.
L524: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L525: ## **Data:**
L526: Body weight scaled allometrically with clearance and volume of distribution in a PopPK analysis. BSA, however, was not identified as a covariate that affects mirdametinib PK. The lack of association with BSA is expected based on the use of BSA-based guidance for selecting the administered dose. Simulations of PK by administered dose identified that PK is largely overlapping regardless of administered dose.
L527: The age range in the PopPK analysis was from 2 through 86 years of age, however few participants over the age of 65 were enrolled in the studies. No clinically meaningful effects on mirdametinib PK were identified for age, sex, race, or baseline liver function tests. No meaningful relationships were identified for participants with creatinine clearance values consistent with mild or moderate renal impairment or for participants with bilirubin values consistent with mild hepatic impairment based on the NCI-organ dysfunction working group (ODWG) criteria.
L528: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L529: Therapeutic individualization based on BSA is proposed using the simplified nomogram supported by the PopPK and PKPDx simulations (Table 14). Individualization of dosing is not required for other demographic factors (age, sex, race), for patients with mild or moderate renal impairment, or for patients with mild hepatic impairment (based on NCI-ODWG). Effects of severe renal impairment or end stage renal disease (ERSD) cannot be estimated from the PopPK analysis. Potential effects of moderate or severe hepatic impairment will be studied in a planned hepatic impairment study.
L530: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L531: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. A PMR for renal impairment is not needed, given that mirdametinib is minimally renally eliminated (&lt;1.5% mirdametinib and PD-0315209 recovered in the urine).
L532: ## 6.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L533: The presented clinical pharmacology program adequately characterizes the PK of mirdametinib and evaluates the need for dosing guidance based on intrinsic/extrinsic factors. Nonclinical evaluations of drug-drug interaction potential suggest low clinical risk for major CYP-mediated interactions or transporters of clinical interest.
L534: An additional clinical study is planned to evaluate the impacts of moderate or severe hepatic impairment on mirdametinib PK.
L535: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L536: The impact of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on mirdametinib exposure is outstanding and will be addressed as a postmarketing commitment (PMC).
L537: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L538: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position, however notes that the effect of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on mirdametinib PK will be addressed as a PMR (instead of a PMC, as stated by the Applicant above).
L539: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L540: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L541: ## **Data:**
L542: The PK of mirdametinib is comparable between healthy adults, adults with advanced solid malignancies, and adults and children 2 years of age and older with NF1 PN. Therefore, observations and conclusions from the clinical pharmacology evaluations in healthy adults and patients without NF1 PN are of direct relevance to NF1 PN adult patients and pediatric patients. An overview of absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion (ADME) properties, clinical PK, and drug-drug interaction (DDI) characteristics of mirdametinib are provided below. For a list of clinical studies referenced in this section, refer to Table 15.
L543: **Absorption:** Mirdametinib was rapidly absorbed, with a median Tmax of approximately 1 hour following administration of tablet or capsule formulations. Differences in Tmax were not observed between healthy adults and patients with NF1 PN or between adult and pediatric patients.
L544: Human absolute bioavailability was not evaluated in clinical studies. Mirdametinib is highly soluble and shows high permeability in in vitro assays. As such, a Biopharmaceutics Classification System Class I compound designation request was submitted for mirdametinib.
L545: **Distribution:** The mirdametinib apparent volumes of distribution estimated from a PopPK analysis using data across clinical studies was 16 L for central compartment and 22.4 L and 54.3 L for shallow and deep peripheral compartments, respectively. Following administration of a single oral 4 mg dose to healthy male participants in the mass balance study (Study MEK-NF-101), the mean apparent volume of distribution (% coefficient of variation [CV]) was 255 L (13.4%).
L546: Mirdametinib is highly bound to protein in human plasma. The mean unbound fraction for mirdametinib in human plasma ranged from 0.176% to 0.254% and is independent of mirdametinib concentration.
L547: **Metabolism:** Mirdametinib undergoes oxidation and glucuronidation and forms predominantly the metabolites PD-0315209 (a carboxylic acid metabolite) and M22 (a secondary O-glucuronide metabolite of mirdametinib). In the mass balance study (Study MEK-NF-101), mirdametinib was highly metabolized, with &lt;10% of mirdametinib excreted unchanged by either route (0.7% and 8% of the dose in urine and in feces, respectively). Mirdametinib and PD-0315209 represented 35.3% and 24.0% of total plasma radioactivity, respectively. M22 represented 14.7% of total plasma radioactivity. In vitro experiments indicate that the metabolism of mirdametinib involves UGT and CES enzymes.
L548: PD 0315209 was significantly less effective than mirdametinib (approximately 2200-fold difference) at reducing ERK phosphorylation in tumor cells grown in culture. PD-0315209 also transiently suppressed pERK in tumor bearing mice; however, the PopPK predicted Cmax,ss for PD-0315209 at the proposed target dose of 2 mg/m2 BID is approximately 8-fold lower than the IC50 for pERK suppression. M22 was significantly less effective than mirdametinib (&gt; 75-fold difference) at reducing ERK phosphorylation in tumor cells grown in culture and, as a Phase 2 conjugation reaction generally renders a compound more water soluble and pharmacologically inactive, no further evaluation was warranted. Neither metabolite is expected to have a substantial contribution to pharmacological activity of mirdametinib.
L549: **Excretion:** In the mass balance study (Study MEK-NF-101), renal excretion of glucuronide metabolites was found to be the major route of elimination for mirdametinib in humans; a mean of 67.7% of the radiolabel was recovered in urine and 27.3% in feces. The urinary excretion of unchanged parent drug was minor, at approximately 0.7%. In the Phase 1/2 study in advanced cancers, urinary excretion of unchanged parent drug was minor (approximately 2% to 6%), and &lt;1% was excreted as PD-0315209 (Study A4581001).
L550: **Clinical Pharmacokinetics:** In participants with advanced cancer, mirdametinib was rapidly absorbed and exposure generally increased proportional to dose from 1 mg QD/BID to 30 mg BID (Study A4581001). The mean t1/2 was 28 hours following a single dose of 4 mg in the mass balance study that was conducted in healthy male participants (Study MEK-NF-101). The observed intersubject variability (coefficient of variation [CV%]) for cold mirdametinib AUC and Cmax in the mass balance study was 12.0 % and 16.5%, respectively. Following multiple oral BID doses, mirdametinib mean accumulation ratio on Day 21 ranged from 1.1 to 1.9 across different dose levels in participants with solid tumors (Study A4581001). The PopPK model predicted mean accumulation ratio for participants with NF1 PN receiving a mirdametinib dose of 2 mg/m2 BID, with a maximum dose of 4 mg BID, in Study MEK NF 201 ranged from 1.88 to 2.09.
L551: **Gastric acid modifying agents:** The solubility of mirdametinib exceeds 16 µg/mL (4 mg/250 mL) across the physiological pH range (1.2 to 7.5). Dissolution data also support rapid dissolution of all strengths of [REDACTED] capsule dose forms across the physiological pH range. Therefore, drugs that modify gastric pH are not expected to impact the dissolution or solubility
L552: of mirdametinib and represent a low risk of altering its clinical exposure.
L553: **Renal Impairment:** Total urinary elimination of mirdametinib and PD-0315209 was 1.3% of the administered drug (Study MEK-NF-101). In the regulatory guidance on PK in renal impairment (FDA 2020), a drug is considered to be substantially eliminated by the renal route when the fraction of systemically available drug or active metabolite eliminated in the urine is &gt;30%. Therefore, effects of renal impairment on the PK of mirdametinib have not been evaluated in a dedicated clinical study. Of note, the enrollment of participants with CLCR indicative of mild renal impairment was allowed in Study MEK NF-201. Additionally, participants with moderate renal impairment were included in Studies A4581001 and A4581002. Covariate analysis for baseline creatinine clearance (CLCR) was conducted in the PopPK analysis. The PopPK analysis population contained 60 participants with CLCR consistent with mild impairment and 10 participants with moderate impairment. No relationship between mirdametinib PK and CLCR was observed. A statistically significant effect was found for CLCR on the clearance of PD-0315209 (CLm/F), suggesting a decrease in CLm/F with increasing severity of renal impairment. PopPK model predicted exposures of PD-0315209 for participants with normal function, mild renal impairment, or moderate renal impairment suggest an increase in exposure to PD-0315209 in participants with moderate renal impairment; however, the exposures remain far below concentrations associated with pERK inhibition. Participants with severe renal impairment and ESRD were not represented in the PopPK analysis and expected effects to mirdametinib and PD-0315209 exposure cannot be extrapolated.
L554: **Hepatic Impairment:** The effects of hepatic impairment on the PK of mirdametinib have not been evaluated in a dedicated clinical study. Covariate analysis for baseline liver function tests was conducted in the PopPK analysis. Based on baseline bilirubin values and the NCI-ODWG classification system, 19 participants in the PopPK analysis population were consistent with mild impairment (Group B2) and 6 were consistent with moderate impairment (Group C). No clinically meaningful relationships were observed between mirdametinib or PD-0315209 PK and baseline liver enzymes levels (aspartate aminotransferase [AST], alanine aminotransferase [ALT], and Bilirubin). The effects of moderate and severe hepatic impairment cannot be inferred from the PopPK analysis. A dedicated clinical study in these populations is planned as a PMC.
L555: **Victim DDI:** Mirdametinib is a substrate of P-glycoprotein (P-gp) and breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP). PD-0315209 is a substrate of BCRP. Concomitant use of P-gp inhibitors showed no impact on PopPK model predicted exposures of mirdametinib and PD-0315209. No dedicated clinical studies have been conducted to assess the risk of victim DDIs from P-gp or BCRP inhibitors.
L556: Nonclinical studies suggest minimal risk for DDIs from inhibitors or inducers of CYP-mediated metabolism. Mirdametinib is a substrate of UGT and CES enzymes. PD-0315209 is a substrate of UGT enzymes. Concomitant use of UGT1A6 inhibitors and inducers, UGT2B7 inhibitors and inducers, and CES inhibitors showed no impact on PopPK model predicted exposures of
L557: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L558: mirdametinib. An effect of UGT2B7 inducers was observed on the fraction metabolized (Fm) to PD-0315209, leading to a 19% increase in Fm. Model predicted exposures for PD-0315209 were still comparable in participants with and without concomitant use of UGT2B7 inducers.
L559: **Perpetrator DDI:** No clinical DDI studies have been performed. In vitro studies suggest a low potential to inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4 at the highest anticipated clinical dose (4 mg BID). There is also low induction potential for CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, and CYP2C19. Mirdametinib induced CYP3A4 mRNA expression with apparent maximum effect (Emax) and apparent half-maximum effect (EC50) values of ~&gt; 3.17 to ~&gt; 5.74 (fold) and ~&gt; 10uM. The calculated R3 value of 0.98 does not indicate that mirdametinib has induction potential in vivo. Mirdametinib also showed no detectable or limited (&lt; 50%) inhibition of UGT1A1, UGT1A3, UGT1A4, UGT1A6, UGT1A9, UGT2B7, and UGT2B15.
L560: There is also low potential for mirdametinib and PD-0315209 to perpetrate DDIs involving inhibition of efflux and uptake transporters (P-gp, BCRP, multidrug and toxin extrusion protein [MATE]1, MATE2K, organic anion transporting polypeptide [OATP]1B1, OATP1B3, organic anion transporter [OAT]1, OAT3, and organic cation transporter [OCT]2) at clinically relevant free plasma concentrations.
L561: **QTc:** Based on a dedicated thorough QT study (MEK-NF-102), mirdametinib does not prolong the QTc interval in healthy subjects and does not have any adverse effect on cardiac repolarization at concentrations well above steady-state peak exposures.
L562: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L563: The clinical pharmacology of mirdametinib and PD-0315209 has been adequately characterized by clinical evaluations. The primary metabolites in humans were identified, results from nonclinical assays demonstrate that contribution of the metabolites to pharmacological activity is not clinically relevant. No clinical evaluations of DDIs were conducted, however, in vitro assessments support low risk of clinically relevant interactions with major CYP enzymes or transporters. The PopPK analysis was sufficient to evaluate expected effects from mild and moderate renal impairment and mild hepatic impairment. No dose adjustments are necessary in these populations and a clinical study is planned to further evaluate the effects of moderate and severe hepatic impairment. The overall evaluation of clinical pharmacology supports the selection of 2 mg/m2 BID as an appropriate dose for adult and pediatric patients with NF1 PN.
L564: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L565: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. FDA notes that the effect of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on mirdametinib PK will be addressed as a PMR (instead of a PMC, as stated by the Applicant above). The DDI assessment based on the PopPK model alone is considered inadequate due to many patients who take multiple medications together at the same time. Regarding DDI evaluations, mirdametinib is not likely to be affected by concomitant
L566: inhibitors to a clinically relevant extent, given that mirdametinib is metabolized via multiple pathways and enzymes. The effect of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inducers (that also co-induce UGTs, P-gp, and CES enzymes) on mirdametinib PK is currently unknown and will be addressed as a PMC.
L567: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L568: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L569: ## **Data:**
L570: Yes. The exposure-response analyses identified a clear relationship between mirdametinib exposure and response within the range of actual doses used in the NF1 PN clinical studies. Mirdametinib has a concentration-dependent impact on PN tumor growth and overall response. The final tumor growth inhibition (TGI) model was used to generate simulations to evaluate target doses (1, 1.5, 2, and 3 mg/m2) of mirdametinib BID by comparing efficacy (response rate, with response defined as tumor volume reduction from baseline exceeding 20%, or percent change from baseline). Desirable clinical efficacy is predicted at target doses as low as 1 mg/m2 BID; however, substantial increases in both magnitude and time of response are predicted at the recommended starting dose of 2 mg/m2 BID. As the starting dose allows for potential reduction due to tolerability, this relationship ensures high probability of response at the initial dose and confidence that a clinically active dose is still expected in the event of individual reductions.
L571: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L572: The mirdametinib clinical pharmacology program supports that exposure resulting from the proposed regimen 2 mg/m2 dose (maximum 4 mg) BID on a 3 weeks on/1 week off schedule provides effective reduction in tumor volume in both adult and pediatric patients with NF1 PN.
L573: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L574: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Additionally, the proposed dosage forms of capsules (1, 2 mg) and tablets (1 mg) are acceptable based on overlapping PK observed between adult and pediatric patients following administration of 2 mg/m2 BID via tablets or capsules, including comparable PK in the overlapping ages of pediatric patients who received tablets vs. capsules (age 5 to 14 years). Efficacy was observed across pediatric age groups (2 to &lt; 12, 12 to &lt; 18 years), while differences in TEAEs by dosage form appear attributable to age-related development differences. The proposed unified dosing nomogram is also acceptable, based on the PPK simulation which showed no expected clinically meaningful differences in systemic exposures.
L575: ## 6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L576: ## Data:
L577: Yes. In the PopPK analysis, no clinically meaningful covariate effects for sex, race, or disease status were found. An allometric effect for weight on the clearance and volumes of distribution for mirdametinib and PD-0315209 was found; the BSA-driven dosing adequately controls for substantial variance across the range of actual doses administered. Most AEs were managed by standard supportive care measures and/or dose modifications based on BSA.
L578: ## The Applicant's Position:
L579: The proposed BSA-based dosing regimen of 2 mg/m2 BID, with a maximum dose of 4 mg BID, on a 3 weeks on/1 week off schedule is suitable for both adult and pediatric patients with NF1 PN.
L580: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L581: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The proposed recommended dosage is acceptable, based on the observed benefit-to-risk profiles of mirdametinib from pivotal Study MEK-NF-201 and supportive Study NF-106.
L582: ## 6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors (e.g. race, ethnicity, age, performance status, genetic subpopulations, etc.)?
L583: ## Data:
L584: The effects of hepatic impairment on the PK of mirdametinib were not evaluated in a dedicated clinical study. Covariate analysis for baseline liver function tests was conducted in the PopPK analysis. Based on baseline bilirubin values and the NCI-ODWG classification system, 19 participants in the PopPK analysis population were consistent with mild impairment (Group B2) and 6 were consistent with moderate impairment (Group C). No clinically meaningful relationships were found between mirdametinib PK and baseline liver enzymes levels (AST, ALT, and bilirubin). The effects of moderate and severe hepatic impairment cannot be inferred from the PopPK analysis. A dedicated clinical study in these populations is planned.
L585: The effects of renal impairment on the PK of mirdametinib have not been evaluated in a dedicated clinical study. Covariate analysis for baseline CLCR was conducted in the PopPK analysis. The PopPK analysis population contained 60 participants with CLCR consistent with mild impairment and 10 participants with moderate impairment. No relationship between mirdametinib PK and CLCR was observed. PopPK-predicted Cmax, Ctrough, and AUC for mirdametinib were not meaningfully different between participants with normal function, mild renal impairment, or moderate renal impairment. No evaluations have been conducted with
L586: ## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L587: participants with severe renal impairment or ESRD. Due to high plasma protein binding, dialysis is unlikely to affect systemic mirdametinib exposure and is unlikely to be beneficial to treat mirdametinib overdose.
L588: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L589: Based on intrinsic patient factors in the PopPK analyses, no alternative dosing regimen or management strategy is required based on demographic factors or for patients with mild hepatic impairment (NCI-ODWG Group B1 and B2), mild renal impairment, or moderate renal impairment. The potential impact to mirdametinib PK from severe renal impairment or ESRD and for moderate or severe hepatic impairment cannot be described at this time.
L590: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L591: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. A PMR will be issued to evaluate the effect of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on mirdametinib PK.
L592: ## ***NF1 Genotype***
L593: Enrollment in MEK-NF-201 was based on a patient having either a clinical diagnosis of NF1 using the NIH Consensus Conference criteria or a pathogenic documented *NF1* variant. In the FAS, 27/58 (47%) adult patients and 25/56 (45%) pediatric patients were enrolled based on documented *NF1* variant status (Table 28). Subject level genotype and type of *NF1* variant was not collected as part of study MEK-NF-201. In response to FDA IR dated 21 August 2024, the Applicant provided available subject level *NF1* variant data for 19/56 (34%) pediatric and 6/58 (10%) adult patients from the FAS. Given the complexity of the *NF1* locus, the Applicant grouped variants into the following categories: deletion, duplication, indel, missense, nonsense, or splice. In pediatric patients, the distribution of *NF1* variants was as follows: nonsense (N = 6, 31.6%), splice (N = 5, 26.3%), duplications (N = 4, 21.0%), deletions (N = 3, 15.8%), and missense (N = 1, 5.3%). In the group of adult patients, 3 patients had *NF1* deletions (50%), 1 patient had an indel variant (16.7%), 1 patient had a missense variant (16.7%), and 1 patient had a nonsense variant (16.7%). In general, the types and distribution of variants across the *NF1* locus were consistent with estimates from the literature (PMID: 34566848, PMID: 38095723, PMID: 23244495). The ORRs in pediatric and adult patients with available *NF1* variant data were similar to the ORRs in the FAS (Table 15, Table 16). In terms of best confirmed objective responses, PRs were observed across all *NF1* variant categories with data from \ge 1 patient per category. Of note, one adult patient with a deletion encompassing the full coding sequence of *NF1*, which is associated with larger numbers and earlier appearance of cutaneous and PNs (PMID: 34566848, PMID: 38095723), achieved a confirmed PR of the target PN. This exploratory analysis suggests that treatment with mirdametinib induces objective responses in patients carrying different types of pathogenic *NF1* variants.

L612: ## 6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L613: ## Data:
L614: **Food Effect:** Study A4581004 evaluated the effect of a high-fat, high-calorie meal on mirdametinib PK in healthy adult participants receiving a single oral 20 mg dose of mirdametinib. The dose evaluated in this study is higher than the proposed dose of 2 mg/m2 BID for NF1 PN because it was conducted at a dose hypothesized to be relevant to the advanced cancer indication under development at the time. PopPK analysis supports dose proportionality up to at least 20 mg, indicating that results from this study are applicable to the 2 mg/m2 dose level. Consumption of a high-fat, high-calorie meal reduced and delayed the rate of absorption (43% reduction in Cmax; Tmax delayed by 2 hours). The extent of absorption was slightly reduced (AUCinf reduced by 7%), but the confidence intervals of the geometric least-squares mean ratio (GLMR) remained within standard bioequivalence limits. Mirdametinib was dosed without regard to food in Study MEK-NF-201 and NF-106.
L615: Exposure-response analyses concluded that average concentration, or AUC, is the primary driver of efficacy for mirdametinib. Therefore, a reduction in Cmax with maintenance of the overall extent of exposure is not considered clinically meaningful for mirdametinib. Mirdametinib may be dosed without regard to food.
L616: **Gastric acid modifying agents:** Mirdametinib is highly soluble across the physiological pH range. Dissolution data also support rapid dissolution of all strengths of both tablet and capsule dose forms across the physiological pH range. Therefore, drugs that modify gastric pH are not expected to impact the dissolution or solubility of mirdametinib and represent a low risk of altering its clinical exposure. Mirdametinib may be taken concomitantly with gastric acid reducing agents.
L617: **Victim DDI:** Mirdametinib is a substrate of P-gp and BCRP. PD-0315209 is a substrate of BCRP. Concomitant use of P-gp inhibitors showed no impact on PopPK model predicted exposures of mirdametinib and PD-0315209. No dedicated clinical studies have been conducted to assess the risk of victim DDIs from P-gp or BCRP inhibitors.
L618: Nonclinical studies suggest minimal risk for DDIs from inhibitors or inducers of CYP-mediated metabolism. Mirdametinib is a substrate of UGT and CES enzymes. PD-0315209 is a substrate of UGT enzymes. Concomitant use of UGT1A6 inhibitors and inducers, UGT2B7 inhibitors and inducers, and CES inhibitors showed no impact on PopPK model predicted exposures of mirdametinib. An effect of UGT2B7 inducers was observed on the Fm to PD-0315209, leading to a 19% increase in Fm. Model predicted exposures for PD-0315209 were still comparable in participants with and without concomitant use of UGT2B7 inducers.
L619: Taken together, mirdametinib is unlikely to be susceptible to CYP- or transporter-mediated DDIs
L620: in a clinically meaningful capacity.
L621: **Perpetrator DDI:** No clinical DDI studies have been performed. In vitro studies suggest a low potential to inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4 at the highest anticipated clinical dose (4 mg BID). There is also low induction potential for CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, and CYP2C19. Mirdametinib induced CYP3A4 mRNA expression with apparent Emax and apparent EC50 values of ~&gt; 3.17 to ~&gt; 5.74 (fold) and ~&gt; 10uM. The calculated R3 value of 0.98 does not indicate that mirdametinib has induction potential in vivo. Mirdametinib also showed no detectable or limited (&lt; 50%) inhibition of UGT1A1, UGT1A3, UGT1A4, UGT1A6, UGT1A9, UGT2B7, and UGT2B15.
L622: Taken together, mirdametinib is unlikely to result in CYP- or transporter-mediated DDIs at clinically relevant exposures.
L623: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L624: Based on in vitro and clinical studies, management strategies for food, gastric PH, and DDIs are not required.
L625: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L626: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. A PMC will be issued to evaluate the effect of concomitant a strong CYP3A4 inducer (that also co-induce UGTs, P-gp, and CES enzymes) on mirdametinib PK.
