L653: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L654: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position and notes that the primary analysis of efficacy and safety was based on results from Study MEK-NF-201. The Applicant submitted a complete data set for this study, using a data cut date of September 20, 2023, at the time of the initial NDA submission. The Applicant submitted an updated data set, with DCO of June 12, 2024, at the time of the 90-day safety update (submitted September 26, 2024, to SDN 19). The updated data from Study MEK-NF-201 will form the basis for this review.
L655: Efficacy from Study NF-106 was evaluated as supportive evidence given differences in the study design that did not allow for full evaluation of ORR and DOR, with patients being required to discontinue treatment if they did not have a response by a certain timepoint. Results from this trial were not independently verified during the review of this application.
L656: Study MEK-NF-201 was open-label and did not include a comparator arm; therefore, the analyses of efficacy and safety are descriptive only. Where possible, confidence intervals are provided to assist in the interpretation of the efficacy data. For additional statistical methodologies, see Section 8.1.1.
L657: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L658: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L659: Studies MEK-NF-201 and NF-106 are the relevant studies used to support efficacy.
L660: ## 8.1.1. Study MEK-NF-201
L661: ## Study Design
L662: ## The Applicant's Description:
L663: Study MEK-NF-201 is an open-label, multi-center, Phase 2b study being conducted to determine the efficacy and safety of mirdametinib in participants \ge 2 years old with symptomatic inoperable NF1-associated PNs. A total of 113 participants enrolled and stratified by age (approximately 50 participants 2 to 17 years of age and 50 participants \ge 18 years of age). Mirdametinib was orally administered at a dose of 2 mg/m2 BID (maximum 4 mg BID) in 4-week cycles on an intermittent (3 weeks on/1 week off) schedule. This study consists of two phases: the Treatment Phase (24 cycles) and the optional Long Term Follow Up (LTFU) Phase.
L664: Participants who completed Cycle 24 of the Treatment Phase and did not meet withdrawal criteria were eligible to participate in the LTFU Phase. Withdrawal criteria included the participant having met the definition of progressive disease, occurrence of a medical condition or circumstance that exposed participant to substantial risk or prevented the participant from adhering to protocol requirements, any SAE which the investigator considered was not in the best interest of the participant to continue, pregnancy, prohibited concomitant medication, failure to comply with protocol requirements, or termination of the study by the Sponsor, IRB or regulatory authority. Eligible participants will continue taking the last dose assigned in the Treatment Phase. Participants will remain in the LTFU Phase until a withdrawal criterion is met or mirdametinib is commercially available. The treatment phase of the study is completed; the LTFU phase of the study is currently ongoing.
L665: Figure 5: Schema
L666: The primary endpoint was confirmed ORR, defined as the proportion of participants who had a confirmed \ge 20\% reduction in target tumor volume compared to baseline as assessed by a blinded independent central reviewer (BICR). The response needed to be confirmed in a consecutive tumor assessment within 2 to 6 months during the treatment phase. All tumor volume measurements were independently determined by 2 reviewers. In the case that the two measurements represented different tumor response statuses, as defined above, an independent adjudicator made the final decision on which response category is recorded at that visit. The primary endpoint analysis was performed using the adjudicator-selected reader for those visits where the two BICR readers did not agree. Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) was performed approximately every 4 cycles throughout the Treatment Phase and the LTFU Phase.
L667: Key secondary endpoints included duration of response (DoR), quality of life (QoL) and pain questionnaires, and safety and tolerability of mirdametinib.
L668: ## Administrative Structure
L669: This study utilized an independent data monitoring committee (DMC) and operated according to an established charter. The committee was composed of 3 members including physicians knowledgeable in the treatment of NF1 and an independent statistician. Sponsor employees were not members of the DMC. The DMC was responsible for ongoing monitoring of the safety, efficacy and benefit/risk profile of participants in the Treatment Phase of the study. Reviews included aggregate safety, targeted medical events of special interest, serious AE data and aggregate endpoint data.
L670: ## Study Location
L671: A total of 50 sites were opened across the US.
L672: ## External Control Data
L673: As the natural history of NF1 PN is incompletely described in the published literature, notably for the adult population, NF1 natural history data were gathered and analyzed to serve as an external historical control to support this application.
L674: Two historical controls studies were included in these analyses: the placebo control arm of Study 01-C-0222 and the natural history Study 08-C-0079; both studies were conducted by the NCI.
L675: ## *Study 01-C-0222*
L676: Study 01-C-0222 was an NCI-sponsored Phase 2 randomized, flexible crossover, double-blinded, placebo-controlled study of tipifarnib, a farnesyltransferase inhibitor, in children and young adults (aged 3-25 years) with NF1 and progressive PNs at baseline with the potential to cause significant morbidity. The study started in Jul2001 and was completed in Feb2009. To be eligible for this study, participants must have had tumors that were measurable, defined as \ge 3 cm in one direction, and progressive, defined as either a \ge 20\% increase in target PN volume, \ge 13\% increase in 2-D, or \ge 6\% increase in 1-D measurement over last 2 consecutive MRI scans or within approximately 1 year prior to screening.
L677: Participants were randomized in a double-blinded fashion to receive tipifarnib or placebo (Phase A) and crossed over to the opposite treatment arm at the time of tumor progression (Phase B) after a 2-week washout period following Phase A. Participants remained in Phase B until tumor progression or they met other criteria for treatment discontinuation. Tipifarnib (200 mg/m2/dose orally every 12 hours) or placebo was administered twice daily for 21 days followed by a 7-day rest period between cycles. The primary endpoints were time to progression (TTP), ORR, and the incidence of AEs. QoL was also monitored by the NIH Impact of Pediatric Illness Scale. PN volumes were measured with MRI at the start of Cycles 1, 4, 7, and 10, followed by every 6 cycles. Progression was defined as \ge 20\% volume increase in at least 1 target PN compared with baseline; a response was defined as \ge 25\% reduction in the sum of the volume of all index lesions for \ge 4 weeks.
L678: Participants in this study could have had up to 3 target lesions with volumetric measurements. All lesions with an adequate number of sequential measurements were analyzed, with the date of progression being the first date that the criteria for progression was met by any lesion.
L679: ## *Study 08-C-0079*
L680: Study 08-C-0079 is an NCI-sponsored observational and longitudinal analysis of participants with clinically confirmed NF1 or a known NF1 mutation to characterize the natural history of NF1. New participants evaluated at the NIH were \le 35 years and there was no upper age limit for participants previously enrolled in clinical trials at NIH or for participants diagnosed with MPNST, with clinical concern for MPNST, or with infrequent or unusual NF1-related manifestations. Participants in the study were allowed to undergo clinically indicated surgeries and medical treatments and concurrently enroll in clinical trials for PNs. Participants remained on study until they met the criteria for discontinuation (including withdrawal of consent, serious protocol violation, lost to follow-up, or completed study objectives per the principal investigator).
L681: ## Diagnostic Criteria
L682: Children and adults with NF1 and PN, with a PN-related morbidity at enrollment as determined by the site PI.
L683: ## Eligibility Criteria
L684: Key inclusion criteria included:
L685: - Participant must be \ge 2 years of age inclusive, at the time of signing the informed consent/assent; participant must have weighed \ge 10 kg with a body surface area of \ge 0.4 m^2
- Participant had EITHER the clinical diagnosis of NF1 using the NIH Consensus Conference criteria OR have a constitutional NF1 mutation documented in a Clinical Laboratory Improvement Amendments/College of American Pathologists certified lab.
- Participants must have a target PN that is causing significant morbidity, such as (but not limited to) head and neck lesions that are compromising the airway or great vessels, brachial or lumbar plexus lesions that are causing nerve compression and loss of function, lesions causing major deformity or are significantly disfiguring, lesions of the extremity that cause limb hypertrophy or loss of function, and painful lesions.
- Participant has a target PN that is deemed inoperable, defined as a PN that cannot be completely surgically removed without risk for substantial morbidity due to: encasement of or close proximity to vital structures, invasiveness, or high vascularity of the PN, or the participant refuses surgery.
- Participants must have a target PN, defined as the clinically most relevant PN, amenable to volumetric MRI analysis. For the purpose of this study, the target PN must be seen on at least 3 consecutive MRI slices and the field of view must contain the entire tumor of interest.
- Participants \ge 16 years of age must have had a Karnofsky performance level of \ge 50\%, and participants &lt; 16 years must have had a Lansky performance level of \ge 50\%
L686: Key exclusion criteria included:
L687: - Participant had a Screening ALT value of &gt; 2.0 \times upper limit of normal (ULN);
- Participant had a Screening total bilirubin value of &gt; 1.5 \times ULN (isolated bilirubin &gt; 1.5 \times ULN is acceptable if bilirubin is fractionated and direct bilirubin &lt; 35\%);
- Any clinically significant active or known history of liver disease, or known hepatic or biliary abnormalities (with the exception of Gilbert's syndrome or asymptomatic gallstones);
- Participants with evidence of an active optic glioma or other low-grade glioma, requiring treatment with chemotherapy or radiation therapy. Participants not requiring treatment were eligible. Ophthalmological findings secondary to long-standing optic pathway glioma (such as visual loss, optic nerve pallor or strabismus) or long-standing orbito-
L688: temporal PN (such as visual loss, strabismus) will NOT be considered a significant abnormality for the purposes of the study;
L689: - Pregnant or breast-feeding females
- Participant recorded a left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF)&lt;55% at Screening or within 3 years of signing informed consent/assent, OR has a history of congestive heart failure;
- Participant had a history of, or evidence of, retinal pathology on ophthalmologic examination that is considered a risk factor for central serous retinopathy, retinal vein occlusion (RVO), or neovascular macular degeneration. Participants were excluded from study participation if they have any of the following risk factors for RVO at Screening:Intraocular pressure &gt; 21 mmHg;
- Serum cholesterol &gt; 300 mg/dL;
- Serum triglycerides &gt; 300 mg/dL;
- Hyperglycemia (fasting blood glucose &gt; 125 mg/dL or random blood glucose &gt; 200 mg/dL);
- Age specific hypertensionParticipants \ge 13 years of age with a blood pressure \ge 140/90 mm Hg
- Participants \le 12 years of age with a blood pressure \ge 95th percentile for age +12 mmHg
- Participant had a history of glaucoma;
- Participant had received NF1 PN-targeted therapy (e.g., farnesyltransferase inhibitors, kinase inhibitors, etc.) within 45 days of first dose of study treatment (or 5.5 half-lives, whichever is longer). All toxicities from prior therapy must resolve to \le Grade 1 or Baseline;
- Participant previously received or is currently receiving therapy with mirdametinib or any other MEK1/2 inhibitor (e.g., selumetinib, trametinib, cobimetinib, binimetinib, etc.).
L690: ## Dose Selection
L691: Mirdametinib was administered orally at a dose of 2 mg/m2 BID with a maximum dose of 4 mg BID on a 28-day Cycle (4-week course) with a 3 week on/1 week off schedule. The nomograms used for the capsule and dispersible tablet formulation are described in Table 27 and Table 28.
L692: **Table 27: Study MEK-NF-201 Capsule dosage form**
L693: BSA (m2)	0.4 to 0.69	0.7 to 1.04	1.05 to 1.49	\ge 1.5
BID unit dose	1 mg	2 mg	3 mg	4 mg
L694: BSA = body surface area; mg = milligrams
L695: All study dosing will be administered on a 3 weeks on/1 week off intermittent schedule

L700: ## Dose Modifications
L701: In the event of significant toxicity, dosing must have been interrupted and/or dose reduced as described in Table 29.
L702: **Table 29: Criteria For Dose Modification For Study Drug Related Adverse Events**
L703: RELATED ADVERSE EVENTS	INTERVENTION
OCULAR/VISUAL
Any Grade ≤ 2 event.	Continue dosing and perform ophthalmologic exams every 2 to 4 weeks until resolution to Grade ≤ 1 or baseline.
Any Grade ≥ 3 event except RVO.	Interrupt dosing; if resolution to Grade ≤ 1 or baseline occurs within 14 days, re-start study treatment at the next lower dose following Tables 30 and 31. Consider ophthalmologic exams every 2 to 4 weeks.
Grade ≥ 3 RVO	Permanently discontinue study treatment
NEUROLOGICAL
Grade ≤ 2 events such as hallucination, confusion or delirium lasting more than 24 hours AND after ruling out other possible causes.	Interrupt dosing; if resolution to Grade ≤ 1 or baseline occurs within 14 days, re-start study treatment at the next lower dose following Tables 30 and 31.
Any Grade ≥ 3 event.	Withhold dose and discuss with the Medical Monitor. Consider permanent discontinuation after re-evaluating the benefit risk of ongoing study drug treatment
MUSCULOSKELETAL
Grade ≥ 3 muscle weakness (symptomatic and interfering with activities of daily living).	Interrupt dosing; if resolution to Grade ≤ 1 or baseline occurs within 14 days, re-start study treatment at the next lower dose following Tables 30 and 31.
DERMATOLOGICAL
L704: Grade \ge 3 acneiform rash.	Interrupt dosing; if resolution to Grade \le 1 or baseline occurs within 14 days, restart study treatment at the next lower dose following Tables 30 and 31
CARDIAC
Asymptomatic, absolute decrease in LVEF of \le 10\% from baseline and is below institutional lower limit of normal (LLN).	Interrupt dosing; if LVEF value improves to above the institutional LLN or baseline, resume at the same dose, if supported by the investigator's assessment of the benefit-risk ratio.
If LVEF does not improve to above the institutional LLN or baseline within 14 days, permanently discontinue study treatment.
Asymptomatic, absolute reduction in LVEF of > 10\% to \le 19\% from baseline and is below institutional lower limit of normal (LLN).	Interrupt dosing; if LVEF value improves to above the institutional LLN or baseline within 14 days, resume at the next lower dose level following Table 5.
If LVEF does not improve to above the institutional LLN or baseline within 14 days, permanently discontinue study treatment.
Symptomatic congestive heart failure.
Absolute decrease in LVEF of greater than 20% from baseline.	Permanently discontinue study treatment.
LIVER (Refer to Figure 6)
OTHER
Grade \ge 3 hematologic toxicities or clinically significant Grade 2 hematologic abnormalities, that do not resolve within 72 hours after initiation of medical management.	Interrupt dosing; if resolution to Grade \le 1 occurs within 14 days, restart study treatment at the next lower dose following Tables 30 and 31.
Grade \ge 3 nonhematologic toxicity that is not controlled by optimal supportive medication.	Interrupt dosing; if resolution to Grade \le 1 or baseline occurs within 14 days, restart study treatment at the next lower dose following Tables 30 and 31.
Other Grade 2 toxicity that is subjectively intolerable (except alopecia) and not controlled by optimal supportive medication.	Interrupt dosing; if resolution to Grade \le 1 or baseline occurs within 14 days, re-start study treatment at the same dose.
Recurrent subjectively intolerable toxicity (at least a week interruption on 2 occasions) that is not controlled by optimal supportive medication.	Interrupt dosing; if resolution to Grade \le 1 or baseline occurs within 14 days, restart study treatment at the next lower dose using Tables 30 and 31. If toxicity is still intolerable, permanently discontinue study treatment

L731: AE = adverse event; d = day(s); ECG = electrocardiogram; EOT = end of treatment; IRT = interactive response technology; LTFU = Long-Term Follow-up; MRI = magnetic resonance imaging; PedsQL = Pediatric Quality of Life Inventory; PN = plexiform neurofibroma; P-OMAQ = Pediatric Oral Medicines Acceptability Questionnaire; SAE = serious adverse event; SFU = safety follow-up; WOCBP = women of childbearing potential
L732: ## Study Endpoints
L733: ## The Applicant's Description:
L734: ## Primary Endpoint:
L735: - The primary efficacy endpoint was confirmed ORR at the end of the Treatment Phase (defined as PN decrease \ge 20\% compared to baseline in consecutive scans within 2-6 months) using centrally read MRI volumetric analysis.
L736: The target PN was selected by the local investigator as the most clinically relevant PN, amenable to volumetric MRI analysis. Tumor response was evaluated at screening and during Cycles 5, 9, 13, 17, 21, and 24 during the Treatment Phase. For participants that elected to continue mirdametinib administration in the Long-Term Follow-up Phase, additional tumor evaluations were conducted during Cycle 28 and every 4 cycles thereafter.
L737: All tumor volume measurements were independently calculated by two BICR readers. In the case that the two readers generated measurements representing different categorical tumor response statuses, an independent adjudicator made the final decision on which reader's assessment was the best representation of tumor response and the selected response category was recorded at that visit. The primary endpoint analyses were performed using the adjudicator-selected reader for those visits where the two BICR readers did not agree.
L738: To determine the level of target tumor response (complete response [CR], partial response [PR], stable disease [SD], or progressive disease [PD]) which are described below, post-baseline measurement scans are compared to the target PN volume in the pre-treatment MRI scan using volumetric data analysis:
L739: - CR: A complete resolution of the target PN
- PR: A \ge 20\% reduction in the volume of the target PN lesion compared to baseline
- SD: Between a &lt; 20\% increase and &lt; 20\% decrease in the volume of the target PN lesion compared to baseline
- PD: A \ge 20\% increase in the volume of the target PN compared to baseline.
(Radiographic disease progression)
L740: ## Secondary Endpoints:
L741: - Safety endpoints included incidence of treatment emergent adverse events (TEAEs), changes in laboratory parameters, vital signs, physical examination findings, and electrocardiograms (ECGs). Incidence of TEAEs was assessed by dose formulation. Tolerability was assessed according to toxicities graded by NCI CTCAE v5.0.
- DoR for participants whose best overall confirmed response was CR or PR using centrally
L742: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L743: read MRI volumetric analysis.
L744: - Change from baseline at Cycle 13 in QoL assessed by the age-specific PedsQL.
- Change from baseline at Cycle 13 in pain severity and pain interference assessed using the NRS-11 and PII.
L745: ## **Key Exploratory Endpoints:**
L746: - Change from baseline in physical function status (PROMIS – Physical Function/Mobility/Upper Extremity Scale) in the Treatment Phase.
- Patient global impression of change (PGIC) and patient global impression of severity (PGIS) scores throughout the Treatment Phase.
- Change from baseline in localized strength (dynamometer and Medical Research Council Muscle Scale), range of motion, and endurance (6-minute walk test) of PN-associated functional impairment in the Treatment Phase.
- Time to progression (TTP) and progression free survival (PFS) in progressive PN
- Pediatric Oral Medicines Acceptability Questionnaire (P-OMAQ) scores recorded as self-report and by caregiver.
- Change from baseline in target tumor volume by centrally read MRI volumetric analysis. Best volumetric percentage change from baseline as well as the time to the best volumetric percentage change from baseline.
- DCR (defined as a confirmed BOR of SD or better post-baseline during the Treatment Phase) measured by centrally read MRI volumetric analysis.
- Time to response for participants whose best overall confirmed response was CR or PR using centrally read MRI volumetric analysis.
L747: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L748: FDA generally agrees with the description of the trial design and endpoints. Notably, under Response Evaluation in Neurofibromatosis and Schwannomatosis (REiNS) criteria, a partial response (PR) is defined as a decrease in the volume of the target lesion by 20% or more compared to baseline and confirmed within 3-6 months, and sustained when the response is maintained for at least 6 months. In contrast, the Applicant considered a \ge 20\% decrease in PN from baseline across consecutive scans within 2-6 months. A sensitivity analysis was conducted, which showed minimal impact on the ORR outcome in pediatric cohort and slightly larger impact in adult cohort. The details of the rationale for the modified criteria and the sensitivity analysis are provided in section 8.1.1.1.
L749: FDA considers the external control data to be supportive in a descriptive manner and as such the data from the placebo arm of Study 01-C-0222 and the natural history Study 08-C-0079 were not independently confirmed and will not be included in FDA's assessment of efficacy.
L750: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments**
L751: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L752: 132
L753: *Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)*
L754: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.*
L755: Two draft versions of the statistical analysis plan (SAP) dated 20Sep2022 and 31Jan2023 were submitted to FDA for review and feedback. The final SAP (v1.0) was dated 02Nov2023.
L756: ## ***Primary Efficacy Endpoint***
L757: The primary efficacy endpoint was the confirmed ORR by the end of Treatment Phase (i.e., Cycle 24). The primary endpoint was based on the full analysis set (FAS), defined as the set of all participants who received at least one dose of mirdametinib. The confirmed ORR was defined as the proportion of participants who had a confirmed \ge 20\% reduction in target tumor volume as compared to baseline as assessed by a blinded independent central review (BICR), and the response needs to be confirmed by BICR in a consecutive tumor assessment within 2- 6 months.
L758: All tumor volume measurements were independently calculated by two BICR readers. In the case that the two readers generated measurements representing different categorical tumor response statuses, an independent adjudicator made the final decision on which reader's assessment was the best representation of tumor response and the selected response category was recorded at that visit. The primary endpoint analyses were performed using the adjudicator-selected reader for those visits where the two BICR readers did not agree.
L759: Only tumor assessments during the Treatment Phase were considered for the determination of confirmed response. Participants who did not achieve a confirmed response were categorized as non-responders. Participants who did not have a post-baseline MRI assessment were also categorized as non-responders and their best overall response was deemed as 'Missing'.
L760: To assess the primary efficacy endpoint, confirmed ORRs by the end of the Treatment Phase as assessed by BICR were presented as percentages alongside the corresponding 2-sided 95% Clopper-Pearson CIs and the p-values from one-sample two-sided binomial tests for the confirmed ORR compared to the minimum clinically relevant response rates (23% in adult participants and 20% in pediatric participants). The overall assessment of the endpoint was based on the exclusion of the minimum clinically relevant response rates by the lower tail of the 95% CIs.
L761: A sensitivity analysis was performed that mirrored this primary efficacy analysis but used the per-protocol set (PPS), defined as the set of all participants in the FAS who had completed at least one post-baseline disease evaluation and who did not have any major CSR reportable deviations, including but not limited to efficacy assessments or compliance, rather than the FAS.
L762: ## ***Secondary Efficacy Endpoints***
L763: ## *Duration of Response*
L764: Duration of response was defined as the time in months between the first instance of response that was subsequently confirmed, until the date of radiographic disease progression or death,
L765: whichever occurred first. The DoR was summarized in months as follows: (date of event [i.e., radiographic disease progression or death] or censoring – date of first response + 1) / 365.25 \times 12.
L766: For participants who entered the LTFU Phase, all MRI assessments in both the Treatment Phase and LTFU Phase were used to determine DoR. Those who achieved a DoR of at least 12 months were considered to have experienced a durable response.
L767: Participants without radiographic disease progression or death while on study had their results censored to their most recent adequate (i.e., evaluable) tumor assessment date.
L768: DoR was analyzed using Kaplan-Meier (KM) methods, with the median, lower, and upper quartile estimates of DoR and their associated 95% CIs using the Brookmeyer and Crowley method.
L769: ## Patient Reported Outcomes
L770: All PROs analyzed in this study were assessed using the mean change from baseline as well as by the proportion of participants who achieved a clinically meaningful change from baseline at the Cycle 13 visit. Cycle 13 was selected as the timepoint that will best allow the treatment effect to be captured in PRO scores. It was projected that a strong majority of participants who present a response would do so at or before Cycle 13, with the median time of response expected to be Cycle 5 to Cycle 9, so any associated pain or QoL improvements should be captured at the Cycle 13 visit. Choosing a later cycle would have captured few additional responses, but may have been susceptible to increased amounts of missing data.
L771: For PedsQL, distribution-based thresholds were &gt;0.5 \times \text{StD}, where StD was calculated from the baseline scale data. MCTs were defined separately for adult, pediatric self-report, and pediatric parent proxy data.
L772: For NRS-11, distribution-based thresholds were presented using change from baseline of \le -1 (this cut point has previously been researched and assessed as a meaningful change in pediatric populations), representing a clinically meaningful improvement, as well as change from baseline greater than the within-patient MCT.
L773: Distribution-based MCTs were applied to interpret the change from baseline results as well as the number and percentage of participants with a clinically meaningful change for PII. Distribution-based thresholds were &lt; -0.5 \times \text{StD} (using StD from the baseline scores).
L774: ## Exploratory Efficacy Endpoints
L775: ## Change from Baseline in physical function status (PROMIS)
L776: Actual values and change from baseline were summarized by visit and confirmed objective response status for each PROMIS Scale, with self-report and parent proxy records summarized
L777: separately.
L778: ## Strength
L779: The strength measured by the dynamometer by each area location was summarized and the percent change from baseline using standard summary statistics was summarized by age cohort and by visit.
L780: ## Range of Motion
L781: The range of motion for that location was summarized and the percent change using standard summary statistics was summarized by age cohort and by visit.
L782: ## 6-Minute Walk Test
L783: Standard summary statistics of length walked and the pre-/post vitals were summarized by each visit.
L784: ## Patient Global Impression of Change/Severity
L785: The absolute count and percentage of participants in each of the PGIS and PGIC categories were summarized separately by visit and by confirmed objective response status.
L786: ## Pediatric Oral Medicines Acceptability Questionnaire
L787: Descriptive statistics were used to summarize P-OMAQ. P-OMAQ-P (parent) and P-OMAQ-C (child) individual question scores and total scores were summarized separately.
L788: ## Disease Control Rate
L789: The number and percentage of participants who achieved disease control (i.e. BOR of SD or better) by the end of the treatment phase were presented by age cohort, alongside the corresponding 2-sided Clopper-Pearson 95% CIs.
L790: ## Time to Response
L791: Time to response was summarized for confirmed responders by age cohort and is defined as the time in months from the start of treatment to the date of first response that was subsequently confirmed. The time to response was summarized for the primary endpoint and by each of the two readers separately for the FAS.
L792: ## Change from Baseline in Target tumor volume
L793: Change from baseline in tumor volume, and percentage change from baseline in tumor volume was summarized descriptively by visit for each age cohort separately. Results were presented by averaged tumor volumes across the two readers (unless response adjudication was
L794: performed) and reported separately for each reader. A Mixed Models for Repeated Measures (MMRM) was fit separately by age cohort with change from baseline in tumor volume as the dependent variable, visit as a fixed effect and baseline tumor volume as a covariate. LS Means estimates were presented with associated standard errors, 95% CIs and p-values by visit.
L795: ## *Best Volumetric Percentage Change from Baseline*
L796: Best volumetric percentage change from baseline as well as the time, in months, to the best volumetric percentage change from baseline was summarized descriptively using averaged tumor volumes across the two readers (unless response adjudication was performed) and reported separately for each reader.
L797: ## *Time to Progression and Progression-Free Survival*
L798: Time to progression (TTP) and progression-free survival (PFS) were analyzed separately for each age cohort using the Kaplan-Meier (KM) method; with median, lower, and upper quartile estimate and associated 95% CIs (using the Brookmeyer-Crowley method), the number of participants with events or who are censored, as well as reason for censoring being summarized.
L799: ## ***External Control***
L800: ## *Yearly Percentage Change from Baseline in Target PN Volume*
L801: For each cohort, an analysis of covariance (ANCOVA) model was applied for treatment comparison between participants in MEK-NF-201 and the historical control at each year. Comparisons of this endpoint were made at each year up to 3 years. Since the 2 studies for historical controls did not have the same assessment timepoints as in MEK-NF-201, a window of \pm 6 months was used for the comparison timepoints at months 12, 24, 36, etc. This rule created the following windows in months for comparison:
L802: - Month 12: (6, 18]
- Month 24: (18, 30]
- Month 36: (30, 42]
- Last assessment time point.
L803: Within each window, the assessment timepoint closest to the yearly comparison timepoint was selected to calculate the percentage change from baseline in target PN volume. Comparisons were performed if each treatment group has at least 5 participants for yearly percentage change from baseline in target PN volume after propensity score matching. In the model, the yearly percentage change from baseline in target PN volume was the dependent variable, and age and target PN volume at baseline were the covariates.
L804: For each comparison, the assessment time from baseline in months [= (time in
L805: days)\times(12/365.25)] and percentage change in tumor volume per-year was summarized with and without modeling.
L806: ## Time to Progression
L807: TTP for each cohort was analyzed using the KM method, with quartiles and associated 95% CI calculated using the log-log transformation, if estimable. Survival curves for both treatment groups in the same cohort were presented. Within each cohort, the p-value for treatment comparison was calculated using a log rank test; the hazard ratio and its 95% CI for Study MEK-NF-201 versus Studies 01-C-0222 and 08-C-0079 were estimated with a Cox regression model using treatment group, age, and target PN volume at baseline as covariates. Participants not experiencing radiographic disease progression were censored on the last adequate MRI assessment that verified lack of disease progression.
L808: ## Progression Free Survival
L809: Progression free survival (PFS) was analyzed using the KM method, with quartiles and associated 95% CI calculated using the log-log transformation, if estimable. Survival curves for both treatment groups in the same cohort were presented. Within each cohort, the p-value for treatment comparison was calculated using a log rank test; the hazard ratio and its 95% CI for the Study MEK-NF-201 versus Studies 01-C-0222 and 08-C-0079 were estimated with a Cox regression model using treatment group, age, and target PN volume at baseline as covariates. Participants not experiencing radiographic disease progression or death were censored on the last adequate MRI assessment that verified lack of disease progression or death.
L810: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L811: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's description of study endpoints.
L812: FDA did not use inferential procedures to evaluate the results of this single arm trial. Instead, FDA relied on overall benefit: risk assessment to evaluate the outcome across primary and secondary endpoints. This evaluation included both the magnitude of BICR-assessed confirmed ORR as well as sufficiently mature duration of response and whether the observed confirmed response rate and durability of the response outweigh any concerns over toxicity and safety in the context of other available therapies.
L813: Due to the benign nature of NF1-PN, FDA considered it important to capture improvement in PN-related symptoms correlating with radiographic responses. The ReNeu study utilized various COA measures to evaluate changes in PN-related functional impairment, symptoms, and disfigurement. See section 8.1 for discussion of COA results.
L814: FDA considered the external control data as supplementary information, appropriate only for cautious descriptive interpretation. The analyses of exploratory endpoints using external control were not verified by the FDA.

(17May2019)	63	A safety run-in cohort was added to the pediatric stratum (stratum 1).
Changed the MRC grading assessment from option to a required strength assessment.
The 6-minute walk test was added as an assessment for participants that have a target PN causing airway or lower extremity motor dysfunction.

Specific study stopping criteria was added. The definition of an overdose was changed from > 12 mg to > 150% of the scheduled dose, within a 24-hour period.
The collection of whole blood for PBMC pharmacodynamic analysis was removed and subsequently the approximate amount of blood required from each participant over the course of the study was decreased.

L832: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L833: The Applicant's description of the protocol amendments is consistent with what was provided in the submission. FDA notes that in Amendment 1, the inclusion criteria were revised so that patients with progressive PN and no associated morbidities were not eligible to enroll. We also note that while inclusion criterion number 5 was modified in Amendment 2 to specify that he PN must be analyzable by volumetrics using the REiNS criteria, a modified version of REiNS criteria was utilized in this study as described in Section 8.1, Efficacy results. As described by the Applicant in Amendment 4, the primary endpoint language was modified to specify that the ORR will be assessed at the end of the Treatment Phase, therefore, patients with objective responses during the long-term follow-up phase were not included in FDA's analysis.
L834: ## 8.1.1.1. Study Results
L835: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L836: ## **Data:**
L837: This study was designed and monitored in accordance with sponsor procedures, which comply with the ethical principles of Good Clinical Practice (GCP) as required by the major regulatory authorities, and in accordance with the Declaration of Helsinki.
L838: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L839: Study MEK-NF-201 is GCP compliant.
L840: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L841: FDA acknowledges the Applicant's statement of compliance with GCP requirements.
L842: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L843: ## **Data:**
L844: All participant data relating to the study were entered into the electronic case report forms (eCRFs) unless transmitted to the sponsor or designee electronically (e.g., laboratory data).
L845: The investigator was responsible for verifying that data entries were accurate and correct by electronically signing the eCRF. The investigator maintained accurate documentation (source data) that supported the information entered in the eCRF. The investigator permitted study-related monitoring, audits, Institutional review board (IRB)/Independent ethics committee (IEC) review, and regulatory agency inspections and provided direct access to source data documents.
L846: Monitoring details describing strategy methods, responsibilities, and requirements, including handling of noncompliance issues and monitoring techniques (central, remote, or on-site monitoring) were indicated in the monitoring plan to ensure the protocol and GCP was followed.
L847: The sponsor or designee was responsible for the data management of this study including quality checking of the data. The sponsor assumed accountability for actions delegated to other individuals (e.g., Contract Research Organizations).
L848: Study monitors performed ongoing source data verification to confirm that data entered into the eCRF by authorized site personnel were accurate, complete, and verifiable from source documents; that the safety and rights of participants were being protected; and that the study was being conducted in accordance with the currently approved protocol and any other study agreements, International Conference on Harmonization (ICH) GCP, and all applicable regulatory requirements.
L849: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L850: Records and documents, including signed ICFs, pertaining to the conduct of this study were retained by the investigator according to specifications in the ICH guidelines, local regulations, or as specified in the clinical trial agreement, whichever was longer. No records were to be destroyed during the retention period without the written approval of the sponsor. No records were to be transferred to another location or party without written notification to the sponsor.
L851: SpringWorks conducts clinical studies according to procedures that incorporate the ethical principles of GCP. To ensure compliance with these procedures and to assess the adequacy of quality control procedures, SpringWorks undertakes a GCP audit program.
L852: Audits were performed by SpringWorks Quality Assurance (or designees) operating independently of the study monitors. The audits within the clinical program were aimed at study documentation, investigator sites, and clinical study reports (CSRs).
L853: The audit program, together with SpringWorks' internal quality control procedures, provide reassurance that study conclusions were based on valid procedures for data management and analysis, and that the clinical study program was carried out in accordance with GCP guidelines.
L854: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L855: The study conduct, quality and integrity were appropriate to allow adequate assessment of the data.
L856: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L857: FDA did not identify quality or integrity issues in the data. The data submitted were organized and adequate to perform a complete review of the efficacy of mirdametinib.
L858: ## **Financial Disclosure**
L859: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L860: All investigators have been researched for conflicts of interest. None were identified as having financial interests/arrangements with the sponsor.
L861: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L862: In accordance with 21 CFR Part 54, the Applicant submitted Form FDA 3454 (Financial Interests and Arrangements of Clinical Investigators) certifying that as the sponsor of the submitted studies, they did not enter into any financial arrangement with the listed clinical investigators whereby the value of compensation to the investigator could be affected by the outcome of the study. All investigators were assessed for equity interest, significant payments of other sorts, and other compensation by the sponsor or proprietary interest. The Applicant certified that none of the financial interests or arrangements described in 21 CFR Part 54 existed for any of the investigators who participated in Study MEK-NF-201. The Applicant provided a list of all investigators for Study MEK-NF-201 in Appendix 16.1.4 of the CSR.
L863: FDA reviewed the financial disclosure information provided by the Applicant and agrees that the integrity of the study data was not affected by the financial interest of the investigators.

L877: ## The Applicant's Position:
L878: A total of 56 participants in the pediatric cohort were exposed to at least one dose of study treatment. During the Treatment Phase, 19 (34%) participants prematurely discontinued treatment; the most common (&gt; 10%) reason for premature treatment discontinuation was radiographic PD (9 [16%] participants). Of the 33 (59%) participants in the pediatric cohort eligible for the LTFU Phase, 28 (85%) participants entered the LTFU Phase. As of the data cutoff, 24 (86% of those who entered the LTFU Phase) participants are ongoing treatment in the LTFU
L879: Phase and 4 (14%) discontinued treatment; reasons for treatment discontinuation included radiographic PD, AE, non-compliance, and other (sponsor request) in 1 [4%] participant each.
L880: A total of 58 participants in the adult cohort were exposed to at least one dose of study treatment. During the Treatment Phase, 27 (47%) participants prematurely discontinued treatment. The most common (&gt; 10%) reasons for premature treatment discontinuation were AE (13 [22%] participants) and participant decision (8 [14%] participants). Of the 31 (53%) participants in the adult cohort eligible for the LTFU Phase, 26 (84%) participants entered the LTFU Phase. As of the data cutoff, 22 (85% of those who entered the LTFU Phase) are ongoing treatment in the LTFU Phase and 4 (15%) discontinued treatment in the LTFU Phase; reasons for treatment discontinuation included participant decision (2 [8%] participants), non-compliance (1 [4%] participant), and other (1 [4%] participant). There was 1 death in the adult cohort during Study MEK-NF-201.
L881: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L882: FDA generally agrees with the description of patient disposition in MEK-NF-201 presented in Table 35. There appears to be differential rates of treatment discontinuation prior to completion of the treatment phase between the pediatric and adult cohorts, particularly for reasons of radiological progression: pediatric cohort (16.1%) compared to the adult cohort (5.2%) and adverse events: pediatric cohort (9%) compared to the adult cohort (22.4%). The higher rate of discontinuation due to PN progression in the pediatric may be attributable to the more rapid growth rate in childhood as compared to adulthood.
L883: ## **Protocol Violations/Deviations**
L884: ## **Data:**
L885: In the pediatric cohort, 3 (5%) participants had at least 1 major protocol deviation and were therefore excluded from the per-protocol set. These deviations included study eligibility; 1 participant did not meet inclusion criterion #5 (their target PN volume at baseline was &lt; 5 mL) and 2 participants did not meet inclusion criterion #3 (reported no morbidity at baseline) and both were enrolled in the study.
L886: In the adult cohort, 6 (10%) participants had at least 1 major protocol deviation and were therefore excluded from the per-protocol set. These deviations included study inclusion (2 [3%] participants; both participants did not meet inclusion criterion #5 and were enrolled in the study with baseline target PN volumes measured &lt; 5 mL) and investigational product (4 [7%] participants; all 4 participants received &lt; 60% prescribed study treatment during the Treatment Phase).
L887: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L888: A CSR-reportable protocol deviation is considered a significant deviation that may impact the accuracy, and/or reliability of the study data or participants rights, safety, or well-being. A
L889: major CSR-reportable protocol deviation would exclude the participant from the per-protocol population and include participants that did not meet inclusion/exclusion criteria, mis-randomizations, mis-stratifications, or may impact accuracy and reliability of the study data.
L890: The 3 participants in the pediatric cohort with major CSR-reportable protocol deviations excluded from the per-protocol set were not expected to impact the data integrity for the analysis. Two additional participants in the pediatric cohort were excluded from the per-protocol set due to not having at least 1 post-baseline disease evaluation. The ORR for the per-protocol set supports the results from the primary analysis for the pediatric cohort (ORR from per-protocol set: 56.9% [95% CI: 42.2 to 70.7] vs ORR from primary analysis: 51.8% [38.0 to 65.3]). These data demonstrate that the lower limit of the 95% CI excludes the null hypothesis of ORR = 20%.
L891: The 6 participants in the adult cohort with major CSR-reportable protocol deviations excluded from the per-protocol set were not expected to impact the data integrity for the analysis. Eight additional participants in the adult cohort were excluded from the per-protocol set due to not having at least 1 post-baseline disease evaluation. The ORR for the per-protocol set supports the results from the primary analysis for the adult cohorts (ORR from per-protocol set: 46.8% [32.1 to 61.9] vs ORR from primary analysis: 41.4% [28.6 to 55.1]. These data demonstrate that the lower limit of the 95% CI excludes the null hypothesis of ORR = 23%.
L892: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L893: FDA generally agrees with the description of protocol violations and deviations and that they are not likely to have impacted the interpretability of the study results.

L929: ## ***External Control Data***
L930: Participant baseline disease characteristics were generally similar between MEK-NF-201 and external control data included for comparison. As Study 01-C-022 only included pediatric participants with a progressive PN, these data were compared against pediatric participants with progressive PN in Study MEK-NF-201 to provide the most accurate comparison. All adult and pediatric participants in Study MEK-NF-201 were compared against adult and pediatric participants enrolled in the external Study 08-C-0079, but disease severity and rate of progression in the external controls were not known, which is a limitation to this evaluation. For comparisons to natural history data, only participants in Study MEK-NF-201 who had both a baseline and post-baseline scan were included for analysis. The Study MEK-NF-201 cohorts for comparison were:
L931: - Pediatric participants with progressive PN: n=34
- Pediatric Participants: n=54
- Adult participants: n=50.
L932: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L933: Disease characteristics were consistent with the disease under study.
L934: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L935: FDA generally agrees that the baseline disease characteristics of patients in the ReNeu study are consistent with the general population of patients with NF1-PN
L936: The FDA views the external control data as supportive in a descriptive manner. The data from Studies 01-C-022 and 08-C-0079 were not independently confirmed and will not be included in FDA's assessment of the efficacy results for the ReNeu study.
L937: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
## Data:
L938: Participant compliance with study treatment was assessed at each visit where study treatment was dispensed. Compliance was assessed by counting returned capsules/tablets in addition to reviewing the dosing diary entries from the home electronic patient-reported outcome (ePRO) device used in the Treatment Phase (participants were trained on the home ePRO device at the Screening Visit). Study treatment exposure is presented in Table 38.
L939: A total of 54 (96%) participants in the pediatric cohort received at least 1 concomitant medication during the Treatment Phase. The most frequently reported (&gt; 30%) Anatomical Therapeutic Chemical (ATC) classifications were anilides (46%), serotonin antagonists (39%), propionic acid derivatives (38%), anti-infectives for the treatment of acne (32%), and other antibiotics for topical use (32%).
L940: A total of 57 (98%) participants in the adult cohort received at least 1 concomitant medication during the Treatment Phase. The most frequently reported (&gt; 30%) ATC classifications were anti-infectives for the treatment of acne (74%), tetracyclines (59%), and other viral vaccines (47%).

L958: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)**
L959: ## Data:
L960: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L961: A summary of the confirmed ORR by the end of the Treatment Phase is provided for the FAS in Table 39. Waterfall plots of best percent change from baseline in tumor volume are provided in Figure 7 (pediatric cohort) and Figure 8 (adult cohort).
L962: The confirmed ORR (95% CI) by BICR was 51.8% (38.0, 65.3) in the pediatric cohort, with no CRs and 29 confirmed PRs. The median (min, max) time to onset of first response that was subsequently confirmed was 7.90 (4.1, 18.8) months for the pediatric cohort. In addition, 1 participant achieved a confirmed PR in the LTFU Phase. Inclusive of this response, the confirmed ORR (95% CI) in the pediatric cohort was 53.6% (39.7, 67.0).
L963: The confirmed ORR (95% CI) by BICR was 41.4% (28.6, 55.1) in the adult cohort, with no CRs and 24 confirmed PRs. The median (min, max) time to first response that was subsequently confirmed was 7.75 (4.0, 19.0) months for the adult cohort. In addition, 2 participants each achieved a confirmed PR in the LTFU Phase. Inclusive of these responses, the confirmed ORR (95% CI) in the adult cohort was 44.8% (31.7, 58.5).

L968: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
L969: ## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L970: p-values were calculated using a one-sample two-sided binomial test compared to the minimally clinically relevant response rates of 23% for the adult cohort and 20% for the pediatric cohort.
L971: Participants who had no post-baseline MRI assessment or no confirmed objective response were treated as non-responders.
L972: Best overall confirmed response status was defined as the best confirmed response prior to disease progression during the Treatment Phase.
L973: Missing contains participants who had no post-baseline assessments.
L974: Source: ADSL, ADEFF
L975: In the pediatric cohort, responses were deep with a median best average percentage change from baseline in target PN tumor volume of -42% and a maximum reduction of 91% (Figure 7). The median (min, max) time to best percentage change from baseline in tumor volume was 13.45 (4.0, 32.7) months.
L976: In the adult cohort, responses were deep with a median best average percentage change from baseline in target PN tumor volume of -41% and a maximum reduction of 90% (Figure 8). The median (min, max) time to best percentage change from baseline in tumor volume was 15.23 (4.0, 40.0) months.

L997: ## ***Yearly Percentage Change from Baseline in Target PN Compared to External Control Data***
L998: For efficacy analyses, one-to-one propensity score matching was used as the balancing method. After one-to-one propensity score matching, variance ratios became within the recommended range of 0.5 to 2, which supports that the groups are well balanced with respect to the selected demographic characteristics (Rubin 2001).
L999: In Cohort 1 (Study MEK-NF-201 pediatric participants with progressive PN and Study 01-C-0222 pediatric participants with progressive PN), participants in Study MEK-NF-201 had a significant decrease in yearly percentage change in the target PN volume while participants from Study 01-C-0222 had increases in PN volume at all the time intervals. After adjusting for potential differences in baselines covariates using one-to-one propensity score matching, the LS mean percentage differences (standard errors) were -43.72 (8.816), -23.76 (5.812), -21.91 (7.874), and -38.72 (8.331), at Year 1, 2, 3, and the last assessment, respectively. This improvement remained significant and robust even after a sensitivity analysis comparing alternative target lesion at baseline.
L1000: In Cohort 2 (Study MEK-NF-201 pediatric participants and Study 08-C-0079 pediatric participants), participants in Study MEK-NF-201 had a significant decrease in yearly percentage change in the target PN volume while participants from Study 08-C-0079 had increases in PN volume at all time intervals. After adjusting for potential differences in baselines covariates using one-to-one propensity score matching, the LS mean percentage differences (standard errors) were -40.63 (6.699), -36.62 (3.891), -27.53 (4.471), and -24.28 (5.701), at Year 1, 2, 3, and the last assessment, respectively.
L1001: In Cohort 3 (Study MEK-NF-201 adult participants and Study 08-C-0079 adult participants), participants in Study MEK-NF-201 had a substantial decrease in yearly percentage change in the target PN volume as compared to an increase in the yearly percentage change in the target PN volume in participants from Study 08-C-0079 at all-time intervals. All results were statistically significant except for Year 3 after propensity score matching. Since only 38% of participants from Study MEK-NF-201 were matched with participants from Study 08-C-0079 after one-to-one propensity score matching without replacement, treatment comparisons based on this matching result need to be interpreted with caution. After adjusting for potential differences in baselines covariates, using IPTW, the LS mean percentage differences (standard errors) were -26.74 (6.470), -24.96 (4.628), -19.79 (5.067), and -27.14 (6.189), at Year 1, 2, 3 and the last assessment, respectively.
L1002: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1003: In the pediatric cohort, there was an ORR of 51.8% (29 of 56 participants dosed; 95% CI 38.0%, 65.3%), as assessed by BICR, during the 24 cycle Treatment Phase. The ORR inclusive of additional responses confirmed in the LTFU Phase was 53.6% (39.7, 67.0). These data demonstrate that the lower limit of the 95% CI excluded the null hypothesis of confirmed ORR =

L1008: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1009: FDA agrees with the results for ORR presented in this section, noting that as a single arm trial with no control for multiplicity in any of the hypothesis tests presented in Table 39 and Table 40, the p-values reported by the Applicant should be interpreted with caution. In addition, there were no complete responses which should be acknowledged when describing responses as "deep."
L1010: The primary endpoint is defined as confirmed ORR at the end of the treatment phase, using centrally read MRI volumetric analysis, where a confirmed response is specified as a PN decrease of \ge 20\% compared to baseline on consecutive scans within 2-6 months. This differs from the REINS criteria for partial response, which defines confirmed PR based on consecutive scans within 3-6 months. The rationale for this modification was primarily to account for patients in whom a first response observed at Cycle 21 would be confirmed at Cycle 24 (end of Treatment Phase) which, per protocol, could occur at 3 months +/- 10 days. Ultimately, three adult patients had a first response at Cycle 21 with a confirmatory scan at 91, 91, and 86 days, falling short of strict REiNs criteria. All three patients continued to have a confirmed response in the long-term follow-up phase. Two patients, one adult, and one pediatric, had unscheduled MRI assessments which led to confirmatory scan intervals of 70 days. The adult patient had an MRI assessment at the scheduled time; however, it was missing the PD/T2 sequence; because this sequence was required according to the Imaging Review Charter v8.0, an unscheduled MRI was conducted at a later date to meet study imaging criteria for volumetric evaluation of the target PN. This patient went on to have progressive disease. The pediatric patient had an
L1011: unscheduled assessment due to an adverse event of upper respiratory infection and the MRI assessment for that cycle was conducted later in the cycle following recovery. This patient went on to have a confirmed response in the long-term follow-up phase. In response to an FDA information request (October 7, 2024), the Applicant provided a sensitivity analysis of confirmed ORR per REINS for both the pediatric and adult cohorts (Table 41). Applying the REINS criteria strictly would remove the 4 adult patients and resulted in confirmed ORR of 35% (95% CI: 22, 48) for the adult cohort. Applying the REINS criteria to remove the pediatric responder resulted in a change in confirmed ORR from 52% (95% CI: 38, 65) to 50% (95% CI: 36,64).
L1012: Sensitivity analyses of confirmed ORR across BICR readers as well as between FAS and PPS populations provide reassurance that trial results are robust.
L1013: The ORR outcomes are generally consistent across subgroups based on demographic and clinical characteristics in both pediatric and adult cohorts, Figure 9 and Figure 10, respectively.

L1023: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L1024: ## Data:
L1025: As of the data cut of 20-Sept-2023, the majority of responders (88% of pediatric responders and 86% of adult responders) were followed for at least 12 months from the onset of response, unless they discontinued earlier. A KM analysis of DoR for participants whose best confirmed response was a CR or PR is provided in Table 41. Swimmer plots of duration of treatment are provided in Figure 11 for the pediatric cohort and Figure 12 for the adult cohort.
L1026: In Study MEK-NF-201, the KM estimated median DoR was not reached for the pediatric cohort due to no events (i.e., PDs or deaths) occurring after a confirmed response as of the data cutoff of 20-Sept-2023 (Table 41). The responses were durable, with 22 (76%) of pediatric responders maintaining a confirmed response for at least 12 months, and no disease progression or deaths being observed (Figure 11). Most participants (27 [93%]) were censored due to no PD/death post response confirmation, 1 (3%) participant was censored due to no adequate tumor assessment post confirmed response, and 1 (3%) participant was censored due to undergoing PN therapy/surgery prior to PD/death. Since 4 out of the 29 pediatric responders had an onset of response less than 12 months from the data cut, updated DoR with a later data cut will be provided as an addendum to the CSR.
L1027: Similar to the pediatric cohort, the Kaplan-Meier estimated median DoR was not reached for the adult cohort due to only 1 event of PD after achieving a confirmed objective response as of the data cutoff (Table 41). The responses were durable, with 18 (75%) of adult responders maintaining an observed response for at least 12 months; 1 PD and no deaths were observed (Figure 12). Of the remaining responders, most participants (20 [87%]) were censored due to no PD/death post response confirmation and 3 (13%) participants were censored due to no adequate tumor assessment post confirmed response. Since 3 out of the 24 adult responders had an onset of response less than 12 months from the data cut, updated DoR with a later data cut will be provided as an addendum to the CSR.

L1046: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1047: At the time of the data cut-off in Study MEK-NF-201 (20Sep2023) 88% of pediatric participants with a confirmed objective response during the 24 cycle Treatment Phase had been followed for at least 12 months from the onset of response, or discontinued study. The K-M estimated median DoR was not reached for the pediatric cohort due to no events occurring as of the data cutoff. The responses were durable, with 22 (76%) of pediatric responders maintaining a confirmed response for at least 12 months, and no disease progression or deaths were observed following a confirmed response. These data demonstrate continuing effect of mirdametinib over time in pediatric participants with NF1-PN.
L1048: At the time of the data cut-off in Study MEK-NF-201 (20Sep2023) 86% of adult participants with a confirmed objective response during the 24 cycle Treatment Phase had been followed for at least 12 months from the onset of response, or discontinued study. The K-M estimated median DoR was not reached for the adult cohort due to a low number of events after achieving a confirmed objective response as of the data cutoff (1 event of PD). The responses were durable, with 18 (75%) of adult responders maintaining an observed response for at least 12 months; 1 PD and no deaths were observed following a confirmed response. These data demonstrate continuing effect of mirdametinib over time in adult participants with NF1-PN.
L1049: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1050: FDA generally agrees with the description of secondary and other relevant endpoints.
L1051: The median DOR was not reached in either the pediatric or adult cohorts due to lack of events. Therefore, the Applicant provided DOR estimates for 8-, 12-, 16- and 24-month landmarks based on the KM rate as shown in Table 41. The Applicant has also included DOR based on observed time which provides additional critical information, as it reflects the conservative estimate.
L1052: A 90-day safety update for DOR, based on a data cut of June 12, 2024, indicates the Kaplan-Meier estimated DOR was still not reached in either pediatric or adult cohorts. Additionally, there was no change in estimated ORRs for both cohorts. A summary of the updated efficacy results including observed DOR is provided in Table 42.

L1059: ## Dose/Dose Response
L1060: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1061: Mirdametinib was dosed in Studies MEK-NF-201 and NF-106 by BSA. The median (minimum, maximum) of actual doses received by participants with NF1 PN was 2.21 mg/m2 (0.806, 2.86) (Figure 4).
L1062: In addition, population and trial simulations were conducted using exposure-efficacy and exposure-safety models to predict the impact of alternative target dose levels from 1.0 to 3.0 mg/m2 BID (1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.2, and 3.0 mg/m2). The simulations support that exposures associated with the proposed marketing dose of 2 mg/m2 of mirdametinib provide an optimal balance of robust efficacy with a tolerable incidence of low severity exposure-related AEs for patients with NF1 PN. Please refer to Section 6.2.2.1 for additional simulation details in support of the proposed 2mg/m2 dose used in Study MEK-NF-201, Figure 4.

L1080: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints
L1081: ## **Data:**
L1082: PRO assessments evaluated as part of the secondary endpoints included quality of life as assessed by PedsQL, pain severity as assessed by NRS-11, and pain interference as assessed by PII, and were assessed at the prespecified Cycle 13 timepoint (Figure 11):
L1083: ## **Pediatric participants:**
L1084: - For the PedsQL PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline in total score at Cycle 13 was 4.01 (n=38; 95% CI: -0.74, 8.76; p = 0.096) by self-report and 5.64 (n=43; 95% CI: 1.75, 9.53; p = 0.005) by parent proxy, demonstrating improvement in quality of life.
- For the NRS-11 PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline at Cycle 13 was -0.79 (n=17; 95% CI: -1.28, -0.29; p = 0.003), demonstrating a decrease in pain severity.
- For the PII PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline at Cycle 13 was -0.46 (n=22; 95% CI: -0.83, -0.09; p = 0.017) by self-report and -0.26 (n=20; 95% CI: -0.49, -0.03; p = 0.025) by parent proxy, demonstrating a decrease in pain interference.
L1085: ## **Adult participants:**
L1086: ## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1087: - For the PedsQL PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline in total score at Cycle 13 was 3.94 (n=34; 95% CI: 0.70, 7.17; p = 0.018), demonstrating improvement in quality of life.
- For the NRS-11 PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline at Cycle 13 was -1.33 (n=21; 95% CI: -1.83, -0.84; p &lt; 0.001), demonstrating a decrease in pain severity.
- For the PII PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline at Cycle 13 was -0.70 (n=22; 95% CI: -1.02, -0.38; p &lt; 0.001, demonstrating a decrease in pain interference.
L1088: Least Squares (LS) mean change from Cycle 1 Day 1 (baseline) over time in PRO estimated from the MMRM analysis through all cycles in pediatric participants showed a significant improvement in parent proxy-reported PedsQL total score, durable and significant reductions from baseline in patient-reported NRS-11 and patient/parent proxy-reported PII, beginning early (at Cycle 3 or 5), and sustained through most cycles (Figure 11). Similarly, in adult participants a significant improvement in PedsQL total score, durable and significant reductions from baseline in NRS-11 and PII, beginning early and sustained throughout the cycles, were observed.
L1089: Additionally, meaningful change threshold (MCT) analyses were performed for each PRO assessment to further describe clinical benefit of mirdametinib using a distribution-based approach and generally demonstrated that the statistically significant PRO findings were associated with clinically meaningful improvements.

L1099: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.*
L1100: A post-hoc exploratory analysis was conducted to assess the correlation of change from baseline in NRS-11 score and percent change from baseline in tumor volume, by cycle, in participants with a baseline NRS-11 score \ge 2. The Spearman rank correlation coefficient (r) was used to measure the strength of this relationship and was assessed as per Cohen (Cohen 1988) using the following ranges (for both positive and negative correlations): r = 0 to 0.3 indicates a weak correlation, r = 0.3 to 0.5 indicates a moderate correlation, and r = 0.5 to 1.0 indicates a strong correlation.
L1101: In the pediatric cohort, a strong positive correlation was observed between percentage change from baseline in tumor volume and change from baseline in NRS-11 score at Cycle 13 (n = 6, r = 0.714) (Figure 14). However, small sample sizes at this time point limit the interpretation of these data.
L1102: In the adult cohort, a weak positive correlation was observed between percent change from baseline in tumor volume and change from baseline in NRS-11 score at Cycle 13 (n = 11, r = 0.164) (Figure 15). However, small sample sizes at this time point limit the interpretation of these data.
L1103: **Figure 14:** Spearman Rank Correlation Plot of Change from Baseline in NRS-11 Score with Percentage Change of Tumor Volume by Cycle in Participants with a Baseline NRS-11 Score \ge 2 – Pediatric Cohort
