L1105: Abbreviations: NRS-11: Numeric Rating Scale-11.
L1106: Source: ADQS, ADTR
L1107: In addition, the P-OMAQ was a clinical outcome assessment conducted as a self-report by pediatric participants (P-OMAQ-P), with an associated adult caregiver observer report (P-OMAQ-C) assessing the acceptability of the tablet dosage formulation.
L1108: The P-OMAQ-P was completed by 13 pediatric participants aged \ge 8 years who received the tablet formulation of mirdametinib. The median (min, max) P-OMAQ-P (pediatric) overall score was 4.7 (3, 5), indicating very good acceptability. A median of 5 (highest score) was achieved for most categories, including number of medicines per day, size/amount of medication, smell of medication, acceptability, and taste.
L1109: The P-OMAQ-C was completed by 17 adult caregivers of participants aged 6 months to 17 years who received the tablet formulation of mirdametinib. The median (min, max) P-OMAQ-C
L1110: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1111: (caregiver) overall score was 4.8 (3.1, 5), indicating very good acceptability. A median of 5 (highest score) was achieved for all categories, including number of medicines per day, size/amount of medication, smell of medication, acceptability, and taste.
L1112: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1113: Statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvements were observed in PRO measures at the pre-specified timepoint of Cycle 13.
L1114: ## **Pediatric Participants:**
L1115: - For the parent-proxy PedsQL PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline in total score at Cycle 13 indicated improved quality of life.
- For the NRS-11 PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline at Cycle 13 indicated decreased pain severity.
- For the PII PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline at Cycle 13 indicated decreased pain interference.
- The tablet formulation was acceptable as indicated by the P-OMAQ-P (pediatric) overall score of 4.7 (3, 5) and P-OMAQ-C (caregiver) overall score of 4.8 (3.1, 5).
L1116: ## **Adult participants:**
L1117: - For the PedsQL PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline in total score at Cycle 13 indicated improved quality of life.
- For the NRS-11 PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline at Cycle 13 indicated decreased pain severity.
L1118: For the PII PRO assessment, the LS mean change from baseline at Cycle 13 indicated decreased pain interference.
L1119: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1120: FDA does not use inferential testing procedures to assess single arm trial results, including for PRO-based endpoints. There was no control for multiplicity in any of the hypothesis tests included in this report, therefore, claims of statistical significance reported by the Applicant should be interpreted with caution.
L1121: FDA notes some observed improvements in functioning relative to baseline in patients treated with mirdametinib, however notes the following limitations in the PRO data:
L1122: - The PRO data from a single arm trial with a small, heterogenous (age, location of PN targets) population may be difficult to accurately interpret.
- Compliance at Cycle 13 for all PROs except the PedsQL was below 70%. In the pediatric population, compliance with the NRS-11 was 41% at cycle 13 and for pain interference it was 57%. Compliance rate for the PedsQL was high at cycle 13 for all patients (&gt;90%).
L1123: - Patients in the adult cohort were administered the PedsQL, which is not designed for patients age greater than 18 however the PedsQL scoring manual does include instructions on scoring for adult patients.
- The magnitude of change from baseline for adult patients was greater than observed for pediatric patients, but as noted above, there was more missing data in the pediatric cohort.
- P-OMAQ was included in protocol amendment #4 and the sample size of patients is too small to draw any conclusions.
L1124: Overall, these exploratory results generally support the primary clinical findings however have some substantial limitations that should be considered when interpreting these results.
L1125: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L1126: ## Data:
L1127: Analysis of exploratory endpoints showed the following for the pediatric cohort:
L1128: - The overall median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 13 for the PROMIS mobility scale score was 7.0 (3, 11) by self-report and 3.0 (-3, 11) by parent proxy.
- The median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 13 for the PROMIS upper extremity scale score was 11.5 (9, 14) by self-report and 9.5 (7, 12) by parent proxy.
- The median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 13 was 4.7 (1, 95) lbs in strength assessment using MicroFET2 dynamometer. The median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 13 in strength assessment using MRC grading was 0.1 (0, 1); given the median score at baseline was 4.4 out of 5, there was not much room for improvement in this assessment.
- The median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 13 in range of motion was 15.0 (-189, 70) degrees.
- The median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 13 in the 6-minute walk test distance was 15.0 (-99, 55) meters.
- The median TTP was not reached for all participants or for participants with a progressive tumor at baseline due to few events of disease progression.External Control Data: Cohort 1There were substantially more events in Study 01-C-0222 and the median was reached; the medians of TTP (95% CI) in months were 13.40 (8.50, 18.80) before matching, 12.80 (8.50, 18.80) after propensity score matching, and 13.40 (8.50, 18.80) after IPTW.
- The HR for TTP comparing participants from Study MEK-NF-201 to those in Study 01-C-0222 showed statistically significant
L1129: improvements in disease progression favoring Study MEK-NF-201 (p-value &lt;0.0001). The HR (95% CI) was 0.13 (0.052, 0.338) before matching, 0.14 (0.049, 0.392) after propensity score matching, and 0.12 (0.062, 0.250) after IPTW.
L1130: ## ■ External Control Data: Cohort 2
L1131: - There were substantially more events in participants from Study 08-C-0079 and the median was reached; the medians TTP (95% CI) in months were 19.71 (16.13, 24.15) before matching, 21.75 (16.36, 32.30) after propensity score matching, and 20.63 (16.36, 30.62) after IPTW.
- The HR for TTP comparing participants from Study MEK-NF-201 to from Study 08-C-0079 showed statistically significant (p-value &lt;0.001) improvements in disease progression favoring Study MEK-NF-201. The HR (95% CI) was 0.22 (0.107, 0.456) before matching, 0.24 (0.110, 0.507) after propensity score matching, and 0.26 (0.161, 0.416) after IPTW.
- The median PFS was not reached for all participants or for participants with a progressive tumor at baseline in Study MEK-NF-201 or in Study 01-C-0222.
- ■ External Control Data: Cohort 2In contrast, there were substantially more events in the historical control and the median was reached; the median PFS (95% CI) in months were 19.71 (16.13, 24.15) before matching, 21.75 (16.36, 32.30) after propensity score matching, and 20.63 (16.36, 30.62) after IPTW.
- The average assessment intervals in Study 08-C-0079 were much longer compared to Study MEK-NF-201, which could be a bias in favor of the historical control. However, there was still a statistically significant reduction in the risk of disease progression or death for participants from Study MEK-NF-201 compared to participants from Study 08-C-0079. The HR (95% CI) was 0.22 (0.106, 0.455) before matching, 0.24 (0.110, 0.508) after propensity score matching, and 0.26 (0.160, 0.415) after IPTW.
- The DCR was 91% (95% CI: 80.4, 97.0). Results were similar by reader.
- The tablet formulation was acceptable as indicated by the P-OMAQ-P (pediatric) overall score of 4.7 (3, 5) and P-OMAQ-C (caregiver) overall score of 4.8 (3.1, 5).
L1132: Analysis of exploratory endpoints showed the following for the adult cohort:
L1133: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1134: - The overall median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 13 for the PROMIS physical function scale score was 1.00 (-10.9, 20.6).
- The median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 13 was 14.5 (-31, 468) lbs in strength assessment using MicroFET2 dynamometer. The median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 13 in strength assessment using MRC grading was 0 (0, 1); given the median score at baseline was 4.5 out of 5, there was not much room for improvement in this assessment.
- The median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 13 in range of motion was 9.5 (-49, 102) degrees.
- The median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 13 in the 6-minute walk test distance was 5.5 (-161, 79) meters.
- The median TTP was not reached for all participants or for participants with a progressive tumor at baseline.
L1135: ## ▪ External Control Data: Cohort 3
L1136: One-to-one propensity score also yielded a substantially reduced sample size. Only 19 adult participants from Study MEK-NF-201 (38%) were matched with adults from Study 08-C-0079. The remaining participants were not matched due to differences in their propensity score after adjusting for age, MEK inhibitor treatment, and lesion type. The reduced sample size and the small number of events make interpretation of the TTP difficult, and the results of this analysis in Cohort 3 are thus inconclusive.
L1137: - The median PFS was not reached for all participants or for participants with a progressive tumor at baseline.
L1138: ## ▪ External Control Data: Cohort 3
L1139: - One-to-one propensity score also yielded a substantially reduced sample size. Only 19 adult participants from Study MEK-NF-201 (38%) were matched with adults from Study 08-C-0079. The remaining participants were not matched due to differences in their propensity score after adjusting for age, MEK inhibitor treatment, and lesion type. The reduced sample size and the small number of events make interpretation of the PFS difficult, and the results of this analysis in Cohort 3 are thus inconclusive.
L1140: - The DCR was 86.2% (95% CI: 74.6, 93.9).
L1141: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1142: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1143: The exploratory PRO endpoints demonstrated improvements in mobility, upper extremity function, strength, range of motion, and 6-minute walk test within the pediatric cohort. Median TTP and PFS were both not reached. Additionally, the tablet formulation achieved high scores on the P-OMAQ-P and P-OMAQ-C, indicating high acceptability among the pediatric cohort.
L1144: Exploratory efficacy endpoints also supported clinical benefit of mirdametinib in the adult cohort. The exploratory PRO endpoints demonstrated improvements in mobility, upper extremity scores, strength, range of motion, and 6-minute walk test. Median TTP and PFS were both not reached.
L1145: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1146: FDA did not independently verify results for some exploratory endpoints including DCR, time to progression or progression-free survival.
L1147: As described above, there were observed general improvements in clinical outcome assessments however the same limitations apply: these results should be interpreted with caution given the small sample size, single arm design of the study, heterogeneous population in terms of age and tumor location, and compliance rate at cycle 13. Because of these limitations and to better assess the clinical meaningfulness of tumor shrinkage, FDA also reviewed individual patient narratives submitted by the Applicant for all responders, given the heterogeneity in target PN location and associated morbidities. On review of the individual narratives, the majority of responders had improvement in at least one symptom or clinical outcome measure. Key information from the responder narratives for patients in the pediatric and adult cohorts, including evidence of clinical benefit for each patient, in ReNeu is summarized in Table 43 and Table 44, respectively.

L1169: ## 8.1.2. Study NF-106
L1170: ## Study Design
L1171: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1172: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1173: Study NF-106 was an investigator-initiated, open-label, multi-center, Phase 2 study to evaluate safety and efficacy of mirdametinib in participants \ge 16 years old with NF1 and symptomatic or progressing PN. This study has been completed. The primary endpoint was confirmed ORR based on volumetric MRI analysis. Key secondary objectives included evaluation of QoL and pain, characterization of the single dose PK profile of mirdametinib in the NF1 population, and the evaluation of safety and tolerability of chronic mirdametinib administration. Mirdametinib was orally administered at a dose of 2 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 BID (maximum 4 mg BID) in 4-week cycles on an intermittent (3 weeks on/1 week off) schedule, for up to 24 cycles. Participants were required to have \ge 15\% reduction in target tumor volume at the end of Cycle 8 of therapy to continue to receive treatment up to Cycle 12. If a participant had \ge 20\% reduction in target tumor volume at the end of Cycle 12 of therapy, they could continue on study up to Cycle 24. Evaluations were performed every 4 cycles of treatment for the first year, followed by every 6 cycles thereafter.
L1174: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1175: The NF-106 study is a supportive study, and the efficacy results were not verified by FDA. The efficacy portion of the label for marketing application is solely based on the MEK-NF-201 study results.
L1176: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L1177: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L1178: Key inclusion criteria included:
L1179: - Participants must be \ge 16 years of age at the time of study entry.
- Participants must have EITHER the clinical diagnosis of NF1 using the NIH Consensus Conference criteria OR have a constitutional NF1 mutation documented in a CLIA/CAP certified lab.
- Participants must have a target PN that is progressive OR causing significant morbidity, such as (but not limited to) head and neck lesions that are compromising the airway or great vessels, brachial or lumbar plexus lesions that are causing nerve compression and loss of function, lesions causing major deformity or are significantly disfiguring, lesions of the extremity that cause limb hypertrophy or loss of function, and painful lesions.For subjects enrolled for tumor progression, progression is defined as presence of new plexiform neurofibroma on MRI or CT OR a measurable increase in plexiform neurofibroma size (\ge 20\% increase in the volume, or a \ge 13\% increase in the product of the two longest perpendicular diameters, or a \ge 6\% increase in the longest diameter) documented by comparison of two
L1180: scans (MRI or CT) in the time period of approximately one year or less prior to evaluation for this study.
L1181: - Participants must have a target PN this is deemed inoperable, defined as a PN that cannot be completely surgically removed without risk of substantial morbidity, or if the participant with surgical option refuses surgery.
- Participants must have measurable PN amenable to volumetric MRI analysis. For the purpose of this study, the target lesion must be seen on at least 3 consecutive MRI slices and the field of view must contain the entire tumor of interest.
- Participants must have had a Karnofsky performance level of \ge 50\%
L1182: Key exclusion criteria included:
L1183: - Active optic glioma or low-grade glioma requiring treatment.
- Subjects with glaucoma, intraocular pressure &gt;21 mmHg, or any other significant abnormality on ophthalmic examination (performed by an ophthalmologist).
- Chronic treatment with systemic steroids or another immunosuppressive agency.
- Prior treatment with a MEK inhibitor of any kind.
L1184: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1185: FDA agrees with the description of the inclusion and exclusion criteria for the NF-106 study.
L1186: ## **Study Endpoints**
L1187: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L1188: Primary Endpoint:
L1189: - The primary efficacy endpoint was confirmed ORR defined as the proportion of participants who have a BOR of PR or CR by the end of treatment with \ge 4 weeks duration.
L1190: Secondary Endpoints:
L1191: - Safety endpoints included TEAEs, serious adverse events (SAEs), adverse event of special interest (AESIs), changes from baseline in laboratory parameters, vital signs and retinal exam abnormalities
- Duration of response for participants whose best response was CR or PR
- Change in pain and quality of life scores from baseline as measured by NRS-11, BPI, and PedsQL NF1 module.
L1192: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1193: FDA acknowledges the Applicants description of study endpoints and notes that the primary endpoint of ORR differs from that of MEK-NF-201 in that patients were discontinued from treatment if they did not achieve at least a 15% or 20% reduction in target tumor volume at Cycle 8 and 12, respectively, precluding a full evaluation of ORR and DOR.
L1194: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L1195: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1196: There were 2 versions of the SAP that were submitted to the FDA. The initial SAP (v1.0) was approved on 18 May 2023. The final SAP (v2.0) was approved 03 December 2023.
L1197: The results of this study are presented overall and by pediatric and adult cohorts.
L1198: ## *Primary Efficacy Endpoint*
L1199: The primary efficacy endpoint was confirmed ORR defined as the proportion of participants who had a BOR of CR or PR by the end of Cycle 24 with \ge 4 weeks duration.
L1200: (b) (4)
L1201: The target PNs were centrally evaluated by MRI by a single reader at the
L1202: Level of target PN response was defined as follows:
L1203: - CR: A complete resolution of the target PN for \ge 4 weeks
- PR: A \ge 20\% reduction in the volume of the target PN lesion compared to the pretreatment volume for \ge 4 weeks
- Stable Disease (SD): Between a &lt; 20\% increase, and &lt; 20\% decrease in the volume of the target PN lesion compared to the pretreatment volume for \ge 4 weeks
- PD: A \ge 20\% increase in the volume of the target PN compared to the pretreatment volume
L1204: To assess the primary efficacy endpoint, the confirmed ORR was presented alongside the corresponding 2-sided 95% Clopper-Pearson CIs and the p-values from a one-sample two-sided binomial test evaluating the null hypothesis that the ORR = 5%. Best overall confirmed response was summarized according to the categories presented above. Tumor volume (mL), change from baseline in tumor volume, and percentage change from baseline in tumor volume were summarized. Additionally, the best observed percentage volumetric change from baseline was also summarized.
L1205: For calculation of the ORR, all participants with missing data who were not previously presented as confirmed PR or CR were imputed as non-responders for the primary endpoint
L1206: analysis. Any participants who terminated study treatment early without a confirmed PR or CR were also imputed as non-responders.
L1207: ## *Secondary Efficacy Endpoints*
L1208: ## *Duration of Response*
L1209: A secondary efficacy objective was to determine the DoR for participants who attained a best overall confirmed response of CR or PR using MRI volumetric analysis. The DoR was defined as the time (in months) between the first instance of response that was subsequently confirmed, until the date of radiographic disease progression or death, whichever occurred first.
L1210: In the absence of radiographic progression or death, participants had their results censored to the date of their most adequate tumor assessment date prior to the start of any surgery to the target PN or the start of any anti-cancer/PN medication (e.g., chemotherapy, immunology, biologic therapy, or radiation therapy).
L1211: DoR was analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier method based on participants with a confirmed CR or PR. Additionally, the KM estimates with corresponding 95% CIs (using the log-log transformation) and number of participants at risk will be estimated at the following time points: 2, 4, 8, 12, 18, and 24 months.
L1212: Time to response was summarized descriptively for confirmed responders and focused on the evaluation of outcomes within each treatment arm.
L1213: ## *Patient Reported Outcomes*
L1214: Absolute values and changes from baseline in NRS-11 scores were summarized. Summaries by ORR status (i.e., participants with confirmed objective response herein denoted as 'responders' versus participants who did not achieve confirmed objective response or 'non-responders') were provided.
L1215: Additionally, a MMRM was fit for overall and adult participants only with change from baseline in NRS-11 score as the dependent variable, visit as a fixed effect and baseline NRS-11 score as a covariate. Covariance structures were trialed in the order of decreasing complexity, with the first structure which converges without error used. The structures tested were as follows: Unstructured, Heterogenous Toeplitz, Heterogenous Autoregressive, Autoregressive, Heterogenous Compound Symmetry, Compound Symmetry, Variance Components.
L1216: Absolute values and changes from baseline in BPI-Pain Interference Subscale scores were summarized descriptively by visit for the corresponding domains and total scores. Summaries by ORR status (responder versus non-responder) were also presented.
L1217: Absolute values and changes from baseline in PedsQL NF1 Total Scale scores were summarized descriptively by visit for the corresponding domains and total scores. Summaries by ORR status (responder versus non-responder) were also presented.
L1218: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1219: FDA acknowledges the Applicant's description of the statistical analysis plan.

Language was added to require MRI review during screening to confirm the lesion is measurable and amenable to volumetric analysis
Text was added to summarize/clarify biopsy collection procedure

Additional endpoints were recategorized from Secondary Objectives to Exploratory Objectives. L1229: ## 8.1.2.1. Study Results
L1230: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L1231: ## **Data:**
L1232: This study was designed and monitored in accordance with sponsor procedures, which comply with the ethical principles of GCP as required by the major regulatory authorities.
L1233: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1234: Study NF-106 is GCP compliant.
L1235: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1236: FDA acknowledges the Applicant's statement of compliance with GCP requirements.
L1237: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L1238: ## **Data:**
L1239: The study investigator and clinical coordinator reviewed the study progress regularly. Participants entered on the study and AEs were reviewed to ensure that the study was conducted as outlined in the protocol. Monthly reports were generated by the NF Consortium to assess completeness of data. There were monthly phone conferences between the NF Consortium and the investigator to address quality assurance issues. A Data Safety Monitoring Board was established for the purpose of ensuring data compliance and regular monitoring of the study.
L1240: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1241: No issues were identified that could potentially impact data integrity, prevent an adequate assessment of the data, or change the conclusions drawn.
L1242: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1243: FDA did not identify quality or integrity issues in the data. The data submitted were organized and adequate to perform a complete review of mirdametinib.

All investigators were assessed for equity interest, significant payments of other sorts, and other compensation by the sponsor or proprietary interest. The Applicant certified that none of the financial interests or arrangements described in 21 CFR Part 54 existed for any of the investigators who participated in Study NF-106. The Applicant provided a list of all investigators for Study NF-106 in Appendix 16.1.4 of the CSR.
L1250: FDA reviewed the financial disclosure information provided by the Applicant and agrees that the integrity of the study data was not affected by the financial interest of the investigators.

L1261: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1262: Most participants prematurely discontinued treatment: the most common reasons for treatment discontinuation were &lt;15% target tumor volume reduction at the end of Cycle 8, refusal of further protocol therapy, and non-compliance.
L1263: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1264: FDA acknowledges the Applicant's position noting that one third of patients discontinued treatment as a result of not meeting target tumor reduction thresholds by cycles 8 and 12. The results of this trial are considered supportive, but the efficacy review of mirdametinib did not rely on these results and they were not independently verified during the review of this application.
L1265: ## **Protocol Violations/Deviations**
L1266: ## **Data:**
L1267: A total of 17 (90%) participants had at least 1 protocol deviation, with the majority (79%) of deviations occurring in the protocol assessment/procedure category. One participant had a deviation that resulted in an AE or an AE and impacted participant safety, which was 'noncompliance with contraception, fathered a pregnancy'. The majority of recorded deviations describe missed doses within a given cycle, and sporadically missed vitals, cytokine blood draws, and PRO assessments. No deviations were determined to affect the scientific integrity of study.
L1268: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1269: The deviations were reviewed and GCP compliance was maintained. None of the observed protocol deviations impacted the primary objective of the study and did not affect the overall safety and efficacy outcomes of the study.
L1270: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1271: FDA agrees with the description of one patient that resulted in protocol deviation with a partner pregnancy reported on Day 330 with a resolution of Day 606.
L1272: ## **Table of Demographic Characteristics**
L1273: ## **Data:**
L1274: The median (min, max) age at the time of consent was 24 (16, 39) years. Most participants were female (63%) and approximately half were White (42%). The most common target PN locations (&gt;20%) were head and neck (5 participants [26%]) and mesentery and pelvis (4 participants [21%]).
L1275: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1276: Demographic characteristics were consistent with the general population with NF1 PN.
L1277: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1278: FDA acknowledges the Applicant's description of demographic characteristics. Demographics for this population are characterized as part of the pooled safety population (see Section 8.2).

L1281: The most common target PN locations (&gt;20%) were head and neck (5 participants [26%]) and mesentery and pelvis (4 participants [21%]).

L1300: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1301: Mirdametinib was tolerated by the majority of participants, as only 1 participant discontinued treatment due to toxicity.
L1302: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1303: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of treatment compliance and concomitant medication use.
L1304: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)**
L1305: ## **Data:**
L1306: There was a total of 19 participants dosed with mirdametinib. Confirmed ORR was defined as the proportion of participants who have a BOR at the end of the Treatment Phase of PR or CR for ≥4 weeks duration. The ORR for the total population was 31.6% (95% CI: 12.58, 56.55; p&lt;0.001); 6 participants had a best overall confirmed response of PR. The remaining 13 participants had SD as their best overall confirmed response.
L1307: The median (min, max) best percentage change from baseline in tumor volume was -17.14% (-28.0, 17.9).
L1308: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1309: Mirdametinib was effective as demonstrated by the confirmed ORR of 32%. No participants had a best objective response of PD. Of note, participants discontinued treatment unless the participant had at least a 15% or 20% reduction in volume of the target tumor at Cycle 8 and 12, respectively. Due to these required efficacy benchmarks that caused participants to discontinue treatment, some participants were withdrawn from study treatment that may have achieved an objective response with longer duration of administration.
L1310: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1311: FDA acknowledges the Applicant's position noting that one third of patients discontinued treatment as a result of not meeting target tumor reduction thresholds by cycles 8 and 12. The results of this trial are considered supportive, but the efficacy review of mirdametinib did not rely on these results and they were not independently verified during the review of this application.
L1312: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L1313: ## **Data:**
L1314: Of the 6 (32%) confirmed responders, the median (95% CI) DoR was not reached because all 6 responders were censored due to no PDs/deaths following a confirmed response. Of these 6 responders, 5 participants maintained their response for \ge 8 months. The onset of response was at Cycle 8 for 1 participant and at Cycle 12 for 5 participants; 4 of these participants had an onset of response at Cycle 12 and maintained this response through Cycle 24, at which point they were censored. The 2 remaining responders discontinued treatment early due to non-compliance and refusal of further therapy.
L1315: The median time to onset of response for all participants was 10.92 (7.16, 11.66) months.
L1316: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1317: The DoR suggest that treatment with mirdametinib at 2 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 BID results in long-term durability. Participants were discontinued from treatment unless they had at least a 15% or 20% reduction in volume of the target tumor at Cycle 8 and 12, respectively. Due to these required efficacy benchmarks that caused participants to be discontinued from treatment, some participants were withdrawn from study treatment that may have achieved an objective response with continued administration.
L1318: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1319: FDA acknowledges the Applicant's position but did not rely on or verify these results.
L1320: ## **Dose/Dose Response**
L1321: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1322: Mirdametinib was dosed in Studies MEK-NF-201 and NF-106 by BSA. The median (minimum, maximum) of actual doses received by participants with NF1 PN was 2.21 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 (0.806, 2.86) (Figure 4).
L1323: In addition, population and trial simulations were conducted using exposure-efficacy and exposure-safety models to predict the impact of alternative target dose levels from 1.0 to 3.0 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2 BID (1.0, 1.5, 2.0, and 3.0 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2). The simulations support that exposures associated with the proposed marketing dose of 2 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 of mirdametinib provide an optimal balance of
L1324: robust efficacy with a tolerable incidence of low severity exposure-related AEs for patients with NF1 PN. Please refer to Section 6.2.2.1 for additional simulation details in support of the proposed 2mg/m2 dose used in Study MEK-NF-201, Figure 4.
L1325: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1326: Refer to Section 6, 19.4.2, and 19.4.3 for clinical pharmacology review details.
L1327: ## **Durability of Response**
L1328: ## Data:
L1329: Median DoR for participants with a confirmed response was not reached.
L1330: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1331: Response was durable among participants with a confirmed response.
L1332: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1333: Among the 19 patients in NF-106, only 6 (32%) had a confirmed response of PR. The small number of patients, lack of events and follow-up limit the ability to interpret DOR and durability of response.
L1334: ## **Persistence of Effect**
L1335: ## Data:
L1336: Refer to efficacy results – secondary and other relevant endpoints above.
L1337: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1338: The study demonstrated persistence of effect over time.
L1339: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1340: Persistence of effect cannot be verified.
L1341: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints**
L1342: ## Data:
L1343: Of 19 participants, 9 completed the NRS-11 at Cycle 12 and 4 participants completed the NRS-11 at Cycle 24. The median (min, max) change in score from baseline at Cycle 12 for the FAS was -1 (-9, 0). At Cycle 24, the median (min, max) change from baseline was 0 (-3, 1). There was statistically significant improvement in the mean NRS-11 change from baseline at Cycle 12 (LS mean=-1.81; p=0.030), indicating decreased pain severity. At Cycle 24, the LS mean change from baseline was -0.30 (p=0.740).
L1344: At Cycle 12, 9 participants completed the BPI. There were improvements found in the BPI with a median (min, max) change from baseline total score of -0.86 (-4.57, 0.86), indicating decreased pain interference.
L1345: At Cycle 24, 4 participants completed the BPI. There were improvements found in the BPI with a median (min, max) change from baseline total score of -1.21 (-5.86, 2), indicating decreased pain interference.
L1346: At Cycle 12, 9 participants completed the PedsQL NF1 module. The median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 12 in PedsQL NF1 module total score was 3.6 (-20, 29), indicating an improvement in NF1-related QoL. All 6 responders completed Cycle 12.
L1347: At Cycle 24, 4 participants completed the PedsQL NF1 module. The median (min, max) change from baseline at Cycle 24 in PedsQL NF1 total score was 15.6 (-8, 34), indicating an improvement in NF1-related QoL.
L1348: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1349: Secondary PRO endpoints showed meaningful improvements in QoL and pain:
L1350: - At Cycle 12 there was statistically significant improvement in the mean NRS-11 change from baseline, indicating decreased pain severity.
- At Cycle 12 and Cycle 24, improvement in pain interference score was observed in the median BPI change from baseline, indicating decreased pain interference.
- At Cycle 12 and Cycle 24, improvement in the PedsQL NF1 module total score, indicating improved QoL, was observed in the median (min, max) change from baseline.
L1351: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1352: As stated above under "Trial Design" FDA did not independently analyze the results of Study NF-106. Based on the Applicant's presentation of this data, the PRO data is not interpretable given the sample size of 19 patients in this study and only 9 patients responding to the BPI and PedsQL at cycle 12.
L1353: ## **Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial**
L1354: ## Data:
L1355: Analysis of secondary PK endpoints showed the following:
L1356: - Mirdametinib was absorbed rapidly, with a median Tmax of 1.0 hour. Following attainment of Cmax, the disposition of mirdametinib exhibited biphasic decline with a median Tlast of 9.98 hours.
- Mirdametinib exposures as measured by geometric mean (geometric CV%) AUClast, AUC0-8, and Cmax were 387.1 ng·h/mL (24.18%), 368.1 ng·h/mL (25.59%), and 188.3 ng/mL (50.46%), respectively.
L1357: ## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1358: - Formation of PD-0315209 was rapid to moderate with median (min, max) Tmax values of 2.00 hours (1.00, 6.00). Median Tlast was 9.98 hours. Following attainment of Cmax, disposition of PD-0315209 appeared to be 1.
- PD-0315209 exposures as measured by geometric mean (geometric mean CV%) AUClast, AUC0-8, and Cmax were 117.3 ng·h/mL (102.98%), 103.4 ng·h/mL (107.55%) and 18.0 ng/mL (110.34%).
L1359: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1360: Analysis was done on first dose pharmacokinetics. These data are in alignment in the adult cohort of MEK-NF-201.
L1361: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1362: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1363: ## 8.1.3. **Integrated Review of Effectiveness**
L1364: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1365: The ReNeu study is a single arm, multi-center study which served as the primary basis for establishing the efficacy of mirdametinib in the treatment of patients with NF1 PN. Study NF-106, an open-label, single arm study that included 2 pediatric and 17 adult patients, provided supportive evidence of mirdametinib's efficacy.
L1366: All 114 patients enrolled in the ReNeu study had measurable disease at baseline as assessed by BICR. The analysis of tumor response included 56 patients in the pediatric cohort and 58 patients in the adult cohort. In the pediatric cohort, the confirmed ORR was 52% (95% CI: 38, 65) and in the adult cohort, confirmed ORR was 41% (95% CI: 29, 55). All the responses observed in the study were partial responses. The ORR subgroups analysis based on demographic and clinical characteristics were mostly consistent across subgroups in both cohorts.
L1367: As of data cutoff date of June 12, 2024 (90-day safety update) the median DOR was not reached in either cohort. The percentage of patients with response who have observed DOR \ge 12 months and \ge 24 months were 90% and 48% in the pediatric cohort and 88% and 50% in the adult cohort, respectively.
L1368: The median time to onset of response was 7.8 months (range: 4.0 months to 19.0 months) for the adult cohort and 7.9 months (range: 4.1 months to 18.8 months) for the pediatric cohort.
L1369: FDA considered the comparisons of efficacy endpoints in the ReNeu study to the placebo control arm of Study 01-C-0222 and the natural history Study 08-C-0079 to be exploratory, due
L1370: to potential between-study differences including patient eligibility criteria, assessment frequencies of endpoint, and endpoint definition.
L1371: ## 8.1.4. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L1372: ## Primary Endpoints
L1373: ## **Data:**
L1374: Study MEK-NF-201 serves as the primary basis for establishing the efficacy of mirdametinib in the treatment of participants with NF1 PN. In addition, data from Study NF-106 are provided as supportive evidence of efficacy. Data from MEK-NF-201 and NF-106 were not integrated. The rationale for this strategy is provided below.
L1375: Both studies evaluated the efficacy of mirdametinib at a dose of 2 mg/m2 BID on an intermittent schedule (3 weeks on/1 week off in a 28-day cycle) in participants with NF1 PN, but differences in the study designs do not allow for an integration of results of the two studies. Specifically, participants in Study NF-106 were discontinued from treatment if they did not meet certain tumor volume reduction benchmarks: treatment beyond Cycle 8 was allowed only for participants who had \ge 15\% reduction from baseline in volume of the target tumor by the end of Cycle 8 and treatment beyond 12 cycles was allowed for participants who achieved \ge 20\% tumor reduction from baseline by volumetric analysis by the end of Cycle 12. In contrast, there were no requirements for volumetric reduction of the target PN at any cycle to continue receiving mirdametinib in Study MEK-NF-201; participants received study treatment unless they met withdrawal criteria.
L1376: TTR results from Study MEK-NF-201 demonstrate that the rule excluding further treatment beyond Cycle 8 in Study NF-106 likely precluded many participants from an opportunity to achieve an objective response with longer durations of mirdametinib administration. Similarly, time to best percentage change in tumor volume results in Study MEK-NF-201 suggest that participants in Study NF-106 who did not achieve the tumor reduction requirements at Cycles 8 and 12 were discontinued from treatment before their tumors could likely reach their greatest reduction in volume, thereby affecting the likelihood of achieving a response. Therefore, efficacy results from these two studies were presented separately with Study NF-106 included as a supportive study of the results in the pivotal Study MEK-NF-201.
L1377: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1378: Efficacy Data from Study MEK-NF-201 and NF-106 were not integrated as agreed upon with the Agency.
L1379: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1380: FDA agrees with the description above that efficacy data from the ReNeu study and NF-106 were not integrated. FDA's assessment of efficacy is primarily based on the ReNeu study results. Refer to Section 8.1.3.

L1405: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1406: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1407: ## **Data:**
L1408: See the Applicant's Position below.
L1409: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1410: The pivotal safety data supporting the safety evaluation of mirdametinib is based on Study MEK-NF-201. The studies used to support the integrated summary of safety are described in Table 26. Data from 8 additional non pooled interventional studies and the compassionate use program were used as supportive safety information, for a total of 577 patients exposed to mirdametinib.
L1411: As agreed with the agency during the Pre-NDA meeting, the integrated safety data presents 4 clinical studies that were grouped into the following study pools based on the targeted underlying condition (NF1 PN or advanced cancers), dosing regimen (body surface area vs unit dosing), participants, and study design:
L1412: - Pool 1: Study MEK-NF-201 and Study NF-106 in adult and pediatric participants with NF1 PN. The data from these studies were analyzed together as a single pool and are the primary safety data used to assess the safety profile of mirdametinib in adult and pediatric participants with NF1 PN.
- Pool 2: Study A4581001 and Study A4581002 in adult participants with advanced cancers. The data from these studies were analyzed together as a single pool and are supportive safety data used to assess the safety profile of mirdametinib.
L1413: This Assessment Aid will provide the safety data from Study MEK-NF-201, followed by Pool 1 and Pool 2, and supported by relevant data from healthy volunteers and the Global Pharmacovigilance database. Data will be compared to the MEK-NF-201 data as it serves as the primary basis for establishing the efficacy of mirdametinib in the treatment of participants with NF1 PN.
L1414: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1415: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the pivotal and supportive datasets included in the NDA to support the safety evaluation of mirdametinib. FDA independently reviewed exposure and safety data for the pivotal ReNeu study and supportive Study NF-106 in which safety data was analyzed together as Pool 1 to support the safety profile for adult and pediatric patients with NF1 PN treated with mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID (maximum 4 mg BID); 3 weeks on/1 week off.
L1416: A 90-day updated safety analysis with corresponding datasets for Pool 1 (data cut-off date of September 19, 2024) was submitted to supplement the summary of clinical safety. The 90-day safety update included an additional 9 months of safety data. There were no new patients enrolled into Pool 1 since the initial data cut (Primary Analysis). Study NF-106 was completed prior to the primary analysis and there was no additional data in the 90-day safety update. The 90-day safety update did not identify new safety signals not already discussed in this review.
L1417: FDA reviewed but did not independently verify the data from Pool 2 which included Study A4581001 and Study A4581002 in adult patients with advanced cancers.
L1418: ## 8.2.2. Review of the Safety Database
L1419: ## Overall Exposure
L1420: ## **Data:**
L1421: A total of 577 patients have received at least one dose of mirdametinib across the clinical program.
L1422: A total of 56 participants in the pediatric cohort of Study MEK-NF-201 received at least 1 dose of study treatment. The median (min, max) duration of study treatment exposure was 22.0 (1.6, 40.0) months. A total of 58 participants in the adult cohort received at least 1 dose of study treatment. The median (min, max) duration of study treatment exposure was 21.8 (0.4, 45.6) months.
L1423: The exposure to mirdametinib in Pool 1 aligns with what was observed in the pivotal study, MEK-NF-201. In Pool 1, a total of 133 participants (58 participants in the pediatric cohort and 75 participants in the adult cohort) received at least one dose of study treatment. As displayed in Table 48, the median (min, max) duration of mirdametinib exposure for the pediatric cohort was 21.87 (1.6, 40.1) months and 18.66 (0.4, 45.6) months in the adult cohort of Pool 1. The median duration of study treatment, median duration to dose reduction and interruption were similar between the pediatric and adult cohorts. The median time to dose modification was slightly longer in the pediatric cohort at 4.2 (range: 0 to 21.6) months compared to the adult cohort at 1.4 (range: 0 to 45) months. In Pool 1, 62% of all participants were on study treatment for 1 year or longer, 38% at 2 years and 12% at 3 years, suggesting that the safety profile of mirdametinib is acceptable over longer term use.
L1424: The median duration of exposure in Pool 2 in participants treated with mirdametinib was much lower than what was observed in the adult cohort of Pool 1. Note that Pool 2 participants had advanced cancers and all Pool 2 studies were terminated early.

L1457: ## **Adequacy of the safety database:**
L1458: ## **Data:**
L1459: Refer to Table 15 for the number of participants enrolled in each study used to support the evaluation of safety for mirdametinib.
L1460: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1461: The evaluation of safety of mirdametinib for the treatment of participants with NF1 PN is based on data from Study MEK-NF-201 and Pool 1 (Studies MEK-NF-201 and NF-106), which comprises both adult and pediatric cohorts (N=133).
L1462: Supportive safety information for mirdametinib as a monotherapy was generated by pooling two studies conducted in adult patients with advanced cancer (A4581001 and A4581002) (N=113). Due to the differences in dosing and patient populations these were pooled separately from the studies in participants with NF1 PN (Pool 2).
L1463: Additional supportive safety data from 3 Phase 1 studies in which healthy volunteers received single doses of mirdametinib (88 patients received mirdametinib in these three studies) as well as data from other interventional studies and compassionate use programs were used to support the safety of mirdametinib. A total of 577 patients have received at least one dose of mirdametinib which is considered adequate to define the risks of mirdametinib treatment in this rare disease.

If there were multiple unscheduled assessments, the assessment closest to the target day was used.

L1510: ## **8.2.4. Safety Results**
L1511: ## **Deaths**
L1512: ## **Data:**
L1513: In Study MEK-NF-201, there were no deaths in the pediatric cohort and there was 1 death on study in the adult cohort due to COVID-19 which was considered by the investigator to be unrelated to study treatment. No additional deaths were reported in Pool 1.
L1514: In Pool 2, with the exception of 1 death in the \le 2 mg BID dose group, the majority (11/12) of deaths were observed in the higher dose groups and none were observed at the 4 mg BID dose group. The TEAEs resulting in deaths included death (7%), hypoxia (1%), respiratory arrest (1%), respiratory failure (1%), cardiac arrest (1%), myocardial infarction (1%), colon cancer metastatic (1%), metastases to the CNS (1%), and cerebrovascular accident (1%). No deaths were attributed to mirdametinib by the investigator.
L1515: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1516: Mirdametinib was not considered a cause of death for any reported event. The increase in number of deaths in Pool 2 compared to Pool 1 was driven by the older population of participants in these early studies who had advanced cancers, including metastatic malignant melanoma and non-small cell lung cancer.
L1517: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1518: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's analysis of 1 death in Pool 1 (adult cohort) due to COVID 19 and not related to study treatment. This patient was a 68-year-old male with NF1 PN who received their first dose of mirdametinib 2 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 at 4 mg twice daily on (b)(6). On Day 70 the patient had Grade 2 ejection fraction decreased that led to a dose interruption from Day 71
L1519: to Day 83. On Day 84 the patient resumed treatment at a reduced dose of 3 mg BID and continued this dose until Day 324. On Day 349 the patient had a fatal serious adverse event of Covid-19. There were no additional deaths reported for Pool 1. This death is described in Section 6 of the USPI.
L1520: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1521: ## **Data:**
L1522: In the pediatric cohort of Study MEK-NF-201, SAEs were reported in 8 (14%) participants. No SAEs were reported by &gt;1 participant.
L1523: In the adult cohort of Study MEK-NF-201, SAEs were reported in 10 (17%) participants. SAEs reported by &gt;1 participant were COVID-19, abdominal pain, and nephrolithiasis in 2 (3%) participants each. None of these events was assessed as related to mirdametinib.
L1524: Overall, the findings regarding SAEs in Pool 1 align with those described in Study MEK-NF-201.
L1525: See Table 51 for SAEs that occurred in Pool 1.

L1537: A total of 4 SAEs were reported in participants who received mirdametinib ≤4 mg BID in Pool 2. No SAEs were reported by more than one participant in this dose range. The SAEs reported included pyrexia, urinary tract infection, bronchitis, and cardiac failure congestive.
L1538: An increased incidence of SAEs can be seen with higher doses of mirdametinib in Pool 2, with 57% of participants in the 8, 10 mg BID dose group and 51% of participants in the 15, 20, 30 mg BID group reporting SAEs. The PT of death was reported most in these dose groups, consistent with the advanced cancer diagnoses and extensive prior treatment in a Phase 1 population in this pool. As described in the deaths section, none of these deaths were considered related to mirdametinib by the investigator.
L1539: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1540: For the adult cohort of Pool 1, the most frequently reported SAEs (\ge 2 participants) were COVID-19, abdominal pain, and nephrolithiasis. In the pediatric cohort, all SAE PTs were reported by 1 participant each.
L1541: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1542: FDA performed an independent analysis of the incidence of SAEs in patients included in Pool 1 (e.g., patients from the ReNeu study and Study NF-106) who received mirdametinib at 2 \text{ mg/m}^2 twice daily (maximum dose of 4 mg BID) for the first 21 days of each 28-day cycle. SAEs occurred in 20 patients (15%) with the most frequently reported SAEs \ge 1\% being abdominal pain, nephrolithiasis, musculoskeletal pain and Covid-19.
L1543: FDA's analysis of the ReNeu study and the incidences of SAEs in the adult and pediatric patient population were used to support Section 6 of the USPI. SAEs occurred in 17% of adult patients who received mirdametinib. SAEs occurring in \ge 2\% of patients were COVID-19 (3.4%) and nephrolithiasis (3.4%), and in 1 patient each: acute kidney injury, abdominal pain, ischemic colitis, urinary tract infection, retinal vein occlusion (RVO), scoliosis, squamous cell carcinoma of skin, cerebrovascular accident, and chronic obstructive pulmonary disease. SAEs occurred in 14% of pediatric patients who received mirdametinib. SAEs in \ge 2\% of pediatric patients included viral gastrointestinal infections (3.6%), and in 1 patient each: diplopia, musculoskeletal pain, seizure, fall, femoral neck fracture, dehydration, and hypertension. Refer to Table 52 for FDA's analysis of SAEs in the ReNeu Study.

L1554: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1555: ## Data:
L1556: In the pediatric cohort of Study MEK-NF-201, 5 (9%) participants reported TEAEs that led to treatment discontinuation. All TEAEs that led to treatment discontinuation were Grade 1 or Grade 2. The only TEAE that led to treatment discontinuation in &gt;1 participant in the pediatric cohort was urticaria. Note that individual participants could have reported more than 1 event leading to treatment discontinuation.
L1557: In the adult cohort of Study MEK-NF-201, 13 (22%) participants reported TEAEs that led to treatment discontinuation. TEAEs that led to treatment discontinuation in &gt;1 participant in the adult cohort were dermatitis acneiform, diarrhea, and nausea. Most TEAEs that led to treatment discontinuation were Grade 1 or Grade 2; 3 TEAEs were Grade 3 (dermatitis acneiform, rash erythematous, and RVO, no TEAEs were Grade 4, and only 1 TEAE was Grade 5 (COVID-19).
L1558: Overall, the findings regarding TEAEs that led to treatment discontinuation in Pool 1 align with those described in Study MEK-NF-201. TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation in Pool 1 are shown in Table 53. In Pool 1, 5 (9%) pediatric participants and 16 (21%) adult participants reported TEAEs that led to treatment discontinuation. In the pediatric cohort, the only TEAE that led to treatment discontinuation in &gt;1 participant was urticaria which resulted in treatment discontinuation in 2 (3%) pediatric participants. This was the same as what was observed in Study MEK-NF-201 in the pediatric cohort. In the adult cohort, TEAEs that led to discontinuation in &gt;1 participant were dermatitis acneiform [6 (8%) participants], diarrhea and nausea [4 (5%) participants each], and vomiting [2 (3%) participants]; these were the same TEAEs that led to discontinuation in &gt;1 participant in Study MEK-NF-201, with the addition of vomiting.

L1566: In Pool 2, 4/16 participants (25%) who received mirdametinib \le4 mg BID discontinued study treatment due to TEAEs. The PTs reported included optic neuropathy and retinal hemorrhage (1 participant), vomiting and pyrexia (1 participant), acute kidney injury (1 participant), and metastases to central nervous system and death (1 participant).
L1567: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1568: Many TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation were consistent with the known safety profile of mirdametinib and MEK inhibitors.
L1569: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1570: In FDA's analysis of Pool 1, adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation occurred in 21% of adult patients and in 9% of pediatric patients. Adverse events which resulted in treatment discontinuation of mirdametinib in the adult cohort in \ge2% of patients included rash (11%), diarrhea (5%), nausea (5%) and vomiting (2.7%). Adverse events which led to treatment discontinuation of mirdametinib in the pediatric cohort in \ge2% of patients included urticaria (3.4%) and rash (3.4%).
L1571: FDA's analysis of the ReNeu study showed that adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation occurred in 22% of adult patients and 9% of pediatric patients. Adverse events which led to treatment discontinuation of mirdametinib in \ge1% of adult patients included rash, diarrhea, nausea, abdominal pain, alopecia, dry skin, left ventricular dysfunction, cough, wheezing, COVID-19, peripheral swelling, RVO, dizziness and vomiting. See Section 8.2.5.1 for a summary of the RVO event.
L1572: Adverse events which led to treatment discontinuation of mirdametinib in \ge1% of pediatric patients included rash, urticaria, abdominal pain, constipation and diarrhea.
L1573: FDA agrees that the adverse events leading to treatment discontinuations of mirdametinib in the ReNeu study were similar to those observed in Pool 1.
L1574: ## **Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects**
L1575: ## **Data:**
L1576: In the pediatric cohort of Study MEK-NF-201, 39 (70%) participants had at least 1 dose modification, including dose interruptions and/or reductions; other than due to changes in BSA, most dose modifications were due to an AE (32%). TEAEs leading to a dose interruption were reported by 17 (30%) participants; the most frequently reported (&gt; 5%) TEAEs leading to dose interruption were SARS-CoV-2 test positive and COVID-19 (3 [5%] participants each). TEAEs leading to a dose reduction were reported by 7 (13%) participants; the most frequently reported (&gt; 5%) TEAE leading to dose reduction was neutrophil count decreased (3 [5%] participants).
L1577: In the adult cohort of Study MEK-NF-201, 31 (53%) participants had at least 1 dose modification, including dose interruptions and/or reductions, with most of these being due to an AE (40%). TEAEs leading to a dose interruption were reported by 18 (31%) participants; the TEAEs that most frequently (&gt; 5%) led to dose interruption were ejection fraction decreased (5 [9%] participants) and COVID-19 (3 [5%] participants). TEAEs leading to a dose reduction were reported by 10 (17%) participants; the TEAEs that most frequently (&gt; 5%) led to dose reduction was dermatitis acneiform (6 [10%] participants).
L1578: In Pool 1, 50 participants (38%; 20 [35%] in the pediatric cohort, 30 [40%] in the adult cohort) had dose modifications due to TEAEs. TEAEs that led to dose modification of study treatment in &gt;1 participant in the pediatric cohort were COVID-19 (3 participants), neutrophil count decreased (3 participants), SARS CoV-2 test positive (3 participants), blood creatinine phosphokinase increased (2 participants), and weight increased (2 participants). TEAEs that led to dose modification of study treatment in &gt;1 participant in the adult cohort were dermatitis acneiform (8 participants), ejection fraction decreased (6 participants), COVID-19 (3 participants), and fatigue (2 participants).
L1579: In Pool 1, 38 participants (29%; 17 [29%] in the pediatric cohort, 21 [28%] in the adult cohort) had dose interruptions due to TEAEs. TEAEs that led to dose interruptions of study treatment in &gt;1 participant in the pediatric cohort were SARS CoV-2 test positive (3 participants), neutrophil count decreased (2 participants), and COVID-19 (3 participants). The median time to dose interruption (min, max) was 7.1 months (0.3, 26.8 months). Ejection fraction decreased (5 participants), COVID-19 (3 participants), and dermatitis acneiform (2 participants) were the only TEAEs that led to dose interruptions of study treatment in &gt;1 participant in the adult cohort. The median time to dose interruption (min, max) was 5.65 months (0.1, 44.7 months).
L1580: Pool 2 had a similar incidence of TEAEs that led to dose interruption as compared to the adult cohort in Pool 1. A total of 5 participants reported TEAEs that led to dose interruption in participants who received mirdametinib ≤4 mg BID in Pool 2. All TEAEs were reported by 1 participant each and included: chills, diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, confusional state, liver function test increased, and cardiac failure congestive. The median duration of exposure in Pool 2 in participants treated with mirdametinib ≤4 mg BID was much lower than what was observed
L1581: in the adult cohort of Pool 1. Pool 2 participants treated at dose levels below the RP2D did not have dose reduction as a pre-specified option.
L1582: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1583: The incidence of AEs leading to dose modification (interruption and/or reduction) was similar in Study MEK-NF-201, Pool 1, and Pool 2. The most common AEs leading to dose modification of mirdametinib in patients with NF1 PN were consistent with the known safety profile of mirdametinib or were commonly reported during the global pandemic.
L1584: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1585: In FDA's analysis of Pool 1, dose reductions due to an adverse event occurred in 15 (20%) adult patients, and in 7 (12%) of pediatric patients. Adverse events in \ge 3\% of patients which required dose reduction in the adult population included rash (11%) and in the pediatric population included decreased neutrophil count (5%) and rash (3.4%). Dose interruptions occurred in 21 (28%) of adult patients and 17 (29%) of pediatric patients. The most frequently reported adverse events leading to dose interruption in the adult population in \ge 3\% of patients were ejection fraction decreased (7%) and COVID-19 (4%). For the pediatric population, the most frequently reported adverse event leading to dose interruption was Covid-19 (5%).
L1586: In the ReNeu study dosage reduction of mirdametinib due to an adverse event occurred in 17% of adults and in 13% of pediatric patients. Adverse events which required dose reduction in \ge 5\% of adult patients included rash (10%). Adverse events which required dose reduction in \ge 3\% of pediatric patients were decreased neutrophil count (5%) and rash (3.6%). Dose interruptions occurred in 31% of adults and in 30% of pediatric patients. Adverse events in \ge 5\% of adult patients that required dosage interruption included left ventricular dysfunction (9%) and COVID-19 (5%). For pediatric patients, the most frequently reported adverse event leading to dose interruption in \ge 5\% of patients was COVID-19 (5%).
L1587: FDA agrees that the adverse events leading to dose reductions and dose interruptions of mirdametinib in the ReNeu study were similar to those observed in Pool 1.
L1588: ## **Significant Adverse Events**
L1589: ## Data:
L1590: In the pediatric cohort of Study MEK-NF-201, 43 Grade \ge 3 events were reported by 22 (39%) participants. Grade \ge 3 TEAEs reported by &gt; 1 participant were neutrophil count decreased, blood creatine phosphokinase (CPK) increased, diarrhea, weight increased, abdominal pain, and syncope. There were 2 participants who reported maximum Grade 4 events (1 participant with blood CPK increased and 1 participant with near drowning and laryngeal edema).
L1591: In the adult cohort of Study MEK-NF-201, 42 Grade \ge 3 events were reported by 21 (36%) participants. Grade \ge 3 TEAEs reported by &gt; 1 participant were SARS-COV-2 test positive, dermatitis acneiform, abdominal pain, COVID-19, back pain, and nephrolithiasis. There were 2 participants who reported maximum Grade 4 events (COPD and blood creatine phosphokinase increased) and 1 additional participant experienced a Grade 4 event of pneumonia bacterial secondary to a Grade 5 event of COVID-19.
L1592: Grade \ge 3 TEAEs reported in Pool 1 by \ge 2 pediatric participants were neutrophil count decreased, blood creatine phosphokinase increased, diarrhea, syncope, weight increased, and abdominal pain. Grade \ge 3 TEAEs reported in Pool 1 by \ge 2 adult participants were dermatitis acneiform, abdominal pain, back pain, COVID-19, nephrolithiasis and SARS-CoV-2 test positive.
L1593: Grade \ge 3 TEAEs reported in \ge 2 participants in either cohort in Pool 1 are displayed in Table 54.

L1598: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1599: The most common Grade ≥3 TEAEs reported are consistent with the known safety profile of mirdametinib or MEK inhibition.
L1600: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1601: Significant adverse events ≥ Grade 3 in severity in Pool 1 occurred in 26 (35%) adult patients and 23 (40%) pediatric patients. The most common ≥ Grade 3 adverse events occurring in ≥3% of patients in the adult population included rash (8%), musculoskeletal pain (7%) and abdominal pain (4%). The most common ≥ Grade 3 adverse events occurring in ≥3% of patients in the pediatric population included syncope (5%), rash (3.4%), increased weight (3.4%) and abdominal pain (3.4%).
L1602: ## **Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions**
L1603: ## Data:
L1604: In the pediatric cohort of Study MEK-NF-201, 56 (100%) reported at least 1 TEAE.
L1605: - SAEs were reported by 8 (14%) participants.
- Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs were reported by 22 (39%) participants.
- Treatment-related TEAEs were reported by 53 (95%) participants.
- TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation were reported by 5 (9%) participants.
- TEAEs leading to dose modification were reported by 20 (36%) participants.
L1606: ## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1607: - No deaths occurred in the pediatric cohort.
- AESIs (AEs of Grade \ge 3 for the following PTs: diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, rash, and acneiform rash. AEs of Grade \ge 2 for the following PTs were also considered AESIs: RVO, uveitis, optic neuropathy, retinopathy, retinal detachment, ejection fraction decreased, cardiac failure, left ventricular dysfunction, confusion, hallucinations, and delirium) were reported by 19 (34%) participants.
L1608: In the adult cohort in Study MEK-NF-201, 58 (100%) reported at least 1 TEAE.
L1609: - SAEs were reported by 10 (17%) participants.
- Grade \ge 3 TEAEs were reported by 21 (36%) participants.
- Treatment-related TEAEs were reported by 57 (98%) participants.
- TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation were reported by 13 (22%) participants.
- TEAEs leading to dose modification were reported by 24 (41%) participants.
- One Grade 5 event (death) of COVID-19 occurred.
- AESIs were reported by 18 (31%) participants.
L1610: In the pediatric cohort of Study MEK-NF-201, 1016 events were reported by 56 (100%) participants. The most frequently reported PTs (&gt;35\%) were diarrhea, dermatitis acneiform, and vomiting.
L1611: In the adult cohort of Study MEK-NF-201, 819 events were reported by 58 (100%) participants. The most frequently reported (&gt;35\%) PTs were dermatitis acneiform, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting.
L1612: TEAEs occurred in all (100%) participants in the pediatric and adult cohorts of Pool 1. In the pediatric cohort, the most frequently reported PTs (&gt;35\%) were diarrhea, dermatitis acneiform, vomiting and headache. In the adult cohort, the most frequently reported PTs (&gt;35\%) were dermatitis acneiform, diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, and fatigue.

L1622: ## **Adverse Drug Reactions**
L1623: Adverse drug reactions (ADRs) for mirdametinib are defined as those AEs considered related to mirdametinib based on the assessment of numerical frequency data and review of individual case details such as temporal association of the event with administration of mirdametinib, dechallenge and rechallenge information, and confounding factors such as intercurrent illnesses and administration of concomitant medications which could be plausible alternative causes for the reported events. The presence of a description of a plausible mechanism by which MEK inhibition could lead to the reported event in the literature was also taken into account when considering if a reported event was an adverse reaction for mirdametinib.
L1624: Based on the totality of the data, AEs that are considered ADRs for mirdametinib in Study MEK-NF-201 and Pool 1 are presented in Table 56 and Table 57, respectively, which forms the basis for the ADR information included in the prescribing information.
L1625: The data described below reflect exposure to mirdametinib in participants with NF1 PN in Study MEK-NF-201 (N=114) or pooled across studies MEK-NF-201 and NF-106 in Pool 1 (N=133). Serious ADRs, permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib due to an ADR, dose interruptions due to ADRs and dose reductions due to ADRs are described below. Note that individual participants could have reported more than 1 event leading to a dose modification or dose modifications, or as reasons for treatment discontinuation.
L1626: ## Study MEK-NF-201
L1627: No serious ADRs occurred in pediatric participants who received mirdametinib. Serious ADRs occurred in 2 (3%) adult participants who received mirdametinib. Serious ADRs occurring in adult participants were RVO (1 [2%] participant) and abdominal pain (1 [2%] participant).
L1628: Permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 3 (5%) pediatric participants. ADRs which resulted in permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib in pediatric participants were abdominal pain, constipation, dermatitis acneiform, diarrhea, and rash in 1 (2%) participant each. Permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 12 (21%) adult participants. ADRs which resulted in permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib in adult participants were dermatitis acneiform (4 [7%] participants), diarrhea (4 [7%] participants), nausea (4 [7%] participants), and abdominal pain, alopecia, dry skin, ejection fraction decreased, rash, rash erythematous, RVO, and vomiting in 1 (2%) participant each.
L1629: Dose interruptions of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 5 (9%) pediatric participants. ADRs which required dose interruption in pediatric participants included neutrophil count decreased (2 [4%] participants), and abdominal pain, blood CPK increased, diarrhea, and ejection fraction decreased in 1 (2%) participant each. Dose interruptions of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 7 (12%) adult participants. ADRs which required dose interruption in adult participants included ejection fraction decreased (5 [9%] participants), and stomatitis and vomiting in 1 (2%) participant each.
L1630: ## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1631: Dose reductions of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 7 (13%) pediatric participants. ADRs which required dose reduction in pediatric participants included neutrophil count decreased (3 [5%] participants), and abdominal pain upper, blood CPK increased, dermatitis acneiform, diarrhea, and rash in 1 (2%) participant each. Dose reductions of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 10 (17%) adult participants. ADRs which required dose reduction in adult participants included dermatitis acneiform (6 [10%] participants), and blood CPK increased, constipation, ejection fraction decreased, and fatigue in 1 (2%) participant each.
L1632: ## Pool 1
L1633: No serious ADRs occurred in pediatric participants who received mirdametinib. Serious ADRs occurred in 3 (4%) adult participants who received mirdametinib. Serious ADRs occurring in adult participants were abdominal pain (2 [3%] participants) and RVO (1 [1%] participant).
L1634: Permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 3 (5%) pediatric participants. ADRs which resulted in permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib in pediatric participants were abdominal pain, constipation, dermatitis acneiform, diarrhea, and rash in 1 (2%) participant each. Permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 15 (20%) adult participants. ADRs which resulted in permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib in adult participants were dermatitis acneiform (6 [8%] participants), diarrhea (4 [5%] participants), nausea (4 [5%] participants), vomiting (2 [3%] participants), and abdominal pain, alopecia, dry skin, ejection fraction decreased, rash, rash erythematous, and RVO in 1 (1%) participant each.
L1635: Dose interruptions of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 5 (9%) pediatric participants. ADRs which required dose interruption in pediatric participants included neutrophil count decreased (2 [3%] participants), and abdominal pain, blood CPK increased, diarrhea, and ejection fraction decreased in 1 (2%) participant each. Dose interruptions of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 9 (12%) adult participants. ADRs which required dose interruption in adult participants included ejection fraction decreased (5 [7%] participants), dermatitis acneiform (2 [3%] participants), and rash pustular, stomatitis, and vomiting in 1 (1%) participant each.
L1636: Dose reductions of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 7 (12%) pediatric participants. ADRs which required dose reduction in pediatric participants included neutrophil count decreased (3 [5%] participants), and abdominal pain upper, blood CPK increased, dermatitis acneiform, diarrhea, and rash in 1 (2%) participant each. Dose reductions of mirdametinib due to an ADR occurred in 15 (20%) adult participants. ADRs which required dose reduction in adult participants included dermatitis acneiform (8 [11%] participants), fatigue (2 [3%] participants), and abdominal pain, blood CPK increased, constipation, ejection fraction decreased, and nausea in 1 (1%) participant each.

L1667: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1668: The overall TEAE profile in Pool 1 was similar to what was observed in Study MEK-NF-201. Overall, the TEAE profile between the pediatric and adult cohorts of Pool 1 was similar. There were more participants in the adult cohort that reported TEAEs that led to study discontinuation (12 [16%] participants in the adult cohort vs 4 (7%) participants in the pediatric cohort) and treatment discontinuation (16 [21%] participants in the adult cohort vs 5 [9%] participants in the pediatric cohort).
L1669: Overall, the incidence of TEAEs in Pool 1 was similar to what was seen in the pivotal study, MEK-NF-201. The most frequently (&gt;35%) reported TEAEs for the total population from Study MEK-NF-201 and Pool 1 were dermatitis acneiform, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting. The most frequently reported PTs were expected with MEK inhibition (Subbiah 2020, Solares 2021).
L1670: The following events are recognized as risks for patients with NF1 PN treated with mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID:
L1671: - The following events were recognized as identified risks because of the frequency of their reports, temporal relationship to the start of treatment with mirdametinib, and presence of a plausible biologic mechanism, and were deemed important due to their potential impact on patients: ejection fraction decreased and retinal vein occlusion. Given their importance, these risks are recommended for inclusion in Warnings and Precautions.
- The following events were reported at an increased incidence relative to that anticipated for the patient population with NF1 PN and are recognized as non-important identified risks as these events were generally Grade 1 or 2 and did not frequently result in the discontinuation of study treatment: diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, constipation, skin events (including dermatitis acneiform, rash, pruritus, dry skin, alopecia, hair color changes, and hair texture), blood CPK increased, alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, blood alkaline phosphatase increased, and fatigue.While dermatitis acneiform and rash are considered non-important identified risks, they are proposed for inclusion in the Warnings and Precautions due to recommended dose modifications for severe events.
- The data presented support recognizing stomatitis as a non-important identified risk for mirdametinib in the pediatric population and as a non-important potential risk in the adult population.
- The data presented support recognizing paronychia, eczema, and neutrophil count decreased, as non-important identified risks for mirdametinib in the pediatric population.
- Given the low frequency and severity of events, the data support vision blurred and detachment of retinal pigment epithelium being recognized as important potential risks. Given their importance, these risks are recommended for inclusion in Warnings and
L1672: Precautions.
L1673: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1674: The safety databases for Pool 1 and the ReNeu study were analyzed at each level of the MedDRA hierarchy for TEAEs. TEAEs are defined as AEs that occurred from the time of the first dose up to 30 days after the last dose of mirdametinib or the data cutoff date if that date was earlier. All patients in the adult and pediatric population that were exposed to mirdametinib had at least one AE during treatment. FDA's analysis of adverse events in Pool 1 and the ReNeu study did not consider relatedness as part of the safety review.
L1675: Table 60 represents the most frequent AEs (\ge 20\%) observed in the ReNeu study. The most common adverse reactions (&gt;25\%) in the adult population were rash, diarrhea, nausea, musculoskeletal pain, vomiting, and fatigue. The most common adverse reactions (&gt;25\%) in the pediatric population were rash, diarrhea, musculoskeletal pain, abdominal pain, vomiting, headache, paronychia, left ventricular dysfunction, and nausea.

L1689: ## Laboratory Findings
L1690: ## Data:
L1691: Refer to Table 58 and Table 59.
L1692: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1693: The data supports recognizing blood CPK increased, AST increased, ALT increased, and blood alkaline phosphatase increased as non-important identified risks for mirdametinib, neutrophil count decreased as a non-important identified risk for pediatric patients receiving mirdametinib, and ejection fraction decreased as an important identified risk for mirdametinib. Of these identified risks, only ejection fraction decreased is assessed as being an important identified risk and is therefore proposed for inclusion in Warnings and Precautions. While the measured decreases in ejection fraction were asymptomatic in study participants, the potential clinical impact of an undetected decrease in ejection fraction is not known, hence this risk is considered important. Blood CPK increased is considered a non-important identified risk in view of the lack of reports of serious events, including no reports of rhabdomyolysis. AST and ALT increased are considered non-important identified risks due to the infrequency of Grade 3 shifts and dose modifications. Blood alkaline phosphatase is considered a non-important identified risk due to the absence of correlation with other markers of liver injury in Pool 1. Neutrophil count decreased is recognized as a non-important identified risk for mirdametinib in pediatric participants only given the infrequent reporting of the event in the adult cohort, and the absence of risks of opportunistic infections to participants reporting or observed to have decreased neutrophils.
L1694: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1695: Laboratory tests to assess hematology and chemistries parameters were measured at baseline, followed by Day 15 during every Cycle. The reported percentages were calculated using the number of patients with baseline and post-baseline results for each respective parameter. Table 50 summarizes the common laboratory abnormalities for adult and pediatric patients with NF1-associated PN receiving mirdametinib in the ReNeu study. The most common Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormality (&gt;2%) in the adult population was increased creatine phosphokinase. The most common Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities (&gt;2%) in the pediatric population were decreased neutrophil count and increased creatine phosphokinase.
