L1718: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1719: The overall data suggests that changes in vital signs during treatment with mirdametinib is likely attributable to normal variability and fluctuations and does not support recognizing any changes in vital signs as an identified risk for mirdametinib.
L1720: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1721: Vital signs were collected at baseline and then once every Cycle until Cycle 7 followed by every other cycle. In Pool 1, the adult cohort had 7 (9%) patients with Grades 1 and 2 hypertension and 1 (1.3%) patient with Grade 3 hypertension. Four patients (7%) in the pediatric cohort had Grade 2 hypertension; one event was captured as a serious adverse event and is included in the USPI. The event of hypertension occurred \ge 1 year from the first study treatment administration and was assessed as not related to mirdametinib. FDA agrees that changes in vital sign were likely consistent with normal variability and generally not a safety concern.
L1722: ## **Electrocardiograms (ECGs)**
L1723: ## **Data:**
L1724: ECG parameters were not collected in Study NF-106 and therefore observations concerning ECGs came from Study MEK-NF-201 only.
L1725: In Study MEK-NF-201, in the pediatric cohort, there were no major changes observed in ECG parameters. In the adult cohort, several participants had QTcF between 450 and 480 msec throughout the study and 1 participant had a QTcF &gt;480 msec on Cycle 5 Day 15. One TEAE of Grade 1 electrocardiogram QT prolonged was reported; this participant did not report a QTcF &gt;450 msec. There were no major changes observed in other ECG parameters.
L1726: There were no major changes observed in ECG parameters in Pool 2.
L1727: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1728: Mirdametinib did not appear to have clinically meaningful adverse effects on ECG values during any study.
L1729: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1730: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1731: ## **QT**
L1732: ## **Data:**
L1733: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1734: A thorough QT/QTc study to evaluate the effect of mirdametinib and metabolite exposures on cardiac repolarization in healthy participants (MEK-NF-102) was conducted. In that study, the relationship between mirdametinib concentration and QTcF was successfully characterized using a pre-specified linear mixed-effects model. No clinically significant effects of both mirdametinib 6 and 14mg doses (approximately 2x and 6x the therapeutic exposure of mirdametinib, respectively) were observed on the ECG.
L1735: Additionally, a search of the MedDRA Standardized MedDRA Queries “Torsades de pointes/QT prolongation” plus the PT of seizure in the pooled clinical safety data was conducted. Five pediatric and 5 adult participants with NF1 PN in Pool 1 were identified. All participants were receiving mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID, with a maximum total daily dose of 4 mg BID. In Pool 1, 1 adult participant reported a non-serious TEAE of ECG QT prolonged. This participant had an average baseline QTcF of 367.1 msec. On C1D15, their average QTcF was 404.92 msec, representing an increase of 37.82 msec, when the event of electrocardiogram QT prolonged was reported. The event resolved with the next triplicate ECG during the C2D15 visit (Day 42), when the average QTcF was 369.17 msec. No events of torsades de pointes were reported.
L1736: In Pool 1, 2 pediatric and 2 adult participants reported TEAEs of seizure, including 1 serious event in a pediatric participant. All 4 participants experiencing seizure had confounding medical history, including seizure, epilepsy, complex partial seizures, or ongoing long-term use of levetiracetam and clonazepam.
L1737: All other TEAEs from Pool 1 were non-serious; these included: 3 pediatric participants and 1 adult participant reporting events of syncope and 1 adult participant reporting an event of electrocardiogram repolarization abnormality. All events of syncope were assessed as not related to study treatment. One participant interrupted study treatment due to syncope; no other participants modified study treatment as a result of syncope events. One participant reported a related event of electrocardiogram repolarization abnormality on Day 15. The event resolved with the next triplicate ECG on Day 42 with no change to study treatment.
L1738: In Pool 2, 10 adult participants with advanced cancers were identified with MedDRA PTs under the Standardized MedDRA Queries for “Torsades de pointes/QT prolongation” plus the PT of seizure. All events in Pool 2 occurred in participants receiving doses of mirdametinib ≥8mg BID, which is twice the upper limit evaluated in participants with NF1 PN in Pool 1. However, this may have occurred because of the small number of participants in Pool 2 receiving mirdametinib doses ≤4mg BID (n = 16 versus n = 97), combined with the participants’ advanced, often metastatic, cancers.
L1739: In Pool 2, 1 participant reported a non-serious TEAE of electrocardiogram QT prolonged. This participant’s triplicate QTcF readings at screening were 427, 422, and 431 msec. On Day 1, their triplicate QTcF readings were 424, 423, and 431 msec. On Day 8, when the event was reported, the triplicate QTcF readings were 420, 427, and 423 msec. The event was assessed as related
L1740: and not resolved as of the end of study treatment. No other ECG reports are available for this participant. No events of torsades de pointes were reported.
L1741: Four participants reported TEAEs of syncope, including 1 participant who also reported cardiac arrest, loss of consciousness, ventricular fibrillation, and ventricular tachycardia. This participant died, with the cause of death noted as myocardial infarction, cerebrovascular accident, and cerebral disorder. This participant's event of syncope which occurred and resolved on Day 41 resulted in dose interruption. The event of myocardial infarction resulted in study drug discontinuation. All other events (cardiac arrest, loss of consciousness, ventricular fibrillation, and ventricular tachycardia) occurred following discontinuation of study treatment and in the context of the participant's myocardial infarction. Importantly, this participant was found to have a total occlusion of the left anterior descending artery, which was assessed as not related to study treatment.
L1742: One additional participant reported cardiac arrest assessed as not related to study treatment. This participant's cause of death was listed as cancer and cardio-respiratory arrest due to disease progression from their metastatic breast cancer.
L1743: One additional participant reported loss of consciousness on Day 4, 1 day after the TEAE of renal failure and 3 days after the TEAE of hypoxia, both of which resulted in study drug discontinuation. The participant died on Day 5 with cause of death cited as hypoxia, disease progression, and renal failure. None of these events were assessed as related to study treatment.
L1744: Two participants reported serious TEAEs of seizure. Both events were assessed as not related to study treatment. One participant interrupted study treatment as a result of the event. The second participant did not modify study treatment as a result of the event; this participant subsequently died, with cause of death listed as disease progression due to malignant melanoma.
L1745: One participant reported a TEAE of electrocardiogram repolarization abnormality resulting in discontinuation from the study. Of note, this participant was found to have a T-wave abnormality noted on the first of their triplicate baseline ECGs. This finding was then noted on their triplicate ECGs on Day 1, along with non-specific ST-T changes, when the event was reported. It was assessed as related to study treatment and study treatment was discontinued.
L1746: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1747: In the thorough QT/QTc study, mirdametinib had no effect on QTc at doses of 6 and 14mg, which resulted in approximately 2x and 6x the therapeutic exposures, respectively.
L1748: ## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1749: An analysis of the pooled clinical safety data demonstrated that mirdametinib has not been shown to cause QT prolongation or Torsade de pointes. In addition, no death or discontinuations were reported due to QT prolongation events.
L1750: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1751: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that mirdametinib had no effect on QTc interval.

L1754: Mirdametinib is a small molecule and not expected to elicit an immune response.

L1757: ## **8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues**
L1758: The adverse events of special interest (AESIs) defined in the pivotal Study MEK-NF-201 included AEs of Grade \ge 3 for the following PTs: diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, rash, and acneiform rash. AEs of Grade \ge 2 for the following PTs were also considered AESIs: RVO, uveitis, optic neuropathy, retinopathy, retinal detachment, ejection fraction decreased, cardiac failure, left ventricular dysfunction, confusion, hallucinations, and delirium.
L1759: To account for legacy studies in which no AESIs were prospectively identified per the protocol, safety topics of interest, which include the AESIs for MEK-NF-201 and other events that emerged upon review of the data across studies including events from Pool 2 and the Global Pharmacovigilance database were analyzed. Important identified risks for mirdametinib included the following topics: Ocular effects: retinal vein occlusion and Cardiac effects: ejection fraction decreased.
L1760: ## **8.2.5.1. Retinal Vein Occlusion**
L1761: ## **Data:**
L1762: In Pool 1, RVO occurred in 2 (3%) adult participants, both from Study MEK-NF-201. One participant reported a SAE of RVO while the other participant was observed to have 2 non-serious asymptomatic Grade 1 RVO events, 1 in each eye occurring approximately 1 year apart. These non-serious events were identified via routine monitoring. No events of RVO have been observed in pediatric participants in Pool 1.
L1763: In Pool 2, RVO occurred in 3 (3%) participants, all of which were serious. Of the 3 participants, 2 (9%) were in the 8, 10 mg BID dose group, while 1 (1%) participant was treated with mirdametinib 15 mg BID.
L1764: An analysis of all participants with RVO in Pools 1 and 2 showed that all participants reported at least 1 predisposing factor for RVO, including hypertension, diabetes, hypercholesterolemia, COVID-19 vaccination, and advanced cancer, as well as concomitant medications. However, the role of mirdametinib cannot be excluded (Li 2023).
L1765: The microvascular abnormalities associated with NF1 may make this patient population susceptible to RVO (Moadel 1994; Mori 2001). RVO has been described in clinical trials of MEK inhibitors (Méndez-Martínez 2019). MEK inhibitors may block antiapoptotic proteins, block natural retinal reactions to stress or oxidative injury, and prevent the repair of damaged retinal pigment epithelial cells, ultimately leading to the accumulation of damage and the degeneration of cells (van der Noll 2013). It has been shown that asymptomatic retinal changes may be present in a large percentage of MEK inhibitor treated patients (van Dijk 2018) and ocular toxicities with MEK inhibitors appear to be a class effect that is generally transient in nature (Leijen 2012; LoRusso 201; van der Noll 2013; van Dijk 2018). Dose and dosing schedules may be important factors for the risk of RVO in patients treated with MEK inhibitors (van der Noll 2013). Individual characteristics that have not been fully characterized likely also contribute to the risk for RVO.
L1766: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1767: The available data, along with a plausible mechanism associated with MEK inhibition, support recognizing RVO as an important identified risk for mirdametinib, and therefore proposed for inclusion in the Warnings and Precautions of the label, though it should be noted that events in patients with NF1 PN may be confounded by the microvascular abnormalities associated with NF1 (Moadel 1994, Mori 2001).
L1768: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1769: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's incidence rates for retinal and visual AEs in Pool 1. In the pooled safety population, ocular toxicity occurred in 25% of patients treated with mirdametinib; of these 20% were Grade 1, 3.8% were Grade 2, and 0.8% were Grade 3. In the adult pooled safety population, ocular toxicity occurred in 28% of patients treated with mirdametinib, of these 21% were Grade 1, 5% were Grade 2, and 1.3% were Grade 3. RVO occurred in 2.7% of adult patients including one Grade 3 case which required permanent discontinuation; a narrative is provided below. Retinal pigment epithelium detachment (RPED) occurred in one adult (1.3%) and blurred vision occurred in 9% of adult patients.
L1770: Patient narrative for the SAE of RVO: 26-year-old female with Grade 3 RVO on Day 130. Posterior segment/fundus imaging showed 360-degree Grade 2 edema of the right optic nerve with blurring of disc margins, tortuous temporal artery occlusion that extended from the optic nerve head (ONH) until the superior region of the macula, and nasal branch retinal vein occlusion; scattered hemorrhages throughout the posterior pole of the eye and midperiphery of the right retina due to the event of RVO. Treatment was discontinued on Day 129 in
L1771: response to the event. The event of RVO resolved with sequelae reported as “fuzzy vision.”
L1772: In the pediatric pooled safety population, ocular toxicity occurred in 19% of patients treated with mirdametinib. Of these, 17% were Grade 1 and 1.7% were Grade 2. Blurred vision occurred in 7% of patients.
L1773: While serious toxicities such as RVO and RPED occurred in the adult population, there is no evidence that pediatric patients have a lower risk for MEK-associated retinal AEs than adult patients.
L1774: Ocular toxicity will be included in the Warnings and Precautions section of the USPI.
L1775: ## 8.2.5.2. Ejection Fraction Decreased
L1776: ## **Data:**
L1777: In Study MEK-NF-201, 20 events of ejection fraction decreased were observed in 15 (27%) pediatric participants. One participant had a Grade 3 event, and all other participants had Grade 2 events. Of note, there is no Grade 1 ejection fraction decreased per CTCAE v5.0 criteria. One pediatric participant (2%) had one event of ejection fraction decreased that led to dose interruption of mirdametinib. No pediatric participants had events of ejection fraction decreased that led to dose reduction or discontinuation of mirdametinib. Fourteen (25%) participants had resolution of events; 5 (9%) of these participants had recurrence of events which were not resolved as of the data cut.
L1778: In Study MEK-NF-201, 13 events of ejection fraction decreased were observed in 9 (16%) adult participants. No adult participants had Grade \ge 3 events. Five (9%) participants had events of ejection fraction decreased that led to treatment interruption. One (2%) participant each had an event of ejection fraction decreased that led to dose reduction or treatment discontinuation.
L1779: In Pool 2, while baseline values are available from all participants receiving doses of mirdametinib ranging from 1 mg QD to 10 mg BID, there are no subsequent on-treatment ejection fraction values reported in these participants. Five (22%) participants receiving mirdametinib at 8, 10 mg BID had an event of ejection fraction decreased. Five (7%) participants receiving mirdametinib at 15, 20, 30 mg BID had events of ejection fraction decreased. In total, events of ejection fraction decreased were less frequently reported in Pool 2 (10 [8%]) than in Pool 1 (24 [18%]), suggesting that there is not a dose response observed with mirdametinib and ejection fraction decreased. While the events were only reported in mirdametinib doses \ge 8 mg BID in Pool 2, this may have occurred due to the limited numbers of participants receiving mirdametinib doses \le 4 mg BID coupled with a lack of on-treatment evaluations. Ejection fraction data is limited from Pool 2; the infrequent reporting of clinically meaningful ejection fraction decreases, the lack of serious events, and infrequent dose
L1780: interruption or discontinuation suggest that these events in Pool 2 are similar to those observed in Pool 1.
L1781: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1782: Ejection fraction decreased is recognized as an identified risk for mirdametinib and therefore proposed for inclusion in the Warnings and Precautions section of the label. While the measured decreases in ejection fraction were asymptomatic in the study participants, the potential clinical impact of an undetected decrease in ejection fraction is not known, hence this risk is considered important.
L1783: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1784: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of data for events of decreased ejection fraction in Pool 1; all events observed were from the ReNeu study as echocardiograms were not routinely obtained in Study NF-106. In the ReNeu study, decreased LVEF of 10 to &lt;20% occurred in 20% of patients and decreased LVEF of ≥20% occurred in 0.9% of patients treated with mirdametinib. All patients with events of decreased LVEF were identified during routine echocardiography and these events resolved in 75% of these patients. Per NCI CTCAE Version 5, severity grading for "decreased LVEF" begins with Grade 2, defined as a reduction of 10 to &lt;20% from baseline.
L1785: In adult patients in the ReNeu study, decreased LVEF of 10 to &lt;20% occurred in 16% of adult patients. Of the adult patients with decreased LVEF, five patients (9%) required dose interruption, one patient (1.7%) required a dose reduction and one patient required permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib. All 7 patients who required dose interruption, dose reduction and discontinuation of mirdametinib, recovered from event of decreased LVEF. The median time to first onset of decreased LVEF in adult patients was 70 days.
L1786: In pediatric patients in the ReNeu study, decreased LVEF of 10 to &lt;20% occurred in 25% of patients, and decreased LVEF of ≥ 20% occurred in 1.8% of patients treated with mirdametinib. Of the pediatric patients with decreased LVEF, one patient (1.8%) required dose interruption of mirdametinib, after which the event resolved. The median time to first onset of decreased LVEF in pediatric patients was 132 days.
L1787: Left ventricular dysfunction will be included in Warnings and Precautions of the USPI.
L1788: ## **8.2.5.3. Skin Events (dermatitis acneiform, rash, pruritus, dry skin, alopecia, hair color changes, and hair texture)**
L1789: ## **Data:**
L1790: Collectively, dermatitis acneiform and a composite of other rash terms including rash, rash maculo-papular, rash pustular, rash erythematous, rash papular, exfoliative rash, papule, rash macular, and rash pruritic were reported by 39 (70%) pediatric participants and 52 (90%) adult participants in Study MEK-NF-201. Similar to Study MEK-NF-201, dermatitis acneiform and a composite of other rash terms were reported by 40 (69%) pediatric participants and 69 (92%) adult participants in Pool 1.
L1791: In Pool 1, dermatitis acneiform and non-acneiform rashes occurred in 69% of pediatric patients. Dermatitis acneiform occurred with a higher frequency in patients aged 12 to 17 years, while non-acneiform rashes occurred with a higher frequency in patients aged 2 to 11 years. Grade 3 dermatitis acneiform occurred in 1.7% of pediatric patients, and Grade 3 non-acneiform rashes occurred in 1.7% of pediatric patients. These rashes resulted in discontinuations in 3.4% of pediatric patients and dose reductions in 3.4% of pediatric patients, but no dose interruptions were reported in pediatric patients.
L1792: In Pool 1, dermatitis acneiform and non-acneiform rashes occurred in 92% of adults. Grade 3 dermatitis acneiform and non-acneiform rashes occurred in 7% and 1.3% of adults, respectively. These rashes resulted in discontinuations in 11%, dose interruptions in 2.7%, and dose reductions in 11% of adult patients.
L1793: In Pool 1, 20 (15%) participants reported dry skin. No significant difference was observed across age cohorts. Of the 20 participants who reported the event of dry skin, 16 (12%) were assessed as related to the study treatment with none being Grade \ge 3 severity. One (1%) participant reported a TEAE of dry skin that led to study treatment discontinuation. No participants reported SAEs and no participants reported TEAEs of dry skin that led to death. The data from the Study MEK-NF-201 were aligned with the data in Pool 1.
L1794: Pruritus was reported in Pool 1 by 17 (13%) participants. Among the 17 participants who reported the TEAE, 10 (13%) were in the adult cohort and 7 (12%) were in the pediatric cohort. A total of 11 (8%) participants reported a TEAE of pruritus that was considered related to the study treatment by the investigator. No occurrences of Grade \ge 3 events or SAEs, and no treatment discontinuations, were reported in Pool 1.
L1795: Collectively, hair changes, including alopecia and hair texture abnormal, were reported by 11 (19%) pediatric and 10 (13%) adult participants in Pool 1. Among the events classified as hair changes, alopecia was the most frequently reported TEAE with an incidence of 13% (17 participants) in Pool 1. Alopecia was reported in 8 (14%) pediatric participants and 9 (12%) adult participants. A total of 16 (12%) participants who reported alopecia were assessed as related to the study treatment by the investigators. One (1%) participant discontinued treatment due to alopecia. A higher incidence of hair color changes and hair texture abnormality were reported in the pediatric cohort compared to the adult cohort. In Pool 1, 7 (12%) out of the 8 participants who reported hair color changes were pediatric.
L1796: Additionally, 4 (3%) participants in Pool 1 reported hair texture abnormality, including 3 (5%) participants who were in the pediatric cohort. Of note, hair texture abnormal was reported less frequently compared to alopecia. None of the reports of hair changes in Pool 1 were Grade \ge 3.
L1797: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1798: Skin events, including dermatitis acneiform, a composite of non-acneiform rashes (including rash, rash maculo-papular, rash pustular, rash erythematous, rash papular, exfoliative rash, papule, rash macular, and rash pruritus), alopecia, hair texture changes, hair color changes, dry skin, and pruritus, were reported at an increased incidence relative to that anticipated for the patient population with NF1 PN. They are recognized as non-important identified risks as these events were generally Grade 1 or 2 and did not frequently result in the discontinuation of study treatment. However, dose modifications may be beneficial for patients; as such, skin toxicity is proposed for inclusion in the Warnings and Precautions section of labeling.
L1799: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1800: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of data for dermatologic adverse events for patients in Pool 1. In the pooled safety population, rash occurred in 84% of patients; of these, 31% were Grade 2 and 6% were Grade 3. FDA's assessment of the grouped term for rash included the following preferred terms (PTs): dermatitis acneiform, eczema, maculo-papular rash, pustular rash, dermatitis, erythematous rash, palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia syndrome, exfoliative rash, skin exfoliation, pruritic rash, papule, papular rash and macular rash. The most frequent PTs included in the grouped term for rash that occurred in \ge 2\% of patients were dermatitis acneiform (65%), rash (11%), eczema (8%), maculo-papular rash (4.5%) and pustular rash (3.8%).
L1801: In the pooled adult safety population, an event of rash occurred in 92% of patients: 37% were Grade 2 and 8% were Grade 3 in severity. An event of rash requiring permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib occurred in 11% of adult patients.
L1802: In the pooled pediatric safety population, rash occurred in 72% of patients: 22% were Grade 2 and 3.4% were Grade 3 reactions. An event of rash resulting in permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib occurred in 3.4% of pediatric patients.
L1803: Dermatitis acneiform occurred with a higher frequency in patients aged 12 to 17 years (77%) than those aged 2 to 11 years (16%), while non-acneiform rashes occurred with a higher frequency in patients aged 2 to 11 years (53%) than those aged 12 to 17 years (15%). This observation is consistent with the known safety profile of MEK inhibitors in adolescent patients for whom dermatitis acneiform-related events occur at a relatively high incidence.
L1804: An instance of discrepant data was noted in the dataset for Study NF-106 in which one patient with an AE of "rash acneiform" was included with an AE outcome of death. However, the AE end date was missing, and the event was graded as "Mild" (Grade 1). FDA sent an
L1805: information request (IR) for clarification and the Applicant confirmed that the fatal outcome on the AE CRF represented an error in data reporting and was not a fatal event or SAE.
L1806: Dermatologic adverse reactions will be included in the Warning and Precautions of the USPI.
L1807: ## Other Noteworthy Adverse Events
L1808: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1809: In addition to the skin events mentioned in Section 8.2.5.3, the following events are considered non-important identified risks: diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, constipation, fatigue, blood CPK increased, alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, and blood alkaline phosphatase increased. While these were reported at an increased incidence relative to that anticipated for the patient population with NF1 PN, and so are recognized as identified risks for mirdametinib, these events were generally Grade 1 or 2 and did not frequently result in the discontinuation of study treatment, hence they are considered non-important.
L1810: Stomatitis was recognized as a non-important identified risk for the pediatric population and as a potential risk in the adult population. In addition, the following risks were recognized as non-important identified risks in the pediatric population only: paronychia, eczema, and neutrophil count decreased.
L1811: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1812: GI toxicity is a known MEK inhibitor class effect. FDA's analysis of events of gastrointestinal disorders in Pool 1 revealed that most adverse events of diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, constipation, and stomatitis were Grades 1 and 2 in severity. Grade 3 adverse events of abdominal pain occurred in 4% of adult patients; Grade 3 diarrhea and abdominal pain occurred in 5% and 3.4% of pediatric patients, respectively. There were no Grade 4 adverse events.
L1813: Based on FDA's analysis of the safety data describing the observed GI toxicity in Pool 1, it was not included in the Warnings and Precautions section of the mirdametinib USPI due to the very low incidence of severe (Grade 3) events and the low incidence of events requiring discontinuation of therapy.
L1814: Increased CPK is a class effect reported with other MEK inhibitors. In Pool 1, increased CPK occurred in 52% of patients and \ge Grade 3 events of increased CPK occurred in 3.8% of patients. Most of the events were Grade 1 or 2 in severity, with Grade 3 events occurring in 3 (5%) pediatric patients and a Grade 4 event occurring in 1 (2%) pediatric patient. One (1%) Grade 4 event was observed in an adult patient.
L1815: Among the 34 (59%) pediatric and 34 (47%) adult patients in Pool 1 with events of increased CPK, coincident events of “pain in extremity” occurred in 2 pediatric and 2 adult patients. Coincident events of “arthralgia” occurred in 1 adult and 1 pediatric patient while coincident event of “musculoskeletal chest pain” occurred in 1 adult patient. No events of rhabdomyolysis were reported.
L1816: In Pool 1, musculoskeletal pain occurred in 41% of adult and pediatric patients. There were 5 (7%) Grade 3 events in adult patients and 1 (1.7%) Grade 3 event in a pediatric patient. Myalgia occurred in 3 (4%) adult patients and 3 (5%) pediatric patients, and all events were Grade 1.
L1817: Based on FDA's analysis of the safety data describing increased CPK in Pool 1, it was not included in the Warnings and Precautions section of the USPI given the high proportion of Grade 1 and 2 adverse events and the low incidence of severe consequences of increased CPK (including no observed rhabdomyolysis events).
L1818: ## Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1819: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1820: PROs are discussed in Section 8.1.1 and Section 8.1.2.
L1821: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1822: Refer to Section 8.1.1 and Section 8.1.2.
L1823: ## 8.2.6. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1824: ## Data:
L1825: ## **Intrinsic factors**
L1826: The safety of mirdametinib was analyzed by the following subgroups: age, sex, race, ethnicity, BMI, and organ dysfunction.
L1827: For Pool 1, TEAEs were described according to age subgroup (2 to &lt;12 years, 12 to &lt;18 years, 18 to &lt;40 years, 40 to &lt;65 years, and ≥65 years). A total of 2 participants ≥65 years of age in Pool 1 were exposed to mirdametinib at doses of 2mg/m2. The small sample size limits the ability to identify differences in the safety of mirdametinib in this age group. In Pool 1, there were some differences in the incidence of TEAEs between the pediatric and adult cohorts. A summary of PTs with an apparent age-associated trend in incidence in Pool 1 is presented in Table 63. Trends were identified using the following criteria: incidence of ≥10% in either cohort and a &gt;1.5x incidence in the adults versus the pediatric participants or the pediatric versus the adult participants. TEAEs of abdominal pain upper, cough, eczema, hair color changes, headache, nasal congestion, neutrophil count decreased, and WBC count decreased, oropharyngeal pain, paronychia, pharyngitis streptococcal, pyrexia, rash, rhinitis allergic and URTI occurred at a higher frequency in the pediatric cohort. An evaluation of TEAEs where
L1828: incidence increases with decreasing age in the pediatric cohort suggested that there were some differences between prepubertal participants (2 to 11 years of age) and post-pubertal participants (12 to 18 years of age). TEAEs of eczema, hair color changes, pharyngitis, streptococcal, pyrexia, rash, rhinitis allergic, stomatitis, and upper respiratory tract infection occurred at a higher frequency in the prepubertal participant. On review of TEAEs where incidence increases with increasing age, the data suggested that dermatitis acneiform mostly occurred at a higher frequency in the post-pubertal participants.
L1829: In Pool 2, TEAEs were described according to age subgroup (18 to &lt;40 years, 40 to &lt;65 years, and ≥65 years). A total of 35 (31%) participants were ≥65 years of age. Participants reported at least one SAE in the age groups: 65 years and above (n=21/35, 60%), 40 to &lt;65 years (n=30/70, 43%), and 18 to &lt;40 years (n=4/8, 50%). There were no differences in SAEs reported in these age groups in Pool 2.
L1830: It is difficult to draw conclusions regarding TEAEs by sex in Pool 1, based on the disproportionate impact of the difference in the number of females (n=79) to males (n=54), and the limited number of participants reporting each event.
L1831: The majority of participants were White (71% in Pool 1 and 89% in Pool 2), limiting the ability to draw conclusions about the effect of race on the safety of mirdametinib.
L1832: The majority of participants in either pool were 'Not Hispanic or Latino' (84% in Pool 1 and 58% in Pool 2), limiting the ability to draw conclusions about the effect of ethnicity on the safety of mirdametinib.
L1833: Due to the small sample sizes in the pediatric BMI subgroups in Pool 1, no apparent trends in TEAEs by BMI could be determined. There were no apparent trends in TEAEs by BMI in the adult cohort. Due to the small sample sizes in the BMI subgroups in Pool 2, no trends could be determined.
L1834: Urinary excretion of unchanged mirdametinib or its primary metabolite is a minor route of elimination. The major route of elimination is renal excretion of the glucuronide metabolite (67.7%), which is substantially less biologically active than the parent mirdametinib. Hence, the potential impact of renal impairment on the PK of mirdametinib is considered to be low.
L1835: No studies with mirdametinib were conducted in participants with altered hepatic function. PopPK analyses suggest that mirdametinib PK is not expected to be altered in patients with mild hepatic impairment. Mirdametinib is extensively metabolized, but to what extent this occurs in the liver is unknown, and it is not a narrow therapeutic index drug.
L1836: At the maximum recommended dose of 4 mg BID, clinically significant QTc interval prolongation was not observed, and the overall safety data do not support recognizing an effect on cardiac
L1837: conduction as a risk for mirdametinib. This data demonstrates that asymptomatic ejection fraction decreased is an important identified risk for mirdametinib, but other cardiac effects, including cardiomyopathy or cardiac failure, are not identified risks for mirdametinib. Given that clinical data in this population is currently limited, there is not sufficient information to draw conclusions on the use of mirdametinib in patients with pre-existing cardiovascular impairment.

L1849: ## Extrinsic factors
L1850: The safety of mirdametinib was analyzed by geographic location, food intake, or use of tobacco and alcohol. Although studies were conducted in the US in accordance with the principles of ICH E5 R1 guidance, the results can be extrapolated to the European Union (EU) population as NF1 PN is an orphan condition and there are no ethnic differences. There are no intrinsic or extrinsic ethnic factors that will preclude the extrapolation of the results. Moreover, the diagnosis and treatment of NF1 PN are similar in the US and EU.
L1851: No overall differences in safety were observed among participants as a result of food intake or use of tobacco and alcohol.
L1852: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1853: Overall, the observed differences among subgroups by age are not considered to have a clinical impact on the safety profile of mirdametinib as they were most likely attributable to the age difference between the groups and age-related development. As hepatic metabolism of mirdametinib has not been studied, its use in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment should be considered to have significant effects on the pharmacokinetics of mirdametinib. Please see Section 6.2.1 for additional discussion of the effect of hepatic impairment on mirdametinib PK.
L1854: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1855: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of safety subgroup analyses for age, sex, race, ethnicity, BMI, and organ dysfunction in the adult and pediatric cohorts of Pool 1. Please refer to Section 6.2.1 for a discussion on the effect of hepatic impairment on mirdametinib PK.
L1856: ## 8.2.7. **Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials**
L1857: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1858: No studies have been conducted with mirdametinib specifically to address safety concerns. Safety monitoring continues in the ongoing long-term follow-up phase of Study MEK-NF-201.
L1859: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1860: The FDA agrees and notes that the ReNeu study includes an optional Long-Term Follow-up (LTFU) Phase with continued safety monitoring that is currently ongoing. Patients who completed Cycle 24 of the Treatment Phase and did not meet withdrawal criteria were eligible to participate in the LTFU.
L1861: ## Additional Safety Explorations
L1862: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1863: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1864: No human carcinogenicity studies have been conducted with mirdametinib.
L1865: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1866: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1867: ## **Human Reproduction and Pregnancy**
L1868: ## **Data:**
L1869: Studies with mirdametinib have not been conducted in women who are pregnant or lactating. Reproductive animal toxicology studies have been completed with mirdametinib and confirm the reproductive toxicity of mirdametinib, consistent with effects observed in nonclinical studies with other MEK inhibitors. In embryo-fetal developmental toxicity studies in pregnant rats and rabbits, oral dosing of mirdametinib induced early resorptions, post-implantation loss and decreased fetal body weights in rats at dosages \ge 3 mg/kg/day and in rabbits at dosages \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day; these exposures were equivalent to the human exposure or approximately 0.4 times the human exposures at the recommended dose, respectively. In the rat study, at 3 mg/kg/day, a single fetus had extremity malformations.
L1870: There are no data on the presence of mirdametinib in human milk or the effects of mirdametinib on a breastfed child or milk production.
L1871: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1872: Mirdametinib should not be used during pregnancy. If a patient becomes pregnant while receiving this drug, treatment with mirdametinib should be stopped and the patient should be apprised of the potential hazard to the fetus.
L1873: Since drugs are commonly excreted in human milk and because of the potential for serious adverse reactions in nursing infants, women should not breastfeed during treatment with mirdametinib and for at least 1 week after the last dose of mirdametinib. Given a terminal elimination half-life of approximately 28 hours, it is expected that mirdametinib would no longer be present 1 week after the last dose.
L1874: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1875: FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement. There were no patients who discontinued from the study due to pregnancy, and birth control measures during treatment and ongoing pregnancy screening were enforced to ensure that no fetus was exposed to study drug.
L1876: On the ReNeu study, one case of pregnancy that occurred 31 days after the patient received her last dose of treatment with mirdametinib was reported. Seventy days after the patient
L1877: discontinued mirdametinib, she had a dilation and curettage of uterus due to a spontaneous abortion.
L1878: ## Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1879: ## Data:
L1880: Fifty-six pediatric participants aged 2 to 17 years were enrolled in the pivotal Phase 2b Study MEK-NF-201. For all participants 2 to 17 years of age, radiographs of the distal femur and proximal tibia were obtained at baseline. If the investigator determined that the physes were open, these participants underwent a radiograph in accordance with the Treatment Phase Schedule of Activities to monitor for changes in phyeal growth plates.
L1881: Relevant bone events from 4 pediatric participants in Study MEK-NF-201 were identified, as shown in Table 64. All 4 events were assessed as not related to mirdametinib treatment. The femoral neck fracture was reported as serious, and Grade 3, while the other events were Grade 1 or Grade 2 and non-serious. The femoral neck fracture was reported by a 12-year old female with a relevant medical history of protrusion acetabuli, leg length discrepancy, and levoscoliosis, on Day 269. The verbatim term was reported as right pathologic femoral neck fracture due to fall. The participant's target tumor was located in the hip affected by the fracture and was noted to be 426.60 mL at baseline (SW-000421). The location and size of the participant's tumor are confounding factors in this event.
L1882: A Grade 2 radius fracture was reported by a 6-year-old female on Day 735. No relevant medical history was noted, though the participant's target tumor was noted to be located in the upper limb. Additional details concerning the event are not available, though forearm fractures are the most common fracture in children under 16 years old, and account for approximately 40% of all pediatric long bone fractures (Oh 2022). The location of the participant's target tumor and epidemiology do not suggest this event was caused by mirdametinib.
L1883: Grade 1 osteopenia was reported by a 5-year-old female (Study MEK NF 201) on Day 664. The participant had a relevant medical history of bowing tibia, dextroscoliosis, and spinal fusion rods, and reported a fall resulting in a fractured spinal fusion rod and requiring surgery from Day 40 to Day 308. The participant's target tumor is noted as occurring in the mediastinum with intrathoracic involvement to the right upper extremity and cervical spine, with a tumor volume at baseline of 140.59 mL. The clinical effect of the participant's tumor, as evidenced by their medical history, is considered a confounder to the event of osteopenia.
L1884: A Grade 1 spinal deformity, described as a mild dextroconvex curvature of the thoracolumbar spine, with apex approximately at T11, and measuring 5 degrees, was reported in a 7-year-old male (Study MEK-NF-201) on Day 636. The participant's target tumor was located in the paraspinal region. The location of the participant's tumor is a confounder for this event.
L1885: All 4 events identified in the search of data from Study MEK-NF-201 are thus confounded, most frequently by the location of the participant's target tumor.

L1894: ## **Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound**
L1895: ## Data:
L1896: ## **Overdose:**
L1897: The MEK-NF-201 protocol defined overdose as any dose greater than 150% of the BSA assigned maximum total daily dose within a 24-hour period. The largest treatment dose administered in Study MEK-NF-201 was 4 mg BID. No overdoses, accidental or otherwise, were reported in Study MEK-NF-201.
L1898: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1899: The largest treatment dose of mirdametinib administered to advanced cancer participants in Pool 2 was in adults at 30 mg BID. Twenty-three participants in Pool 2 received mirdametinib doses of 8, 10 mg BID, in addition to 74 participants who received doses of mirdametinib of 15, 20, or 30 mg BID. Treatment emergent SAEs were reported by 13 (57%) participants in the 8, 10 mg BID dose group and by 37 (50%) participants in the 15, 20, 30 mg BID dose group, respectively. Grade \ge 3 TEAEs were reported by 11 (48%) and 49 (66%) participants in the 2 dose groups, respectively. Grade 5 TEAEs were reported by 3 (13%) and 9 (12%) participants in each dose group, respectively, and all were due to disease progression.
L1900: Table 65 summarizes the incidence of TEAEs that were reported in at least 15% of participants in either of the two highest dose groups in Pool 2, in comparison to the same TEAEs in both Pool 1 cohorts. Given that mirdametinib generally has a dose-proportional increases in exposure in the dose range of 1 mg QD/BID to 30 mg BID, mirdametinib exposure in these two Pool 2 dose groups represent approximately 2 to 8 times the maximum recommended NF1 PN dose for mirdametinib of 4mg BID. Thus, data from the highest dose groups in Pool 2 may represent possible outcomes for mirdametinib overdoses. However, dosing of mirdametinib in participants in Pool 2 was sustained so the events observed may not necessarily represent events that may occur in the setting of a single excessive dose of mirdametinib in a patient receiving treatment with mirdametinib 4 mg BID or in the setting of accidental ingestion of mirdametinib.
L1901: Events with an incidence in Pool 2 of at least double or &gt;20% higher than the incidence in either of the Pool 1 cohorts included fatigue, edema peripheral, edema, pyrexia, face edema, rash, pruritis, erythema, dyspnea, visual impairment, vision blurred, muscular weakness, ALT increased, hypoalbuminemia, decreased appetite, dehydration, confusional state, and anemia. Hypoalbuminemia and anemia are unlikely to occur acutely following an acute overdose of mirdametinib since changes in these laboratory parameters typically occur gradually (with the exception of acute anemia due to blood loss).
L1902: Thirteen of these events are considered identified risks for mirdametinib at doses studied in MEK-NF-201, and thus are not specific toxicities of an overdose. These events are diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, constipation, abdominal pain, fatigue, rash, pruritis, dermatitis acneiform, dry skin, ALT increased, AST increased, and ejection fraction decreased.

L1910: ## Drug abuse:
L1911: The potential for abuse was assessed by reviewing the psychiatric disorders SOC for TEAEs that might suggest a psychoactive effect that could lead to drug abuse. In both Pool 1 and Pool 2, there were no psychiatric TEAEs for euphoria, disinhibition, dissociation, or any other PTs that might suggest a psychoactive effect that could lead to drug abuse.
L1912: ## Withdrawal and rebound:
L1913: Dependence was assessed by reviewing TEAE tables from Pool 1 and Pool 2. There were no TEAEs for drug dependence, drug withdrawal, or other terms that might suggest mirdametinib could cause physical dependence or symptoms of withdrawal.
L1914: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1915: In general, the adverse reactions associated with a mirdametinib overdose are likely to be consistent with the known adverse reactions at recommended doses of \le 4 mg BID, albeit potentially more severe. Clinical data above 30 mg BID in adults and above 4 mg BID in pediatrics are not available. No specific antidotes exist for the treatment of mirdametinib overdose. Hemodialysis is unlikely to result in additional elimination as the estimated fraction unbound for mirdametinib is 0.3%. Consequently, in the case of overdose, general supportive and symptomatic care is recommended.
L1916: No potential for abuse or dependence has been identified for mirdametinib.
L1917: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1918: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.

The observed safety profile represents a manageable safety profile for mirdametinib 2mg/mg2 BID for the treatment of adult and pediatric patients 2 years of age and older with NF1 PN, and can be reasonably expected to reflect the safety profile to be observed when used as indicated following marketing authorization.

L1930: ## **8.2.9. Integrated Assessment of Safety**
L1931: ## **Data:**
L1932: ## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1933: Mirdametinib has been studied in 133 participants with NF1 PN, including 58 pediatric and 75 adult participants (Pool 1). In Pool 1, 83 (62%) participants have been treated for at least 12 months, 50 (38%) for at least 24 months, and 16 (12%) for at least 36 months.
L1934: The most frequently (&gt;35%) reported TEAEs for the total population from Study MEK-NF-201 and Pool 1 were dermatitis acneiform, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting which are expected with MEK inhibition (Sulares 2021, Subbiah 2020).
L1935: For the total population of Pool 1, the most frequently reported Grade ≥3 TEAEs (≥4%) were dermatitis acneiform (5%), blood creatine phosphokinase increased (4%), neutrophil count decreased (4%), and abdominal pain (4%). Grade ≥3 TEAEs reported by ≥2 pediatric participants were neutrophil count decreased, blood creatine phosphokinase increased, diarrhea, syncope, weight increased, and abdominal pain. Grade ≥3 TEAEs reported by ≥2 adult participants were dermatitis acneiform, abdominal pain, back pain, COVID-19, nephrolithiasis and SARS-CoV-2 test positive.
L1936: In Pool 1, there were 2 participants in the pediatric cohort of Study MEK-NF-201 who reported Grade 4 events (blood creatine phosphokinase increased by 1 participant, and near drowning and laryngeal edema by the second participant). In the adult cohort of Pool 1, there were 2 participants from Study MEK-NF-201 who reported Grade 4 events (COPD and pneumonia bacterial, by 1 participant each).
L1937: There were no deaths on study in the pediatric cohort of Pool 1 or in the healthy volunteer studies. The 1 death in an adult participant in Pool 1 due to COVID-19 was assessed by the investigator as due to the infection and not to mirdametinib.
L1938: For the total population and adult cohort of Pool 1, the most frequently reported SAEs (≥2 participants) were COVID-19, abdominal pain, and nephrolithiasis. In the pediatric cohort, all SAE PTs were reported by 1 participant each.
L1939: In Pool 1, 21 (16%; 5 [9%] pediatric, 16 [21%] adult) participants discontinued study treatment due to TEAEs. The PTs reported in the pediatric cohort were: urticaria (2 participants [3%]), dermatitis acneiform, rash, diarrhea, abdominal pain, and constipation (1 participant each [2%]). TEAEs that led to the discontinuation of study treatment in &gt;1 participant in the adult cohort were dermatitis acneiform, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting.
L1940: The following events were recognized as risks for patients with NF1 PN treated with mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID:
L1941: - The following events were recognized as identified risks because of the frequency of their reports, temporal relationship to the start of treatment with mirdametinib, and presence of a plausible biologic mechanism, and were deemed important due to their potential impact on patients: ejection fraction decreased and retinal vein occlusion.
- The following events were reported at an increased incidence relative to that
L1942: ## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L1943: anticipated for the patient population with NF1 PN and are recognized as identified risks as these events were generally Grade 1 or 2 and did not frequently result in the discontinuation of study treatment: diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, constipation, skin events (including dermatitis acneiform, rash, pruritus, dry skin, alopecia, hair color changes, and hair texture), blood CPK increased, alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, blood alkaline phosphatase increased, and fatigue.
L1944: - The data presented support recognizing stomatitis as an identified risk for mirdametinib in the pediatric population and as a potential risk in the adult population.
- The data presented support recognizing paronychia, eczema, and neutrophil count decreased, as identified risks for mirdametinib in the pediatric population only.
- Given the low frequency and severity of events, the data support vision blurred and detachment of retinal pigment epithelium being recognized as important potential risks.
L1945: Nonclinical data support embryo-fetal toxicity being recognized as an important potential risk for mirdametinib, and mirdametinib should not be used during pregnancy. Mirdametinib had no adverse effects on male and female rat fertility.
L1946: The following relevant observations were made, but they do not represent adverse clinical findings: decreased hemoglobin, decreased leukocytes, lymphocyte count increased, increased cholesterol, increased creatinine, decreased bicarbonate, hypo calcium corrected for albumin, and increased glucose.
L1947: No clinically significant changes in urinalysis, vital signs, or ECG data were observed.
L1948: The most clinically impactful identified risks associated with the use of mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID in the treatment of NF1 PN and included in the Warnings and Precautions of the label are retinal vein occlusion, ejection fraction decreased, dermatitis acneiform, and rash. Impactful potential risks associated with the use of mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID in the treatment of NF1 PN and included in the Warnings and Precautions of the label are vision blurred, detachment of retinal pigment epithelium, and embryo-fetal toxicity. None of these events, nor any of the other risks associated with the use of mirdametinib, in isolation or in aggregate, would preclude the use of mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID for the treatment of patients with NF1 PN.
L1949: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1950: The observed safety profile described in the integrated summary of safety represents a manageable safety profile for mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID for the treatment of adult and pediatric patients 2 years of age and older with NF1 PN. No aspects of the observed safety profile would preclude the use of mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID for treating adult or pediatric patients aged ≥2 years with NF1 PN and the benefit: risk profile is favorable.
L1951: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1952: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's integrated assessment of safety.
L1953: The pooled safety data from the ReNeu study and Study NF-106 (Pool 1) and the additional safety analysis (90-day-update) allowed for an informed risk assessment of mirdametinib in the intended NF1 PN populations. Among 133 patients who received mirdametinib, 62% were exposed for one year or longer, 38% were exposed for 2 years or longer, and 12% were exposed for 3 years or longer, allowing for a reasonable evaluation of risk for cumulative toxicities with chronic dosing.
L1954: In Pool 1, serious adverse events occurred in 16% of adult patients and 14% of pediatric patients. There was one fatal adverse event due to Covid-19. AEs leading to permanent discontinuation occurred in 21% of adults and 9% of pediatric patients and AEs leading to dose reduction occurred in 20% of adult and 12% of pediatric patients. The most common AEs (&gt;25%) in the adult population included rash, diarrhea, nausea, musculoskeletal pain, vomiting, and fatigue. The most common AEs (&gt;25%) in the pediatric population included rash, diarrhea, musculoskeletal pain, abdominal pain, vomiting, fatigue, upper respiratory tract infection, paronychia, headache, left ventricular dysfunction and nausea. Grade 3-4 AEs occurred in 33% of adult patients and 40% of pediatric patients. Given the relatively low discontinuation and reduction rate, it appears that AEs were tolerable and manageable with dose modifications and supportive care in patients treated with mirdametinib.
L1955: Important risks of mirdametinib are similar to those of other MEK inhibitors including ocular, cardiac and dermatologic toxicities. Some rare, but serious, class related safety signals (e.g., rhabdomyolysis) were not identified in the Pool 1 population. RVO and RPED were not observed in the pediatric population. No new safety signals were identified for mirdametinib compared to commercially approved MEK inhibitors. The identified toxicities can be managed through labeling, including relevant warnings, precautions, and dose modification guidelines.
L1956: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1957: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1958: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1959: The ReNeu study is an open-label, single arm, multi-center trial which served as the primary basis for establishing the efficacy of mirdametinib for the treatment of pediatric and adult patients with NF1-PN. The primary endpoint of the trial was confirmed ORR per BICR using modified REINS criteria, supported by the secondary endpoint of DOR. Since the ReNeu study is a single-arm trial, there was no control for multiplicity in any of the hypothesis tests included in this report. Therefore, the p-values reported by the Applicant should be interpreted with caution.
L1960: The Applicant also provided results from a second study, NF-106, which were evaluated solely as supportive evidence given significant study design differences whereby one-third of patients were discontinued from treatment unless they had at least a 15% or 20% reduction in target tumor volume at Cycle 8 and 12, respectively, precluding a full evaluation of ORR and DOR. In addition, external control data from NCI trials 01-C-022 and 08-C-0079 were presented. While these descriptive data were considered supportive, the primary review of mirdametinib relied on a benefit:risk assessment of the data from Study MEK-NF-201 (ReNeu).
L1961: During the review it was noted that the Applicant used a modified REiNS criteria for disease assessments, confirming response within 2-6 months as opposed to 3-6 months per the criteria. Results using strict adherence to the criteria were consistent with the pre-specified analysis (50% and 52% in pediatric cohorts, respectively; 35% and 41% in adult cohorts, respectively). See Section 8.1 for more detail. Additional sensitivity analyses of ORR across BICR readers and in the Per Protocol Set population confirm the observed ORR benefit is robust.
L1962: Median estimates of DOR per Kaplan-Meier methods were not met in either cohort even at the time of the 90-day safety update, due to only a small number of events occurring after a response. A total of 90% of pediatric and 88% of adult responders had a DOR of \ge 12 months and 48% of pediatric and 50% of adult responders had DOR \ge 24 months indicating that the observed responses are durable.
L1963: In summary, the primary efficacy data of confirmed ORR and DOR from the ReNeu study demonstrate clinically meaningful durable anti-tumor activity of mirdametinib in the treatment of pediatric and adult patients with NF1 PN.
L1964: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1965: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1966: Study MEK-NF-201 (ReNeu) is a multicenter, single-arm trial investigating mirdametinib in adult and pediatric patients with NF1 and progressive or symptomatic plexiform neurofibromas not amenable to complete surgical resection. Eligible patients were at least 2 years of age with PN causing significant morbidity, and a target PN that was evaluable by volumetric analysis. Patients were enrolled by age group into pediatric and adult cohorts. Treatment consisted of mirdametinib at a dosage of 2 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 orally twice daily for the first 21 days of each 28-day cycle. The study included a treatment phase lasting 24 cycles and an optional long-term follow-up phase. The primary endpoint to demonstrate the efficacy of mirdametinib for this population was confirmed ORR, defined as PN decrease \ge 20\% compared to baseline in consecutive scans within 2-6 months, using independent central review of MRI volumetric analysis. DOR was a key secondary endpoint. The primary efficacy population consisted of 114 patients, including 56
L1967: pediatric and 58 adult patients.
L1968: The confirmed BICR-assessed ORR in the primary efficacy population of pediatric patients was 52% (95% CI: 38, 65); the median DOR was not reached. A total of 90% of responders had a DOR of \ge 12 months and 48% of responders had a DOR of \ge 24 months. The confirmed ORR as assessed by BICR in the primary efficacy population of adult patients was 41% (95% CI: 29, 55); the median DOR was not reached. A total of 88% of responders had a DOR of \ge 12 months and 50% of responders had a DOR of \ge 24 months. While NF1-PNs are benign tumors, they carry potential for severe associated morbidity including profound impact on quality of life, as described by participants at FDA's listening session for patients and caregivers with NF in June 2019 (see Section 2). In this context, FDA considered it critical that the ReNeu study demonstrate unequivocal evidence of a treatment effect supported by data showing improvement in physical function or an improvement in specific symptoms related to NF1-PN disease that correlates with radiographic tumor shrinkage. Despite the limitations of interpretation of the formal COA data, there was a general trend toward improvement in symptoms associated with radiographic responses. FDA's detailed review of patient-level narratives for each responder, which included assessments of physical function, use of pain or other symptom-ameliorating medications, as well as patient reported outcomes, provided clear supportive evidence of the clinical benefit of tumor shrinkage. It is noted, however, that narratives were not available for non-responders. FDA considers the totality of efficacy data to represent a clinically meaningful treatment effect in patients with symptomatic, unresectable NF1-PN and to constitute substantial evidence of effectiveness in this setting.
L1969: The safety profile of mirdametinib is acceptable for the intended population when considered in the context of a serious and life-threatening disease. Important toxicities of mirdametinib that were noted in this review include ocular toxicity, left ventricular dysfunction, dermatologic adverse reactions, and embryo-fetal toxicity. These toxicities are addressed in the Warnings and Precautions in the product label. Instructions for monitoring and dose modifications are provided in the USPI.
L1970: Based on the evaluation of clinical data from the ReNeu study, the review team recommends regular approval for mirdametinib for adult and pediatric patients 2 years of age and older with neurofibromatosis type 1 (NF1) who have progressive or symptomatic plexiform neurofibromas not amenable to complete surgical resection. In this study, mirdametinib demonstrated a robust and clinically meaningful effect on ORR that is durable and associated with improvements in disease-related morbidities. These factors represent direct clinical benefit to patients and support regular approval of the drug.

L1982: ## 10 Pediatrics
L1983: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1984: This NDA is for a pediatric and adult indication. Data supporting the pediatric indication are described throughout the application.
L1985: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1986: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. This NDA is exempt from the requirements under Section 505B of the FD&amp;C Act as amended by Title V of the FDA Reauthorization Act of 2017 (FDARA) because the marketing application is not intended for the treatment of an adult cancer. Additionally, because the product has an orphan drug designation for this indication, this NDA is exempt from the requirements to conduct pediatric assessments under the Pediatric Research Equity Act (PREA). Therefore, an Initial Pediatric Study Plan (iPSP) is not required for mirdametinib for the treatment of NF1-PN.
L1987: Given that there is limited long-term safety data for mirdametinib and a signal of growth delay has been identified, the review team recommends a PMR to further characterize the potential serious risk of long-term adverse effects of mirdametinib on growth and development in pediatric patients (See Section 13).
L1988: ## 11 Labeling Recommendations
L1989: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1990: This is an original application. The proposed label is included in the NDA.
L1991: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1992: The proposed labeling submitted by the Applicant required extensive revision by FDA. The format, language, and content of the proposed labeling was evaluated and revised for consistency with 21 CFR, labeling guidances and current labeling practices of the Office of Oncologic Diseases. The following table summarizes key changes.
L1993: **Table 67 Key Labeling Revisions**
L1994: Label Section	FDA Revised Text
1. INDICATIONS AND USAGE	FDA revised the indication statement to include the qualifiers “not amenable to complete resection” for consistency with the studied population.
2.1 Recommended Evaluation and Testing Before Initiating GOMEKLI	FDA added a new labeling section to ensure proper patient evaluation prior to administration of GOMEKLI.
2.2 GOMEKLI Dosage Form Overview	FDA added a new labeling section to highlight the 2 dosage forms for prescribers.
2.3 Recommended Dosage	FDA added text regarding missed dose and vomiting to ensure safe use.
2.4 GOMEKLI Preparation and Administration Instructions	FDA added a new labeling section to provide instructions for preparation of GOMEKLI as an oral suspension.
Table 3: Recommended Dosage Modifications and Management of Adverse Reactions for GOMEKLI	FDA revised the proposed 2-column table to a 3-column format for consistency across oncology labeling. Dosage modifications and management of adverse reactions advice was revised for safety and adherence to protocol specifications.
5.1 Ocular Toxicity	FDA revised the ocular toxicity warning to provide the incidences of toxicity for each safety population: total pool, adults and pediatric patients.
5.2 Left Ventricular Dysfunction	FDA revised the left ventricular dysfunction warning to provide the incidences of toxicity for adults and pediatric patients in the ReNeu study. Data on left ventricular dysfunction was not collected for the rest of the safety pool.
5.3 Dermatologic Adverse	FDA revised “skin toxicity” to dermatologic adverse
L1995: Reactions	reactions for consistency across oncology labeling. Text was revised for clarity and brevity.
5.4. Embryo-Fetal Toxicity	FDA revised text to include human data of a pregnancy reported 31 days after the last dose of GOMEKLI that resulted in a first trimester spontaneous abortion and to provide data from studies of rats and rabbits demonstrating embryo-fetal mortality, structural abnormalities and alterations to growth.
6.1 Clinical Trials Experience	FDA revised this section for consistency across oncology labeling.
7 Drug Interactions	FDA removed this section as non-actionable, consistent with 21 CFR 201.56(d)(4).
8.1. Pregnancy	FDA revised this section for consistency with the Guidance Pregnancy, Lactation, and Reproductive Potential: Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products-Content and Format (July 2020). FDA added data from studies of rats and rabbits demonstrating embryo-fetal mortality, structural abnormalities and alterations to growth. FDA added the subsection, Human Data to include human data of first trimester spontaneous abortion where a pregnancy was reported 31 days after the last dose of GOMEKLI. Additional details from the rat and rabbit studies were provided that demonstrate nonclinical embryo-fetal toxicity.
Contraception Females	FDA revised the Applicant's proposed requirement of female contraception for 1 week to 6 weeks, based on human and animal demonstration of embryo-fetal toxicity.
Contraception Males	FDA revised the Applicant's proposed requirement of male contraception to 3 months, based on nonclinical studies.
8.4. Pediatric Use	FDA provided information on the data supporting the pediatric indication and included nonclinical toxicity findings that were not demonstrated in the clinical studies.
8.5 Geriatric Use	FDA revised text for consistency with 21 CFR 201.57(c)(9)(v)(B)(1).
8.6 Renal Impairment	FDA revised text for clarity and brevity.
8.7 Hepatic Impairment	FDA revised text for clarity and brevity.
L1996: 10 OVERDOSAGE	FDA removed this section as non-actionable, consistent with 21 CFR 201.56(d)(4).
11 DESCRIPTION	FDA provided minor revisions for consistency with 21 CFR 201.57(c)(12).
12.1 Mechanism of Action	FDA revised the text to be specific to the indication statement.
12.2 Pharmacodynamics	FDA revised this section for consistency with 21 CFR 201.57(c)(13)(i)(B) requirements and specified that the metabolism pathway via UGT [uridine diphosphate (UDP)-glycosyltransferase] is primarily UGT1A6 and UGT2B7.
14.1 Neurofibromatosis Type 1 Associated Plexiform Neurofibromas	FDA revised the text for clarity, brevity and consistency across oncology labeling. Duration of response >12 months and >24 months was added.
17 PATIENT COUNSELING INFORMATION	FDA revised this section for consistency with Section 5.
L1997: The agreed upon labeling conveys adequate information for the safe and effective use of GOMEKLI.
L1998: The Patient Information document and Instructions for Use were reviewed and revised by the Patient Labeling Team, Office of Prescription Drug Promotion and the Division of Medication Error Prevention and Analysis.
L1999: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L2000: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2001: There were no significant safety concerns identified during the NDA review requiring risk management beyond labeling or warranting consideration for a Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategy (REMS). Safety concerns are adequately addressed by information in the Warnings and Precautions and Dosage and Administration sections of product labeling.
L2002: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L2003: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2004: The review team recommends the following PMRs/PMCs which were described in the 6-week PMR/PMC Communication Letter to the Applicant:
L2005: **PMR 1:** Complete a carcinogenicity study in rats to evaluate the potential serious risk of carcinogenicity.
L2006: Trial Completion:	10/2027
Final Report Submission:	04/2028
L2007: **PMR 2:** Conduct an integrated safety analysis of data from clinical trials to characterize and evaluate the potential serious risk of long-term adverse effects of mirdametinib on growth and development in pediatric patients. Include long-term follow-up data from pediatric patients enrolled on the MEK-NF-201 (ReNeu) trial (NCT03962543) and from ongoing and completed clinical trials of mirdametinib. Pediatric patients must be evaluated for growth and sexual development milestones annually for at least 7 years from initiation of mirdametinib or until a patient has achieved sexual maturity. Evaluations must include growth as measured by weight, height, height velocity, and height standard deviation scores (SDS); if available, include, age at thelarche (females), age at adrenarche (males), age at menarche (females), and Tanner Stage progression.
L2008: Trial Completion:	12/2028
Final Report Submission:	06/2029
L2009: **PMR 3:** Conduct an integrated safety analysis of data from clinical trials to further characterize the long-term incidence, severity, and outcomes of the known serious risks of ocular toxicity, left ventricular dysfunction, and dermatologic adverse reactions. Include the long-term follow-up safety data (minimum of 7 years) from adult and pediatric patients enrolled on the MEK-NF-201 (ReNeu) trial (NCT03962543) and all other ongoing and completed trials of mirdametinib to include an analysis of the following toxicities: ocular toxicity (including but not limited to retinal pigment epithelial detachment and retinal vein occlusion), cardiac toxicity (including but not limited to left ventricular dysfunction), and serious dermatologic toxicity.
L2010: Trial Completion:	12/2028
Final Report Submission:	06/2029
L2011: **PMR 4:** Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the potential serious risk of increased drug toxicity, and to determine an appropriate, safe dose of mirdametinib, in patients
L2012: ## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L2013: with moderate (total bilirubin &gt; 1.5 – 3.0 x ULN, any AST) and severe (total bilirubin &gt; 3 – 10 x ULN, any AST) hepatic impairment. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled “Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling.”
L2014: Draft Protocol Submission: 11/2024 (complete)
L2015: Final Protocol Submission: 02/2025
L2016: Trial Completion: 07/2026
L2017: Final Report Submission: 01/2027
L2018: **PMC 1:** Complete the MEK-NF-201 (ReNeu) trial (NCT03962543) to further characterize the clinical benefit of mirdametinib in adult and pediatric patients with neurofibromatosis type 1 (NF1) who have symptomatic plexiform neurofibromas (PN) not amenable to complete resection. To characterize response rate and duration, patients will be followed for at least 24 months from the onset of response.
L2019: Trial Completion: 12/2028
L2020: Final Report Submission: 06/2029
L2021: **PMC 2:** Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial with a strong CYP3A4 inducer (that also induces UGTs, P-gp, and CES enzymes) with mirdametinib to evaluate the effect of strong CYP3A4 induction on decreasing the systemic exposure of mirdametinib and to provide dosage recommendations for mirdametinib when used concomitantly with strong CYP3A4 inducers. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled “M12 Drug Interaction Studies.”
L2022: Draft Protocol Submission: 06/2025
