L2180: ## 19.4.2. Population PK Analysis
L2181: ## 19.4.2.1. Executive Summary
L2182: A PopPK model was developed to support the dose rationale for mirdametinib and is included in this NDA. This activity included the following objectives:
L2183: - Develop fit-for-purpose PopPK models to characterize the PK of mirdametinib and its active metabolite, PD-0315209, in healthy participants and participants with NF1 PN;
- Evaluate the effect of covariates on the variability of mirdametinib and PD-0315209 PK;
- Evaluate the magnitude and clinical relevance, when possible, of the statistically significant findings from the analysis;
- Predict mirdametinib and PD-0315209 exposures following doses of mirdametinib under scenarios of clinical importance.
L2184: **Methods:** Fit-for-purpose PopPK models were developed separately for mirdametinib and PD-0315209. The PD-0315209 model was developed sequentially, using empirical Bayes estimates (EBE) from the mirdametinib model. PD-0315209 PK was driven by fraction of parent drug converted to metabolite (Fm) from the mass balance study (MEK-NF-101). Nonlinear mixed effects modeling was employed to evaluate additional effects on model parameters and improve the ability of the model to describe all available data. Covariate-parameter relationships of interest were explored graphically and formally tested in each model if substantial trends were evident. Numerical checks of parameter precision and model robustness, goodness of fit (GOF) model diagnostics, and visual predictive checks (VPCs) were used to qualify the models for use in simulations.
L2185: **Results:** Apparent clearance (CL/F) of mirdametinib was 4.17 L/h for a typical 75 kg participant. At the median weight in pediatric participants (34.8 kg), CL/F of mirdametinib was 2.57 L/h. At the median weight in adult participants (72.8 kg), CL/F of mirdametinib was 4.09 L/h. Apparent clearance (CL/F) of mirdametinib was 4.17 L/h for a typical 75 kg participant. No difference in mirdametinib CL/F was observed between healthy participants and patient populations. Estimates for Vc/F, apparent peripheral volume of distribution – shallow compartment (Vp1/F) and apparent peripheral volume of distribution - deep compartment (Vp2/F) were 16.0, 22.4 and 54.3 L, respectively. The PopPK model included allometric scaling of time-varying weight on clearances and volumes of distribution. The estimated allometric coefficient for clearances was 0.631 and the estimate for volumes of distribution was 0.586.
L2186: The rapid absorption of mirdametinib following oral administration was described using a transit compartment absorption model (TCAM) model, driven by a mean transit time (MTT) of 0.542 h and 2.46 transit compartments. No statistically significant differences in absorption were found between fasted and unknown food status. However, administration of mirdametinib with a high-fat meal resulted in increased variability in TCAM model parameters, delayed absorption (MTT = 3.08 h), and an 8.80% decrease in relative bioavailability (Frel). No statistically significant differences were observed between the tablet and capsule formulations.
L2187: For PD-0315209, apparent clearance (CLm/F) was 0.583 L/h. At the median weight in pediatric participants (34.85 kg), CLm/F of PD-0315209 was 0.328 L/h. At the median weight in adult participants (72.8 kg), CLm/F of mirdametinib was 0.570 L/h. No statistically significant difference in CLm/F was identified between healthy participants and patient populations. In addition to allometric effects of weight on PD-0315209 clearances and volumes, the final PD-0315209 PopPK model includes effects of CLcr on CLm/F and concomitant UGT2B7 inducer use and age on Fm. While these effects were statistically significant when tested individually in the model, the impact of each effect is limited relative to the overall variability of PD-0315209 exposures.
L2188: The applicant tested the effect of concomitant use of several inhibitors or inducers (e.g. CES1, UGT1A6 and UGT2B7) through popPK analysis. The comedication analysis included the use of multiple comedication and multiple classifications for the comedication used. The design of the studies, in which multiple concomitant medications were allowed, along with the lack of specificity of these medicines for a single enzyme, limits the precise characterization of enzyme modulator interactions on the PK of mirdametinib using the PopPK analysis. Therefore, the review team does not think popPK results alone can support the proposed DDI statement in the label.
L2189: The sponsor also proposed unified dosing table to simplify the dosing regimen. The exposures at steady state of unified dose are comparable to the previously tested tablets in the trial in each BSA band based on the simulation. Therefore, the review team thinks the proposed unified dosage is acceptable.
L2190: ## **Overall Conclusions:**
L2191: - Mirdametinib PK was adequately characterized by a three-compartment model with absorption described by a TCAM.
- PD-0315209 PK was adequately characterized by a three-compartment model driven by Fm with IIV on CLm/F, Vmc/F, Fm, Qm1/F, and Vmc/F.
- Mirdametinib and PD-0315209 exposure is linear with dose over the range of doses evaluated (up to 20 mg).
- While a statistically significant effect for CLcr on CLm/F was identified, CL/F was not impacted by CLcr and mild and moderate impairment were not associated with clinically meaningful changes in exposure for mirdametinib and PD-0315209.
- Fm in participants using concomitant UGT2B7 inducers was 19% higher than without concomitant UGT2B7 inducers. In MEK-NF-201 participants, the median Fm with and without UGT2B7 inducers was 16.7% and 11.7%, respectively; however, steady-state observed and model-predicted exposures following nominal BID mirdametinib dosing were similar.
- Model qualifications show that the PopPK models describe the PK of mirdametinib and PD-0315209 adequately. VPCs support use of the models for exposure predictions and for simulations in support of predictive applications.
- Due to use of multiple comedication and multiple classifications for the comedication used, and the limitation of the study design, PopPK results alone can not support the proposed DDI statement in the label.
- The proposed unified dosage is acceptable as the exposures at steady state of unified dose are comparable to the previously tested tablets in the trial in each BSA band based on the simulation.
L2192: ## 19.4.2.2. PPK Assessment Summary
L2193: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2194: The PK of mirdametinib was well described by a three-compartment model with central clearance and a transit compartment absorption model. Covariates with significant but minor impact included fed status on mean transit time and bioavailability, and body weight on clearance and central volume. A three-compartment model was also used to describe the PK of the metabolite PD-0315209. Covariates with significant but minor impact for the metabolite included time-varying weight on clearance and central volume, creatinine clearance on clearance, age on fraction metabolized, and concomitant use of UGT2B7 inducers on fraction metabolized. All covariate effects were minor and none considered clinically relevant. There was no relevant difference in PK between patients and healthy volunteers.
L2195: General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis	Develop fit-for-purpose PopPK models to characterize the PK of mirdametinib and PD-0315209 in healthy participants and participants with NF1 PN
Evaluate the effect of covariates on the variability of mirdametinib and PD-0315209 PK
Evaluate the magnitude and clinical relevance, when possible, of the statistically significant findings from the analysis
Predict mirdametinib and PD-0315209 exposures following doses of mirdametinib under scenarios of clinical importance.

Studies Included	A4581001, A4581002, A4581004, NF106, and MEK-NF-201

NF1 PN studies: 2 mg/m2 BID (maximum dose of 4 mg BID intermittent 3 weeks on/1-week off dosing). Actual administered doses ranged from 1 to 4 mg BID.

Residual unexplained variability was described by a proportional plus additive error with estimates of 27.3% for the proportional error and 7.4% for the additive error.

BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	
The LLOQ for all methods was 0.1 ng/mL [A4581001 (Phase 1) and A4581004] or 1 ng/mL [A4581001 (Phase 2), A4581002, MEK-NF-101, NF106 and MEK-NF-201].
Since BLQ frequency was relatively low, missing values were unlikely to unduly influence model development. As such, likelihood-based approaches for handling BQL data were not considered.

Significant Covariates and Clinical Relevance	Effects of intrinsic and extrinsic factors, including sex, race, age, baseline creatinine clearance (CLcr), baseline liver enzymes (alanine aminotransferase (ALT), aspartate aminotransferase (AST), and bilirubin (BILI)), baseline albumin, and baseline alkaline phosphatase (ALP) were evaluated graphically. Concomitant medications, including CES1 inducers, P-glycoprotein (P-gp) inhibitors, and UGT1A6 and UGT2B7 inducers and inhibitors were also evaluated graphically. No relationships with mirdametinib PK parameters were observed. Statistically significant effects were found for CLcr on apparent clearance of PD-0315209 (CLm/F), UGT2BY use on fraction metabolized (Fm), and age on Fm; however, the impacts were limited relative to overall variability of PD-0315209 exposures. Thus, the limited changes to predicted exposures matched with the low concentrations relative to in vitro IC50s suggest that these effects are not clinically relevant. L2225: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2226: - Mirdametinib PK was adequately characterized by a three-compartment model with absorption described by a TCAM.
- PD-0315209 PK was adequately characterized by a three-compartment model driven by Fm with IIV on CLm/F, Vmc/F, Fm, Qm1/F, and Vmc/F.
- Mirdametinib and PD-0315209 exposure is linear with dose over the range of doses evaluated (up to 20 mg).
- While a statistically significant effect for CLCr on CLm/F was identified, CL/F was not impacted by CLCr and mild and moderate impairment were not associated with clinically meaningful changes in exposure for mirdametinib and PD-0315209.
- Fm in participants using concomitant UGT2B7 inducers was 19% higher than without concomitant UGT2B7 inducers. In MEK-NF-201 participants, the median Fm with and
L2227: without UGT2B7 inducers was 16.7% and 11.7%, respectively; however, steady-state observed and model-predicted exposures following nominal BID mirdametinib dosing were similar.
L2228: - Model qualifications show that the PopPK models describe the PK of mirdametinib and PD-0315209 adequately. VPCs support use of the models for exposure predictions and for simulations in support of predictive applications.
- Due to use of multiple comedication and multiple classifications for the comedication used, and the limitation of the study design, PopPK results alone can not support the proposed DDI statement in the label.
- The proposed unified dosage is acceptable as the exposures at steady state of unified dose are comparable to the previously tested tablets in the trial in each BSA band based on the simulation.

L2230: None

L2232: None
L2233: ## 19.4.3. Exposure-Response Analysis
L2234: ## 19.4.3.1. FDA's ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
L2235: PKPDx models were developed to identify exposure-response relationships with TGI, best overall response (BOR), duration of response (DOR), and time-to-progression (TTP). This activity included the following objectives:
L2236: - Characterize the PKPDx relationships between mirdametinib dose/exposure and change in tumor volume.
- Conduct population and trial simulations in support of dose justification and dose optimization.
L2237: **Methods:** The TGI model was developed sequentially, beginning with a natural history model to describe PN tumor growth in untreated participants from historical studies (01-C-022 and 08-C-0079). Tumor volume measurements and PopPK-model predicted exposure parameters for participants receiving mirdametinib in Studies NF-106 and MEK-NF-201 were included to characterize the drug effect attributable to mirdametinib treatment. Covariate-parameter relationships of interest were explored and structurally tested using stepwise covariate
L2238: modeling (SCM) and final models were qualified by visual predictive checks (VPCs) and numerical checks of parameter precision and model robustness.
L2239: Bayesian post hoc pharmacokinetics (PK) parameter estimates were used to estimate mirdametinib for subsequent exposure-response analysis. To assess the impact of changes in exposure for DOR, BOR, and TTP exposure-response relationships were graphically evaluated in all patients in MEK-NF-201 and NF-106. Covariate relationships were explored when the ER relationship was found to be significant. BOR, DOR, and TTP were evaluated by logistic regression or Cox regression analyses.
L2240: **Results:** Disease progression was best described by a Gompertz growth model with natural decay. The natural history data (NHD) model showed effects of age and tumor volume baseline on natural growth parameters. Participants aged 2-17 years old had higher natural growth rates (K_G) combined with lower tumor decay rates (K_D) but a higher drug effect (K_{Drug}). Adults (&gt;17 years) were found to have a lower K_G and higher K_D but a lower K_{Drug}. These factors led to a similar change in tumor volume over time between the two age groups. A higher tumor baseline was also linked to a higher maximum tumor volume (TS_{max}).
L2241: The NHD model was used as a basis for the TGI model to describe tumor growth in the absence of treatment. When mirdametinib treated participants were included in the model, a linear drug effect of mirdametinib on tumor growth with a slope of 0.189 week^{-1}/(ng/mL) described the data best. The effect was estimated to diminish exponentially over time and this decay was 82.8% lower in the pediatric population (2-17 years old) compared to adults, changing from 0.172 month^{-1} in adults to 0.0292 month^{-1} in the pediatric population. The half-life of the decay of the drug effect in adults was estimated to be 4.03 months, compared to 23.7 months in the pediatric population.
L2242: BOR was analyzed as a function of nominal mirdametinib maximum concentration at steady state (C_{max,ss}), mirdametinib minimum concentration at steady state (C_{min,ss}), mirdametinib area under the concentration vs. time curve at steady state within a dosing interval (AUC_{0-12,ss}). AUC_{0-12,ss} and C_{min,ss} were statistically significant predictors of BOR; C_{max,ss} was not a significant predictor of BOR. No statistically significant covariates were identified with the relationship to AUC_{0-12,ss}. No significant relationships were found between exposure parameters and DOR or TTP.
L2243: Comparison of efficacy showed that probability of response and reduction in tumor growth both increase with target dose and mirdametinib exposure. Longitudinal simulations suggest an increasing and earlier rate of response as dose increases and a greater rate of response in adult and adolescent subjects compared to children. All responses were associated with substantial, overlapping prediction intervals (PIs). Based on a utility analysis of evaluating a smaller cohort at a target dose of 1.5 mg/m^2, the probabilities of achieving 50, 60, or 70% response rates were substantially higher for the 2 mg/m^2 cohort than the 1.5 mg/m^2 cohort in the full population.
L2244: ## Overall Conclusions:
L2245: - Mirdametinib was predicted to have a concentration-dependent impact on tumor growth, with an exponential decay of this effect over time. This decay was higher in adults, possibly due to the differences in disease presentation between the pediatric population and adults. Given this difference in decay, the half-life of effect in adults was estimated to be 4.03 months, compared to 23.7 months in the pediatric population.
- Of the mirdametinib exposure metrics, AUC0–12,ss was found to be the best predictor of BOR, with an increasing probability of BOR as AUC0–12,ss increased. No exposure-response relationships were identified for DOR or TTP.
- Simulations suggested an increasing magnitude of %CFB and increasing response rate with increasing dose in the full population, and in children (2-12), adolescents (12-17), and adults. However, this trend should be taken with caution as it has not been verified in the clinical trial.
L2246: ## 19.4.3.2. ER (efficacy) Assessment Summary
L2247: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2248: PKPDx and exposure-response analyses established clear relationships between exposure and both tumor growth inhibition and overall response in participants with NF1 PN. Mirdametinib has a concentration-dependent effect on tumor growth, with increasing steady-state exposure being predictive of greater reduction in tumor volume, increased probability of response, and shorter time to response. The models are sufficiently robust to predict tumor response at target dose levels below 3 mg/m2. Simulations at target dose levels of 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.2, and 3.0 mg/m2 provide support for efficacy against PN in this range, with expectation of substantially improved probability of efficacy with increasing target dose. Further evaluation of a lower target dose in clinical studies is unlikely to alter the selection of 2 mg/m2 BID as optimal for the treatment of NF1 PN.
L2249: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	PKPDx models were developed to evaluate the PKPDx and ERR for mirdametinib in adult and pediatric participants with NF1 PN, which included the following objectives:Characterize the PKPDx relationships between mirdametinib dose/exposure and change in tumor volume;
Characterize the relationships between mirdametinib exposure and efficacy in NF1 PN participants;
Conduct population and trial simulations in support of dose justification and dose optimization.

L2253: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L2254: Model Structure	
The relationships between mirdametinib exposure and efficacy endpoints were evaluated as PKPDx or ERR models. The PKPDx model development for tumor size used individual parameter estimates from the final PopPK model added to the PKPDx datasets. For the ERR analyses of DOR, BOR, and TTP the final PopPK model was used to calculate individual estimates of exposure at nominal steady state.
For the TGI model, a disease progression model was first developed based on data from 01-C-0222 and 08-C-0079. The model was based on a Gompertz growth model and was expanded with a natural tumor decay rate (KD), an age-effect on tumor growth rate (KG) and KD, and an effect of baseline tumor volume on maximum tumor volume (TSmax).
A drug effect for mirdametinib (KDrug) was then estimated for treated participants in MEK-NF-201 and NF-106 with disease progression model parameters fixed. KDrug is modeled as a decaying linear effect as a function of Cavg,ss with a categorical age effect on the decay rate for KDrug (\lambda). Proportional error is included for Study 80079, Study 10222, and for each reader in MEK-NF-201. IIV is included for KG, TSmax, KD, KDrug, and \lambda.
Exposure-response relationship with BOR was evaluated by logistic regression with participants reaching stable disease, progressive disease, and with missing evaluations categorized as non-responders. AUC0-12,ss was the best predictor of response and no statistically significant covariates were identified.
Acceptable

Covariates and Clinical Relevance	
TGI: The natural history model showed effects of age and baseline tumor volume on natural growth parameters. Pediatric participants had higher natural growth rates (K_g) combined with lower tumor decay rates (K_d) and higher drug effect. Adult participants had lower K_g and higher K_d but with lower drug effect. These factors lead to a similar change in tumor volume over time between the age groups. No other covariate effects were supported by the data.
BOR: No statistically significant covariates were identified for the relationship between BOR and mirdametinib exposure. A longer time-to-tumor growth (TTG), a predicted parameter from the TGI model was associated with higher probability of BOR. Age group (2 to <12, 12 to 17, and >17) and baseline tumor volume were significant covariates in the relationship with BOR, suggesting that a better response is correlated with younger age and smaller baseline tumor volume. However, correlation with baseline tumor volume is not considered clinically relevant because a single tumor lesion is not reflective of the overall disease burden.

L2272: ## 19.4.3.3. FDA's ER (safety) Executive Summary
L2273: PKPDx models were developed to identify exposure-response relationships with TEAEs. This activity included the following objectives:
L2274: - Characterize the relationships between mirdametinib and PD-0315209 exposure and safety response in NF1 PN participants.
- Conduct population and trial simulations in support of dose justification and dose optimization.
L2275: **Methods:** Bayesian post hoc PK parameter estimates were used to estimate mirdametinib and PD-0315209 exposures for subsequent exposure-safety analysis. To assess the impact of changes in exposure for safety endpoints, exposure-response relationships were graphically evaluated in all patients in MEK-NF-201 and NF-106. Covariate relationships were explored when the ER relationship was found to be significant. TEAEs and time to first dose reduction were evaluated by logistic regression or Cox regression analyses.
L2276: **Results:** Mirdametinib and/or PD-0315209 AUC0–12,ss were found to be the best exposure predictors for all significant TEAEs. Of the grade 1+ binary endpoints, the probability of an ALT elevation, AST elevation, or CPK increase event were found to have statistically significant relationships with mirdametinib exposure. The probability of acneiform rash, ALT elevation, and AST elevation were found to have statistically significant relationships with PD-0315209 exposure. The similarity in TEAE ER relationships was driven by the strong correlation between mirdametinib and PD-0315209 as PD-0315209 exposure is a function of mirdametinib exposure.
L2277: Age group (2-12, 12-17, &gt;17) was a significant covariate for the probability of grade 1+ AST elevation (mirdametinib and PD-0315209 models), grade 1+ CPK increase (mirdametinib model), and grade 1+ acneiform rash (PD-0315209 model only). For grade 1+ AST elevation as a function of either mirdametinib or PD-0315209 AUC0–12,ss, the probability of events was greater in adults, but lower in adolescent participants compared to the reference group of children less than 12 years of age. The probability of grade 1+ CPK increase was shown to increase with mirdametinib AUC0–12,ss and decreased with increasing age group. The probability of grade 1+ acneiform rash increased with PD-0315209 AUC0–12,ss and increasing age. Baseline tumor volume was the only significant covariate for grade 1+ ALT elevation with both mirdametinib and PD-0315209 AUC0–12,ss. While this significantly improved the model, baseline tumor volume was not found to be statistically different from zero and there is no clinical reason to support this covariate selection in the model.
L2278: No ER relationships for grade 2+ events were significant with mirdametinib or PD-0315209 exposure. No mirdametinib exposure-response relationships were found for dose reductions or time to first dose reduction.
L2279: Comparison of safety events showed that probability of incidence increases with target dose and mirdametinib exposure; however, statistically significant ER relationships were only observed for grade 1+ events. The predicted increase in probability of grade 1+ events is unlikely to be clinically impactful.
L2280: ## Overall Conclusions:
L2281: - Mirdametinib and PD-0315209 AUC0–12,ss were found to be the best exposure predictor for significant safety events. For grade 1+ events, the probability of ALT elevation, AST elevation, and CPK increase was found to increase with mirdametinib
L2282: exposure. The probability of grade 1+ acneiform rash, grade 1+ ALT elevation, and grade 1+ AST elevation was found to increase with PD-0315209 exposure.
L2283: - Age group (2-12, 12-17, &gt;17) was shown to be a significant covariate for the occurrence of grade 1+ AST elevation (mirdametinib and PD-0315209 models), grade 1+ CPK increase (mirdametinib model), and grade 1+ acneiform rash (PD-0315209 model).
- Baseline tumor volume was found to be a significant covariate for the occurrence of grade 1+ ALT elevation with both mirdametinib and PD-0315209 AUC0–12,ss. While this significantly improved the model, baseline tumor volume was not found to be statistically different from zero and no clinical rationale was identified to support this covariate selection in the model.
- No exposure-response relationships were observed for the occurrence of other binary grade 1+ safety events. No grade 2+ events were significant with mirdametinib or PD-0315209 exposure.
- No mirdametinib exposure-response relationships were found for the occurrence of dose reductions or the time to first dose reduction.
L2284: ## 19.4.3.4. ER (safety) Assessment Summary
L2285: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2286: Exposure-response analyses were conducted using both the mirdametinib and PD-0315209 PopPK model to assess relationships with selected safety endpoints obtained from patient Studies NF-106 and MEK-NF-201. Of the Grade 1+ binary endpoints, the probability of an ALT elevation, AST elevation, or CPK increase event were found to have statistically significant relationships with mirdametinib exposure. The probability of acneiform rash, ALT elevation, and AST elevation were found to have statistically significant relationships with PD-0315209 exposure. The similarity in TEAE ER relationships was driven by the strong correlation between mirdametinib and PD-0315209 as PD-0315209 exposure is a function of mirdametinib exposure.
L2287: No ER relationships for Grade 2+ events were significant with mirdametinib or PD-0315209 exposure. No mirdametinib exposure-response relationships were found for dose reductions or time to first dose reduction.

Model Structure	
The relationships between (mirdametinib and PD-0315209) exposure and safety endpoints were evaluated as PKPDx or ERR models. For the ERR analyses of safety endpoints evaluated by logistic regression, the final PopPK model was used to calculate individual estimates of exposure on the day of onset, as available. For each safety endpoint, nominal steady state exposure was used for participants with no event.
Exposure-response relationships with safety events were evaluated by logistic regression. For significant relationships, covariates were tested in a stepwise forward search at a p < 0.05 level of significance. Only covariates that were significant in a univariate search were included in multivariate testing. Mirdametinib and/or PD-0315209 AUC0-12,ss were found to be the best exposure predictors for all significant TEAEs. Of the grade 1+ binary endpoints, the probability of a ALT elevation, AST elevation, or CPK increase event were found to have statistically significant relationships with mirdametinib exposure. The probability of acneiform rash, ALT elevation, and AST elevation were found to have statistically significant relationships with PD-0315209 exposure.

L2291: Model Evaluation	Final safety PKPDx models were determined based on model fit improvement using AIC: Akaike's Information Criterion, Chi-square test statistic statistical significance, physiological plausibility of parameter values, successful numerical convergence.	Acceptable
Covariates and Clinical Relevance	Age group (2-12, 12-17, >17) was a significant covariate for the probability of grade 1+ AST elevation (mirdametinib and PD-0315209 models), grade 1+ CPK increase (mirdametinib model), and grade 1+ acneiform rash (PD-0315209 model only).	Acceptable
Simulation for Specific Population	Population simulations of tumor volume response to varying dose levels of mirdametinib treatment were conducted using virtual participants with weight, BSA, and age sampled from NHANES demographic distributions. Simulations were conducted for no treatment and at 1, 1.5, 2, 2.2, and 3 mg/m2 BID administered 3 weeks on and 1 week off for 24 cycles. Percent response, defined as a reduction in volume of at least 20%, is plotted by dose along with safety event predictions and exposure distributions in Figure 16.	Acceptable
Visualization of E-R relationships	For the population simulations, percent response, defined as a reduction in volume of at least 20%, is plotted by dose along with safety event predictions and exposure distributions in Figure 16.	Acceptable
Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	• Mirdametinib and PD-0315209 AUC0-12,ss were found to be the best exposure predictor for significant safety events. For grade 1+ events, the probability of ALT elevation, AST elevation, and CPK increase was found to increase with mirdametinib exposure. The probability of	Acceptable
L2292: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
## Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
L2293: grade 1+ acneiform rash, grade 1+ ALT elevation, and grade 1+ AST elevation was found to increase with PD-0315209 exposure.
Age group (2-12, 12-17, >17) was shown to be a significant covariate for the occurrence of grade 1+ AST elevation (mirdametinib and PD-0315209 models), grade 1+ CPK increase (mirdametinib model), and grade 1+ acneiform rash (PD-0315209 model).
Baseline tumor volume was found to be a significant covariate for the occurrence of grade 1+ ALT elevation with both mirdametinib and PD-0315209 AUC0-12,ss. While this significantly improved the model, baseline tumor volume was not found to be statistically different from zero and no clinical rationale was identified to support this covariate selection in the model.
No exposure-response relationships were observed for the occurrence of other binary grade 1+ safety events: Rash, LVEF decrease, Paronychia (in pediatric cohort only due to low incidence in adults), Fatigue & Malaise, Headache & Migraine, Leukopenic events, Eye disorders, Gastrointestinal disorders, or ALP elevation. No grade 2+ events were significant with mirdametinib or PD-0315209 exposure.
No mirdametinib exposure-response relationships were found for the occurrence of dose reductions or the time to first dose reduction.

L2295: ## **19.4.3.5. ER Review Issues**
L2296: None
L2297: ## **19.4.3.6. Reviewer's Independent Analysis**
L2298: None
L2299: ## **19.4.3.7. Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses**
L2300: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2301: Mirdametinib has a clear exposure-response relationship with tumor volume, with increasing exposure being predictive of greater reductions in tumor response, increased probability of response, and shorter time to response. Exposure-response relationships were identified for both mirdametinib and PD-0315209 with some Grade 1+ TEAEs, however these relationships were not apparent when severity was limited to Grade 2+. Additionally, the events for which significant relationships are identified have not been correlated to observations of clinical intolerance. The models were sufficiently qualified to provide predictive application and simulations of various target dose levels support the conclusion that 2 mg/m2 BID is an optimal dose level for treatment of NF1.
L2302: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2303: Positive trend between mirdametinib exposure and efficacy endpoints were observed. However, the prediction of efficacy for untested dosing regimen should be interpreted with caution as it has not been verified in the clinical trial.

L2319: Figure 3 shows three panels (A, B, C) illustrating the efficacy of mirdametinib at low doses. Panel A is a bar chart showing the number of blood vessels per high-powered field (HPF) in neurofibroma tissue. The Vehicle group shows approximately 1.6 vessels/HPF. Panel B is a bar chart showing the percentage of Ki67+ proliferating cells. The Vehicle group shows approximately 30% (***) and the 0.5 mg/kg group shows approximately 20% (***) and the 1.5 mg/kg group shows approximately 12% (**). Panel C is a Western blot showing the ratio of phosphorylated ERK (pERK) to total ERK (ERK) in protein lysates from tumors. The Vehicle (V) group shows a ratio of 0.9. The 0.5 mg/kg group shows a ratio of 1.1. The 1.5 mg/kg group shows a ratio of 0.6. The ratios for 0.5 and 1.5 mg/kg are significantly lower than the Vehicle group, indicating reduced ERK phosphorylation.

L2321: Flowchart illustrating the study schema:
Patient eligibility (Morbid PN) leads to Cohort 1 (2 to 17 years of age) and Cohort 2 (\ge 18 years of age).
Both Cohorts proceed to the Treatment Phase: 24 Cycles (3 weeks on / 1 week off) of Mirdametinib (PD-0325901). Dose: 2 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 BID. Max dose of 4 \text{ mg} BID.
Following the Treatment Phase, participants proceed to 30 day Safety Follow-up.
From 30 day Safety Follow-up, participants may proceed to Optional Long-Term Follow-Up.
L2322: • If participant is to be monitored weekly, must refer to Liver Safety: Suggested Actions and Follow-up Assessments
Flowchart: Liver Chemistry Stopping Criteria and Increased Monitoring Algorithm
The flowchart starts with the decision point: ALT\ge3xULN.
If No, the path leads to "Continue Study Treatment".
If Yes, the path leads to the next decision point: Plus Bilirubin\ge2x ULN (>35% direct) or plus INR>1.5, if measured* Possible Hy's Law.
From the Plus Bilirubin\ge2x ULN (>35% direct) or plus INR>1.5, if measured* Possible Hy's Law decision point:
If Yes, the path leads to "Discontinue Study Treatment".
If No, the path leads to the next decision point: ALT\ge5xULN.
From the ALT\ge5xULN decision point:
If Yes, the path leads to "Discontinue Study Treatment".
If No, the path leads to the next decision point: ALT\ge3xULN Plus Symptoms of liver injury or hypersensitivity.
From the ALT\ge3xULN Plus Symptoms of liver injury or hypersensitivity decision point:
If Yes, the path leads to the next decision point: ALT\ge3xULN but able to monitor weekly for 4 weeks.
If No, the path leads to "Discontinue Study Treatment".
From the ALT\ge3xULN but able to monitor weekly for 4 weeks decision point:
If Yes, the path leads to the next decision point: ALT\ge3xULN persist for 4 weeks or stopping criteria met.
If No, the path leads to "Discontinue Study Treatment".
From the ALT\ge3xULN persist for 4 weeks or stopping criteria met decision point:
If Yes, the path leads to "Discontinue Study Treatment".
If No, the path leads to "Continue Study Treatment".

The x-axis for both plots is Time (cycles), ranging from 1 to 24. The y-axis for the top plot is Mean Tumor Volume (mL), ranging from 150 to 300. The y-axis for the bottom plot is Mean %CFB Tumor Volume, ranging from -40 to 20.
The top plot shows that tumor volume decreases over time for all regimens, with the 0 mg/m^{2} regimen showing the highest volume (around 270 mL) and the 3 mg/m^{2} regimen showing the lowest volume (around 150 mL) at cycle 24. The bottom plot shows that the percentage change from baseline decreases (becomes more negative) over time for all regimens, indicating tumor shrinkage. The 0 mg/m^{2} regimen shows the highest %CFB (around 10%) and the 3 mg/m^{2} regimen shows the lowest %CFB (around -40%) at cycle 24. Shaded areas represent the 95% CI of the mean response across 500 virtual trials.
